- `fx-notifications.net.shouldsend` — Minor residual: NOTIF_TEAM/TEAM_EXCEPT require `d.Target is Player` (NotificationReaches :2757/:2764); a null-team or non-Player target silently reaches nobody (QC NOTIF_TEAM dereferences other_client.team, so a non-player target is an edge case there too). Benign.
- `fx-notifications.args.tokens` — Tokens are resolved SERVER-side reading the SERVER's notification_show_location / _show_sprees* cvars; in Base these are CSQC-side per-client, so the port loses per-client control of location/spree display (direct consequence of server-side formatting — see fx-notifications.net.protocol intended divergence). This is the ONLY residual on the token path — every QC NOTIF_ARGUMENT_LIST token is now resolved (race/key-bind/hash included).
- `fx-notifications.multi.fanout` — A missing sub-notification is silently dropped (LastError set, no throw), so a partially-registered MULTI loses the unregistered cue rather than erroring like QC's init-time check.
- `fx-notifications.center.render` — Duration is now durcnt-driven (see fx-notifications.center.durcnt); an absent/'0 0' durcnt still falls back to the panel DefaultDuration=3s, matching QC's 'no explicit duration -> default' behaviour.
- `fx-notifications.center.durcnt` — The durcnt is NOT serialized on the wire as a separate field; the client recovers it from the notification's Durcnt field via the RegistryId. Correct for every registered center notif; a raw/unregistered centerprint (CenterRaw) carries no durcnt and uses the panel default (matches the engine builtin's behaviour).
- `fx-notifications.info.killfeed` — Constant MULTIICON_INFO (dynamic icon) (1 entry (MINIGAME_INVITE, iconargs) / not modelled)
- `fx-notifications.announcer.timecountdown` — Recorded divergence — Base runs Announcer_Time/Announcer_Countdown CSQC-locally per client; the port has no CSQC announcer timer, so AnnouncerController runs on the SERVER and broadcasts MSG_ANNCE/MSG_CENTER to all clients. cl_announcer_maptime is therefore treated as a server config value (global broadcast) rather than per-client. The hysteresis window, rounding and 1..10 PickNumber mapping are preserved exactly. Per-client cl_announcer_maptime preference is lost (one global value); a client wanting only the 1-minute warning gets the server's setting.
- `fx-notifications.gentle.variants` — Recorded divergence — Because the port formats notification text server-side, the gentle selector is driven by sv_gentle (the SVQC GENTLE source) rather than a per-client cl_gentle. GameWorld mirrors sv_gentle onto NotificationSystem.GentleMode each tick (StartFrame, alongside the WarmupStage mirror), so SelectTemplate prefers each notification's gentle variant for everyone when sv_gentle is set. Per-CLIENT cl_gentle preference is lost — a deliberate consequence of server-side text formatting (same class as fx-notifications.net.protocol). Per-client cl_gentle preference is lost: gentle mode is a single server-global value (sv_gentle). A client wanting gentle text on a non-gentle server cannot get it.
- `fx-notifications.net.kill` — Call-site coverage NARROWED this audit (now 7 distinct sites): warmup abort (CPID_ROUND), idle warning (CPID_IDLING), world reset x2 (KillAll), team change (CPID_REMOVE), campcheck death (CPID_CAMPCHECK), instagib find-ammo (CPID_INSTAGIB_FINDAMMO). The prior 'idle/join-prevention and instagib find-ammo not wired' note is closed. Base's keyhunt, vehicles and nades kill sites remain unported (those features aren't ported). Coverage correct where wired.
- `fx-notifications.center.title` — Recorded divergence — Base sets the title CSQC-locally inside Announcer_Gamestart/Announcer_Duel; the port has no CSQC announcer timer, so GameWorld.BroadcastCountdownTitle runs server-side and broadcasts MSG_CenterTitle/CenterDuelTitle to all clients (same server-side announcer divergence as fx-notifications.announcer.timecountdown). On screen equivalent for a standard match; the title is global rather than per-client. The CenterPrintPanel must render the title/duel-title strings above the countdown in the same style as Base centerprint.qc; this audit verified the send/route/setter wiring (SetTitle/SetDuelTitle/ClearTitle exist and are called) but did not visually confirm the on-screen layout.
- `fx-notifications.center.raw` — Routed through the notification reliable channel as MSG_CenterRaw rather than the engine centerprint() builtin; functionally equivalent (a no-cpid panel push) but not a literal builtin port.
- `fx-sounds.emit.sound_play` — Recorded divergence — SoundWire replaces DP's bit-packed SVC_SOUND with a self-describing record (sample string + raw floats + chan + netid + loop/stop/pitch flags) because the port has its own snapshot protocol and no DP sound-index table; behaviorally equivalent (vol/atten/channel/origin/loop/stop/pitch all carried). Constant wire_encode (SVC_SOUND bit-packed (idx/flags/quantized) / SoundWire self-describing record)
- `fx-sounds.emit.sound_allowed` — QC MSG_ONE 'sounds to self always pass' branch omitted (no per-recipient msg_entity context at the gate; documented cosmetic edge-case — only matters under bot_sound_monopoly 1, default 0).
- `fx-sounds.voice.live_body_callers` — Minor: the speed gate uses 2D horizontal velocity (Vec2LenSq) while QC vdist uses 3D velocity — negligible in practice (grounded motion is near-horizontal), but not byte-identical when moving up/down a slope.
- `fx-sounds.voice.persounds_manifest` — GlobalSound_pitch audible model-scale shift is now ported+wired (this pass) but NOT runtime-verified on a non-default .scale entity: for stock players ScaleFactor==0 -> pitch 1.0 (no shift), so the gradient is exercised live but only meaningfully diverges from 1.0 for a scaled emitter (giant/mini mutators, scaled map entities) — which was not played in-game side-by-side. Per-skin fallback chain partially simplified: QC tries skin N, then skin 0; the port resolves a single ModelSoundsFile(model,skin) and the default pack (a missing per-skin manifest falls through to default rather than to skin-0). Negligible for stock models, which ship one pack.
- `fx-sounds.presentation.dp_attenuation` — Recorded divergence — DP's SND_Spatialize falloff (radius/exponent) is reproduced in C# with Godot's own attenuation DISABLED (AttenuationModel.Disabled, MaxDistance 0, low-pass cutoff raised), instead of Godot's inverse-distance curve. This is the FAITHFUL DP curve (method 1), not an arbitrary tweak. ATTEN_NONE plays centered on the listener (2D).
- `fx-sounds.presentation.hitsound_sample` — Anti-spam uses real-time (Time.GetTicksMsec) rather than sim time; QC also has an arc_hack that ignores cl_hitsound_antispam_time when the Arc is held + cl_hitsound>=2 (not ported). Minor: port adds an explicit Clamp([min,max]) the QC curve approaches but doesn't apply — negligible (the gradient stays in range).
- `invasion.core.registration` — Port does not cvar_settemp g_monsters 1; it spawns monsters directly, so the equivalence relies on the spawn path, not the global toggle. Constant g_monsters_forced (1 (cvar_settemp) / not forced (monsters spawned directly))
- `invasion.spawn.pick_monster` — Type-flag classification is by netname, not a descriptor TypeFlags field: IsExcludedFromRandomPick→wyvern (the catalog's only FLY), IsSupermonster→golem, IsUndead→zombie. Exact for the 5 ported monsters but brittle if the catalog grows (a real MON_FLAG/MONSTER_TYPE field on the descriptor is the durable fix).
- `invasion.spawn.pick_spawnpoint` — MoveToRandomMapLocation fallback (when no spawnpoints registered) still replaced by Vector3.Zero — monsters pile at world origin instead of being scattered across the map (no headless world-bounds sampler yet).
- `invasion.spawn.chosen_monster` — spawn_point target_range/target2 copy is not modeled (no spawn-point entity in the port; only the origin is carried).
- `invasion.round.handler_wiring` — The GameWorld-level Rounds handler (created by the bare EnableRounds() at the Invasion arm) still runs in parallel with DEFAULT CA-style predicates, but its result is NOT read for Invasion (match-end reads inv.MatchEnded only) and for solo/non-team it never starts (DefaultCanRoundStart needs PlayerCount>=2) — inert. With 2+ players it could in principle fire OnRoundReset→ResetMap; this is a pre-existing latent concern, not introduced by the port waves.
- `invasion.win.point_limit` — Base's g_invasion_point_limit cvar treats 0 as 'play without limit'; the port's PointLimit getter resolves <=0 (incl. an explicit 0) to the mapinfo 50 default, so an operator setting 0 to disable the limit would still get a 50-kill cap. Minor (-1 default path matches Base exactly).
- `invasion.bots.targeting` — Recorded divergence — The port has no g_bot_targets/g_monster_targets intrusive lists. Base clears player.bot_attack to drop the player from those lists; the port flags the player EntFlags.NoTarget instead, which BOTH monster target acquisition (MonsterAI.ValidTarget) and bot aim (BotBrain) already honor — one mechanism covers PlayerSpawn (bot_attack clear) AND Bot_ForbidAttack (bots only shoot monsters) together. Behaviorally equivalent for the co-op intent.
- `invasion.hud.monster_count` — Live for a listen/host server (the scoreboard publish reads _serverWorld.GameType). A pure remote client with no local _serverWorld falls back to the natural MonsterAI totals — but Base publishes these as server STATs, so the host-side publish is the parity-correct surface.
- `items-pickups.touch.gate` — Recorded divergence — The only remaining divergence is the pickup-sound CHANNEL: QC plays a non-resource powerup's pickup sound on CH_TRIGGER_SINGLE (self-cutting) and everything else on CH_TRIGGER; the port plays all items on the auto/TriggerAuto channel so two quick pickups stack instead of one cutting the other off — a deliberate, documented choice in ItemPickupRules.PlayPickupSound. Constant pickup-sound channel (powerup (non-resource) = CH_TRIGGER_SINGLE; else CH_TRIGGER / all items on TriggerAuto (channel distinction dropped))
- `items-pickups.respawn.scheduling` — Recorded divergence — QC's per-second sound uses soundto(MSG_ONE) gated to each client the waypoint is visible to; the port's Common layer has no per-client soundto seam, so it emits ONE positional ITEMRESPAWNCOUNTDOWN cue on the item entity (ATTEN_NORM falloff approximates the proximity gate). For SpectatorOnly items (Mega/Big Health+Armor) this over-broadcasts slightly to nearby live players vs QC's strict waypoint-visibility gate — a documented, minor presentation divergence.
- `items-pickups.show.visibility` — Constant sv_simple_items (ITS_ALLOWSI) -> _simple sprite swap (client/items/items.qc:78 (default 1) / NOT ported — no _simple model resolver, bit left unset (Show ll.525-528))
- `items-pickups.team.finditem` — Recorded divergence — Base defers Item_FindTeam via InitializeEntity(INITPRIO_FINDTARGET) so it runs after all map entities spawn; the port has no INITPRIO queue and calls FindTeam directly from the spawn tail. This is safe because the BSP entity lump is fully parsed before the first gameplay tick — all same-team members exist in Api.Entities.All by the time any spawn tail runs. The only edge case (a teamed item spawned mid-game) has no stock-map producer. Same end state, no Base-behavior change.
- `items-pickups.spawn.driver` — q3compat item origin offset (z += -15 - mins.z): code ADDED (StartItem.cs:249-256) but gated on Q3CompatProvider, declared at StartItem.cs:50 and NEVER assigned — so it no-ops. Items on pure Quake3/QL maps still sit ~15qu low. Faithful the moment a host wires Q3CompatProvider to real .arena/.defi detection. item_armor1 always maps to ArmorMedium; Base spawns ArmorSmall (shard) on a Q3 map (autocvar_sv_mapformat_is_quake3) — same unwired Q3CompatProvider story. Constant q3compat origin z += -15 - mins.z (applied / code ADDED (StartItem.cs:249-256) but gated on Q3CompatProvider which is NEVER WIRED -> no-op; DEAD)
- `items-pickups.presentation.bob_ghost_despawn` — Recorded divergence — Pickup/respawn particles + ghost/despawn fades are driven from the authoritative server pickup/respawn EVENT + the client render flags rather than the CSQC ITS_AVAILABLE net transition (with its isnew/PVS guards). Documented in ItemPickupRules.EmitItemEffect as equivalent-but-guard-free: it fires only on the real gameplay event, never when an item merely streams into a client's PVS. RESOLVED 2026-07-05 (playtest #29): stay-weapon COLORMOD (cl_weapon_stay_color '2 0.5 0.5') + the ghost tint (cl_ghost_items_color) are now applied via ApplyItemTint (renamed from SetTreeColormod 2026-07-11; cached per-surface override variants — never mutates the shared/cached materials). Still open: vehicle-hud item tint (cl_items_vehicle_*, no vehicle-hud item pass) and ShaderMaterial item surfaces (left untinted, alpha-only). simple items (sv_simple_items default 1 + cl_simple_items) not ported — always renders the full 3D model with bob, never the flat non-bobbing _simple sprite. distance-fade edge: ItemDistanceAlpha only runs for entities IsItemEntity accepts (ItemAnimate!=0 || expiring || !available). A non-animating, available item (e.g. spawnflag 1024 suppressing animation) skips the distance branch. Stock items animate, so this is a corner case. Constant simple items (sv_simple_items/cl_simple_items) (swap to _simple sprite, no bob / NOT ported (always 3D)) Constant stay-weapon COLOR + vehicle-hud tint (cl_weapon_stay_color, cl_items_vehicle_*) (colormod multiply in ItemDraw availability branch / stay-weapon colormod APPLIED 2026-07-05 (playtest #29; mechanism now ApplyItemTint (2026-07-11): per-surface override variants of the shared materials); vehicle-hud tint still missing (no vehicle-hud item pass))
- `items-pickups.itemstime.hud_feed` — Recorded divergence — Per-player visibility tiers (sv_itemstime 1 spectators-only vs 2 also-alive-players) reduce to the client panel's own hud_panel_itemstime gate in this single-view port; the server-side producer is identical, and the tier choice is enforced client-side. Recompute runs every server frame (a small scan) rather than on the QC reset/spawn/connect events, kept allocation-free. hidebig (hud_panel_itemstime_hidebig) toggling whether Big Health/Armor are in the timed set is a client cvar not modeled server-side; the port always includes big health/armor in CurrentTimes.
- `items-pickups.itemgroup.networking` — Constant group small items within 120qu radius into a shared item_group id (items.qc:1250-1283 FOREACH_ENTITY_RADIUS 120, IS_SMALL / none) Constant item_group_count per group = member count (items.qc:1285-1294 / none)
- `items-pickups.registry.items_contract` — Recorded divergence — QC's REGISTRY macro infrastructure (numeric m_id, alphabetical REGISTRY_SORT with a CSQC category-order snapshot) is replaced by C# per-class Pickup defs + ItemSpawnFuncs' classname-keyed spawn table. Each Base consumer of the id contract is handled at behavior level instead: itemstime HUD ordering is a hand-maintained list in ItemsTimePanel (matching the Base category order), and the pickup-HUD inventory networking is replaced by stat-delta inference (see items-pickups.give.pickup_dispatch_inventory). The dumpitems console command (GENERIC_COMMAND in all.qh -> Dump_Items) is not ported — no equivalent item-dump diagnostic exists (grep dumpitems/Dump_Items: zero matches). Constant registry capacity / numeric m_id (32 slots, m_id = registry index (24 usable as .items bits) / no numeric item-id registry — per-class Pickup singletons keyed by classname)
- `items-pickups.give.pickup_dispatch_inventory` — Recorded divergence — The ENT_CLIENT_INVENTORY per-item-id count networking is replaced by pure client-side stat-delta inference (NetGame.UpdatePickupFeed), documented in PickupPanel.cs as deliberate so the feed works on a pure --connect client with no local server entity; spawn re-baselining prevents loadout false-positives. The pickup feed shows GENERIC labels for resources — a Mega Health reads 'Health', any armor reads 'Armor' — where Base's inventory net carries the itemdef id (specific name + icon). Weapons are the only per-def entries. Powerups/held items (strength/shield/jetpack/fuelregen/buffs) and fuel never feed the panel: UpdatePickupFeed tracks only the weapon set + health/armor/shells/bullets/rockets/cells deltas. Constant per-item-id inv_items[] count networked (ENT_CLIENT_INVENTORY) (++inv_items[m_id] + delta net / none)
- `items-pickups.give.give_grammar` — Constant POSTGIVE GiveSound cues (item pickup sounds on gained resources/weapons) (GiveSound per resource/weapon/bit / none) Constant GiveRot rot/regen timer reset on give (GiveRot(pauserot*/pauseregen* fields) / none)
- `items-pickups.presentation.client_render_defaults` — Constant cl_simple_items / cl_simpleitems_postfix (0 / "_simple" / menu checkbox exists (DialogSettingsGame.cs:383) but NO consumer — simple items unported) Constant cl_items_vehicle_alpha / _color (0.75 / '2 0.5 0.5' / not applied (no vehicle-hud item tint))
- `items-pickups.spawn.item_initialise_api` — Recorded divergence — QC funnels loot AND permanent through the single Item_Initialise entry; the port splits it into StartItem.SpawnLoot (loot) / StartItem.Spawn (permanent) and lets each caller (monster drops, overkill loot, random_items, weapon throwing) call the right half directly. Same observable states; the classname-based half of Item_Initialise is the SpawnFuncs dispatch (documented in RandomItemsMutator.cs:195).
- `keepaway.ball.pickup` — Constant carried_scale (12/16 = 0.75 / none (no entity scale field))
- `keepaway.score.timepoints` — Tick (live per-frame DriveGametypeFrame) has a real carrier to score because the pickup path is live — the float2int-remainder accrual awards score while the ball is held. Default timepoints=0 so the column is opt-in (matches Base cfg g_keepaway_score_timepoints 0).
- `keepaway.score.bctime` — BCTIME accrual runs while carrying (the carrier is live); the secondary-sort column is declared in Activate (live, ka.Activate). MINOR (intended): the port banks whole seconds (int column) and carries the fractional remainder; QC adds raw frametime to a float scoring field. Observable difference is negligible (scoreboard shows whole seconds).
- `keepaway.score.killbonuses` — OnDeath bonuses reach a real carrier — killing the carrier awards bckill + KEEPAWAY_CARRIERKILLS; a kill made while carrying awards killac. All gated correctly (attacker != victim, IS_PLAYER). The unset fallbacks DefaultScoreBcKill/KillAc are 1, matching the Base cfg defaults (gametypes-server.cfg:437-438 = 1/1), so headless/tests award the right bonus. GiveFragsForKill is suppressed — OnGiveFragsForKill zeroes FragScore and returns true; the live Scores.cs GiveFragsForKill.Call reads back 0, so the normal +1 FFA kill frag no longer leaks into the ka score (matches QC ka GiveFragsForKill). MINOR: Base only adds bckill score under `if(autocvar_g_keepaway_score_bckill)` (nonzero guard); the port adds (int)ScoreBcKill unconditionally. With default=1 these are identical; a server explicitly setting bckill 0 would award 0 anyway (0 added). No observable divergence at any sane cvar value.
- `keepaway.win.pointlimit` — FIXED (was STILL OPEN, values partial→faithful): the PointLimit getter now models the QC -1→mapinfo resolution — `if (TryCvar(CvarPointLimitKa, out pl) && pl != -1f) return pl;` skips the -1 cfg value, then falls through to fraglimit, then the literal 30 (== gametype_init pointlimit=30). So loading the cfg's -1 no longer leaks a no-limit match; a carrier reaching 30 ends the match as in Base.
- `keepaway.damage.matrix` — The matrix affects combat — OnDamageCalculate is registered on MutatorHooks.DamageCalculate in Activate, and the live DamageSystem DamageCalculate.Call invokes it on every player-vs-player hit (the IS_PLAYER guard mirrors QC: returns early for non-player attacker/target). ScaleFactor/CvarVectorComponent parse the VECTOR cvar STRING and index the component (x=self, y=other-carrier, z=noncarrier), matching Base's single-vector autocvar. Component selection (attackerCarries→carrier vs noncarrier cvar; target==attacker→x, targetCarries→y, else→z) matches the QC branch table.
- `keepaway.carrier.highspeed` — RE-VERIFIED Wave 16/17 (faithful/live): OnPlayerPhysics is registered on MutatorHooks.PlayerPhysics in Activate (Keepaway.cs:213-214); the live PlayerPhysics step fires it at PlayerPhysics.cs:283 (MutatorHooks.PlayerPhysics.Call). When the player carries the ball it does `player.SpeedMultiplier *= BallCarrierHighspeed` (Keepaway.cs:243-244), exactly QC's `STAT(MOVEVARS_HIGHSPEED, player) *= autocvar_g_keepaway_ballcarrier_highspeed`. CONFIRMED the seed ordering: SpeedMultiplier = mp.HighSpeed is set at PlayerPhysics.cs:253 BEFORE the hook Call at :283, so the multiply composes cleanly with powerup/swamp/buff factors. Default is 1 (no effect), so the visible impact is nil unless a server sets it — but the hook is present + live.
- `keepaway.hud.modicon` — The whole mod-icon stack is live end-to-end (RE-VERIFIED Wave 16/17). Server packs ka.Ball.Carrier's net id into the per-recipient Keepaway status block (GametypeStatusBlock.cs:115-120 writes netIdOf(carrier), Kind.Keepaway=7), produced per-peer in the live snapshot writer. The client reads ms.CarrierNetId, sets panel.KeepawayCarrying = (CarrierNetId != 0 && CarrierNetId == LocalNetId) (NetGame.cs:4467-4469), and DrawKeepaway renders the keepawayball_carrying icon with the blink(0.85,0.15,5) pulse + the status-change expand transition — faithful to HUD_Mod_Keepaway. MINOR (intended): the port transmits the carrier NET ID rather than a per-player KA_CARRYING bit; the client derives the local carrying flag from it (carrier == self). Equivalent result, and it also feeds the carrier waypoint. MINOR (presentation): when the keepawayball_carrying skin texture is missing the panel draws a ball-glyph + 'BALL' stand-in (a deliberate never-invisible fallback, not a Base behavior).
- `keepaway.warn.noncarrier` — FIXED (was missing→faithful/live): the OnDeath branch is now ported. When the attacker != victim, IS_PLAYER, the victim was NOT carrying AND the attacker is NOT the carrier, it reads g_keepaway_noncarrier_warn (CvarNonCarrierWarn, Keepaway.cs:561) and, when nonzero, sends CENTER KEEPAWAY_WARN to the killer (NotifBroadcast.OneOnly, Keepaway.cs:562) == QC ka PlayerDies Send_Notification(NOTIF_ONE_ONLY, frag_attacker, MSG_CENTER, CENTER_KEEPAWAY_WARN). OnDeath is on the live Combat.Death bus (added in Activate), so the warn fires on real kills. The notification is registered (NotificationsList.cs:986 CENTER KEEPAWAY_WARN). BASELINE CORRECTION: the cfg default is 1 (gametypes-server.cfg:446 'warn players when they kill without holding the ball'), so this warn is ON by default in Base — the prior row's claim of default-0/nil-impact was wrong on BOTH counts (default value AND it being unported).
- `keepaway.score.countfragsremaining` — FIXED (was missing→faithful/live): the announce gating is now modeled. RecomputeLeader (called live each tick at GameWorld.cs:2820) calls AnnounceFragsRemaining, which early-returns when ScoreTimePoints != 0 (Keepaway.cs:620-621) — exactly QC's `return !autocvar_g_keepaway_score_timepoints`. When timepoints==0 (the default) it computes top/second primary scores and fires GameScores.CountFragsRemaining (the REMAINING_FRAG_{1,2,3} announcer), Keepaway.cs:644. ResetFragsRemaining is also re-armed in Activate (Keepaway.cs:173) so the cue can fire again each match.
- `keepaway.log.eventlog` — FIXED (was missing/na→faithful/live): the :ka: gamelog echoes are now ported + live. PickUp calls EmitEventLog("pickup", player) (Keepaway.cs:355) and Drop calls EmitEventLog("dropped", carrier) (Keepaway.cs:380); EmitEventLog formats `:ka:<mode>:<playerid>` and invokes the EventLogEcho seam, which GameWorld.cs:2916 wires to `if (Cvars.Bool("sv_eventlog")) GameLog.Echo(line)` on ka.Activate — exactly QC ka_EventLog (gated by autocvar_sv_eventlog, format strcat(":ka:", mode, ":", ftos(actor.playerid))). Low impact: affects only server-side stat-tracking log scraping, not gameplay or any client-visible behavior — but it is now wired end-to-end.
- `keepaway.score.resetmapglobal` — Constant reset_scope (FOREACH_CLIENT(true) it.float2int_decimal_fld = 0 / Keepaway._timePointsRemainder = 0 (single accumulator, reset in Activate))
- `keepaway.score.preferplayerscore` — FIXED (was partial/na→faithful/live): the PreferPlayerScore_Clear hook is now ported + live. MutatorHooks.PreferPlayerScore_Clear exists (MutatorHooks.cs:598) with a global FirePreferPlayerScore_Clear dispatch (MutatorHooks.cs:604); ka.Activate registers OnPreferPlayerScoreClear on it (Keepaway.cs:220), which returns true unconditionally == QC `MUTATOR_HOOKFUNCTION(ka, PreferPlayerScore_Clear){ return true; }`. Live path: GameScores.ClearPlayerOnJoin is called on every player join (ClientManager.cs:294) and, under g_score_resetonjoin -1, consults FirePreferPlayerScore_Clear (GameScores.cs:637) — ka's handler vetoes the per-player score wipe, exactly the QC mechanism (server/scores.qc:289 PlayerScore_Clear -1 branch). RE-VERIFIED Wave 16/17.
- `keyhunt.key.carried_orbit` — Minor: Base advances the orbit yaw against (time % 360) (the raw sim time wrapped to 360), per-think at 0.05s; the port uses the same expression (now % 360f) * KeyXySpeed off key.SpawnAngle. Bit-faithful at the formula level. Base attaches via setattachment + only rewrites x,y each think (z fixed from attach); the port recomputes the full origin (carrier + fwd*XYDIST + z*ZSHIFT) each think — observationally identical ring.
- `keyhunt.capture.all_owned_and_in_range` — Base measures every carrier against the FIRST key's owner.origin; port measures against an 'anchor' (first key's carrier). Equivalent geometry at radius 150. Not a real defect.
- `keyhunt.capture.scoring` — Minor mechanism divergence: the per-carrier SP_SCORE chunk is credited via c.ScoreFrags += chunk (then AddTeamScore for the team side) rather than QC's single GameRules_scoring_add_team_float2int(player, SCORE, f, ..., team:1) call that credits both at once. Magnitude faithful; same mechanism class as carrier_frag/push.
- `keyhunt.loss.loser_team_push_destroy` — Minor mechanism divergence: the push credit (pusher.ScoreFrags += score_push) and the per-player destroyed chunks (p.ScoreFrags += f + AddTeamScore) route through ScoreFrags + the team total rather than QC's team-aware kh_Scores_Event SCORE float2int pipeline. Magnitude and distribution faithful; same mechanism class as carrier_frag.
- `keyhunt.drop.on_death` — Minor: the DropSpecialItems hook (Base kh_Key_DropAll(target, false) when a player's special items are stripped) has no dedicated port analogue. The PlayerDies (OnDeath) + MakePlayerObserver + ClientDisconnect (OnPlayerRemoved) paths are all wired, covering every player-visible drop edge; DropSpecialItems is a narrow edge (e.g. strength/shield strip) that in KH would also drop keys — minor residual.
- `keyhunt.drop.voluntary_use` — Minor: Base PlayerUseKey gates on MUTATOR_RETURNVALUE == 0; the port's OnUseKey unconditionally attempts the drop and returns whether it dropped. Observationally equivalent (single gametype per match). Not a defect for KeyHunt.
- `keyhunt.combat.carrier_frag` — Base kh_HandleFrags credits via kh_Scores_Event (SCORE float, +1 implicit engine frag); the port adds (ScoreCarrierFrag-1) to attacker.ScoreFrags directly. Functionally equivalent; mechanism (ScoreFrags vs the team-aware SCORE pipeline) divergence, not magnitude.
- `keyhunt.unreachable.damage_destroy` — Constant g_balance_keyhunt_damageforcescale (1 / unread (force re-team uses the raw force vector, not damageforcescale))
- `keyhunt.notify.center_info` — Capture VFX uses TE_EXPLOSION at the midpoint as a stand-in for te_customflash; the NEXUIZPLASMA carrier-chain trail is faithful.
- `keyhunt.hud.objective_status` — Recorded divergence — VERIFIED self-consistent: Base packs the SVQC NUM_TEAM code (red 5/blue 14/yellow 13/pink 10) and CSQC HUD_Mod_KH decodes ((s>>5i)&31)-1, comparing against CSQC NUM_TEAM (4/13/12/9). The port instead packs (teamIndex+1) (red 2..pink 5), and ModIconsPanel.DrawKeyhunt uses the SAME ((s>>5i)&31)-1 decode but compares against Num1..Num4 = 1..4 (a 1-based index) with MyTeam also 1-based. dropped (30->29 / case 29) and self (31->30) handling match Base exactly. Net: the rendered icons are observationally identical; only the on-wire team encoding differs (a port-internal convention, never interoperating with a Base server). Flagged in KeyHunt.PackKeyState. Constant slot_encoding (4 x 5-bit; 0 none / 30 dropped / 31 self / else key.team = SVQC NUM_TEAM code (5/14/13/10), CSQC decode -1 -> CSQC NUM_TEAM (4/13/12/9) / 0 none / 30 dropped / 31 self / else team INDEX+1 (2..5), panel decode -1 -> 1..4)
- `lms.lives.eliminate_at_zero` — Base sets frag_target.frags = FRAGS_PLAYER_OUT_OF_GAME (sv_lms.qc:617) so the engine/scoreboard treat the player as ranked-out; the port tracks OutOfGame in LmsState (and networks LMS_RANK, which the local InfoMessage + win count read) but never sets a Player.FragsStatus = FragsOutOfGame sentinel (the -616 constant exists but only CTS uses it). The ClientCommand_Spectate gate IS now implemented (Commands.cs:1555-1560), but keyed on LmsState.OutOfGame && Rank>0 rather than the frags field — a different source of truth than Base. UPDATED this pass (the gate is no longer absent; see lms.rules.no_throw_and_spec_lockout). Rank is counted over LmsState players, not the QC FRAGS_PLAYER set; equivalent on the live path now that RemovePlayer reshuffles ranks on disconnect (lms.forfeit.remove_player) but a different source of truth than Base.
- `lms.win.last_standing` — Ranks/totalPlayed are counted over LmsState, not the QC FRAGS_PLAYER / LMS_RANK set; equivalent result on the live path but a different source of truth. Win latched from a per-frame poll (DriveGametypeFrame, GameWorld.cs:3404) vs Base's CheckRules_World tick — the result is equivalent and the tie now feeds the same OverTimeManager cascade the other timed modes use.
- `lms.lives.starting_count` — Constant legacy_defaults (9 20 0 (lives=9 legacy) / not modeled)
- `lms.join.new_player_lives_gate` — Base tracks lms_lowest_lives as a running global updated in GiveFragsForKill; the port recomputes LowestLives() from a running _lowestLives field updated in OnDeath (equivalent result, different mechanism). The late joiner gets the correct lowest-life count and the lockout is enforced; the late-join least-healthy HEALTH/ARMOR clone (Base PlayerSpawn, tracking last_forfeiter_*) is now ALSO applied — RemovePlayer records the last-forfeiter values and OnPlayerSpawn (LastManStanding.cs:1001) consumes them on the joiner's first spawn (FIXED this pass; see lms.loadout.start_items).
- `lms.loadout.start_items` — DEVIATION (port mechanics, not Base behavior): the late-join least-healthy clone reads the recorded last_forfeiter_* from the LmsState/_lastForfeiter fields and scans the live LmsState roster for same-lives living players, rather than Base's FOREACH_CLIENT over the QC LMS_LIVES field. Equivalent result; different source of truth.
- `lms.loadout.weapon_arena` — Port's ExpandWeaponArena resolves `most_available` to weapons_most (the no-map-weapon fallback Base itself uses when no map weapons are present) rather than intersecting with the actual map weapon set — a documented port-wide divergence. For LMS this is benign in practice (LMS suppresses map items anyway), but it is not a true `_available` intersection.
- `lms.items.no_pickups` — FilterItemDefinition is faithful + live. The HealthMega→extralife *replacement* (Base's separate FilterItem hook + lms_replace_with_extralife, sv_lms.qc:653-672) is folded into OnFilterItemDefinition: a HealthMega is retagged in place to item_extralife (RES_HEALTH zeroed) and grants lives via the live MutatorHooks.ItemTouch path. Since g_lms_extra_lives defaults 0, both Base and port suppress everything, so the live default behavior matches (see lms.item.extra_life). DEVIATION (port mechanics): the port matches item definitions by ClassName/NetName string ('item_extralife'/'item_health_mega'/'item_healthmega') because the item registry isn't fully ported, vs Base comparing def == ITEM_ExtraLife/ITEM_HealthMega pointers. Same stand-in Mayhem/NIX use.
- `lms.leader.computation` — UpdateLeaders runs each frame (DriveGametypeFrame line 2827) and sets IsLeader/LeadersLivesDiff faithfully. The leaders now drive the live VISIBLE_LEADER/OTHER centerprints (lms.leader.notifications), the live EF_ADDITIVE|EF_FULLBRIGHT leader glow (lms.leader.glow), the live WP_LmsLeader radar waypoint (lms.leader.waypoint), AND the live HUD mod-icon via the networked LeaderCount/LeadersLivesDiff/LeadersVisible stats (lms.hud.mod_icon) — ALL leader effects are now wired. No remaining dead leader effect. Base also re-runs lms_UpdateLeaders from PlayerDied and lms_RemovePlayer; the port drives it once per frame AND from RemovePlayer (LastManStanding.cs:467), but the PlayerDied re-trigger is folded into the per-frame call only (acceptable equivalent).
- `lms.leader.glow` — Base keys the glow off the leader's attached waypoint sprite (player.waypointsprite_attachedforcarrier); the port keys it directly off LmsState.IsLeader (the same set the waypoint attaches to via UpdateLeaders / CollectWaypoints). Equivalent leader→glow mapping, different keying — minor, since the waypoint set IS the IsLeader set.
- `lms.leader.waypoint` — Faithful + live: the periodic show-for-5s / hide-for-25s + random*10s jitter VISIBILITY-WINDOW TIMER (DriveLeaderVisibility, LastManStanding.cs:811, wp_time/interval/jitter 5/25/10 matching sv_lms.qc:17-19) gates BOTH the centerprints AND the WP_LmsLeader sprite. CollectWaypoints (LastManStanding.cs:856) emits the 'LmsLeader' sprite per in-game leader (RADARICON_FLAGCARRIER=1, +64z, VisibleForPlayer=LeadersVisible && !observer), pulled live each tick via ServerNet.SendWaypoints (ServerNet.cs:1056). The sprite is registered in Waypoints.cs:145 (Add("LmsLeader","Leader","",Cyan)) and rendered client-side by WaypointSpriteLayer. Minor: Base uses the player's per-client colormap radar tint (colormapPaletteColor) for the sprite; the port uses the fixed Cyan registry color (Team.None). The visible-for-spectator-of-leader suppression is approximated as !observer rather than Base's exact 'spectator viewing THIS leader' check.
- `lms.leader.notifications` — Faithfully fired on the rising edge, but the port centerprints EVERY in-game player (leader vs other variant) without Base's IS_REAL_CLIENT guard — harmless for bots (NotificationSystem.Center no-ops on a non-real client) but a different gate than Base's `if (... && IS_REAL_CLIENT(it))`.
- `lms.hud.mod_icon` — DrawLms renders the full HUD_Mod_LMS_Draw: the leader-count icon (player_neutral, with a flag_stalemate overlay while LmsLeadersVisible), the leader count number, and the colored '+N' lives-diff (yellow / orange at diff==3 / red at diff>=4). The art (player_neutral/flag_stalemate icons) falls back to a stand-in glyph if the textures aren't present — a presentation-asset detail, not a logic gap.
- `lms.hud.no_lives_message` — Port string is the no-lives info/center wording vs Base's exact phrasing — semantically equivalent. The Base hook keys on scores(ps_primary) [SP_LMS_RANK] > 0; the port reads the local row's networked LMS_RANK column, the same signal.
- `lms.damage.dynamic_vampire` — Port reads attacker/target lives from the live LmsState (GetState, lazily seeded to StartingLives) rather than the QC LMS_LIVES scoring field; result is equivalent on the live path (every player is seeded on first GetState), but it is a different source of truth than Base's GameRules_scoring_add(.., LMS_LIVES, 0).
- `lms.item.extra_life` — Replacement is wired live: OnFilterItemDefinition folds in Base's FilterItem + lms_replace_with_extralife by retagging the HealthMega edict in place (ClassName='item_extralife', RES_HEALTH zeroed) and keeping it; OnItemTouch (subscribed to MutatorHooks.ItemTouch, fired from ItemPickupRules.FireItemTouch) then grants g_lms_extra_lives lives + CENTER_EXTRALIVES and consumes the item. Dormant under the default g_lms_extra_lives=0. DEVIATION (port mechanics, not Base behavior): the replacement retags the EXISTING HealthMega edict in place instead of spawning a fresh ITEM_ExtraLife (Item_CopyFields + StartItem, Base sv_lms.qc:653-658) — the port has no item_extralife Pickup def in the registry. End state is equivalent. SpawnExtraLife/ExtraLifeTouch remain an unused alternate free-standing spawn API.
- `lms.scoreboard.columns` — Column declaration + sort are faithful; the join-time LMS_LIVES seed (AddPlayer→SyncColumns) is live via GametypeOnJoin, so a fresh joiner's lives column is populated on join rather than only after their first death.
- `lms.forfeit.remove_player` — Both removal paths are now wired: the DISCONNECT path (InfraClientDisconnect → RemovePlayer voluntary:true) AND the forced/voluntary MID-MATCH SPECTATE path (Base lms_RemovePlayer from MakePlayerObserver, sv_lms.qc:413-436). LMS now subscribes to MutatorHooks.MakePlayerObserver via OnMakePlayerObserver (LastManStanding.cs:969, Activate line 331), and that hook is dispatched live from ClientManager.PutObserverInServer (line 542) on any live-player→observer demote. NOTE: the port maps ALL MakePlayerObserver demotions to RemovePlayer(voluntary:true) — Base sets lms_spectate=true for both is_forced and killindicator_teamchange==-2 so both also route into the voluntary branch, but Base's PutClientInServer non-disconnect forced-out at pl_cnt+1 (the involuntary frags-OUT_OF_GAME rank) is collapsed into the voluntary cleanup; equivalent for the spectate/kick-to-spec case. RemovePlayer records last_forfeiter_* and the spawn-side consumer (PlayerSpawn least-healthy clone, OnPlayerSpawn LastManStanding.cs:1001) now reads them on a mid-match joiner's first spawn (FIXED this pass; see lms.loadout.start_items).
- `lms.rules.no_throw_and_spec_lockout` — DEVIATION (source of truth): the ClientCommand_Spectate lockout keys on the port's LmsState.OutOfGame && Rank>0 rather than Base's player.frags == FRAGS_SPECTATOR || FRAGS_PLAYER_OUT_OF_GAME (the port never sets Player.FragsStatus = FragsOutOfGame for LMS; the -616 constant exists but only CTS uses it). Functionally equivalent for an eliminated/ranked-out player; the FRAGS_SPECTATOR half of Base's OR (forbidding an existing spectator from re-spectating) is not separately modeled but is benign (a real spectator isn't running `spectate`).
- `lms.reset.map_players` — Base reset_map_players also re-PutClientInServer per client (re-spawns each re-livened player); the port's ResetMapPlayers re-seeds the per-player LmsState (lives/rank/leader) and the host's ResetMap loop handles the re-spawn separately. The leader-waypoint kill is implicit in the port (CollectWaypoints stops emitting for a cleared IsLeader set). Equivalent end state.
- `lms.respawn.deadflag_reset` — Base PlayerPreThink (sv_lms.qc:444-454): (a) exports REDALIVE/BLUEALIVE leader stats (covered by GametypeStatusBlock Kind.Lms, see lms.hud.mod_icon); (b) flips DEAD_DYING → DEAD_RESPAWNING so living players auto-respawn without a fire press. Port covers (b) via RespawnFlag.Force (ApplyRespawnTiming, LastManStanding.cs:225) + DeadPlayerThink's Dying branch (GameWorld.cs:3537): with forced=true and RespawnTime == RespawnTimeMax on the first post-death tick, `forced && !(RespawnTime < RespawnTimeMax)` = true → immediately transitions DeadFlag.Dying → DeadFlag.Respawning, then waits in Respawning until now > RespawnTime. Exactly equivalent to Base's explicit flag flip. No separate per-frame LMS PlayerPreThink hook is needed.
- `lms.scoreboard.rank_intermission_write` — DEVIATION (port mechanics, not Base behavior): Base uses AddPlayerScore/AddToPlayer with a mutator hook that claims the write (bypassing the game_stopped clamp) specifically for SP_LMS_RANK. The port's SyncColumns uses GameScores.SetPlayer (LastManStanding.cs:348-349) which is unconditional — it directly sets the column array without checking GameStopped at all (GameScores.cs:446-452). So intermission rank writes work in both cases, via different mechanisms. No LMS-specific AddPlayerScoreHook registration is needed.
- `mapinfo.gametype.registry` — Constant fallback pretty name ("@!#%'n Tuba Throwing" (MapInfo_Type_ToText NULL) / none (DisplayName empty fallback))
- `mapinfo.generate.entity_scan` — Recorded divergence — Generation is kept in a per-content in-memory cache, cleared on fs_rescan. Existing authored and autogenerated sidecars take precedence and are never rewritten. Quake 3 shortcut and seeding modes are covered by mapinfo.arena.q3compat. The scan captures worldspawn music metadata and the target/trigger music override. Random stock CD-track selection and playback of generated music metadata remain open. Constant autogen fallback cdtrack (random 1..n from g_cdtracks_remaplist excluding g_cdtracks_dontusebydefault / none)
- `mapinfo.arena.q3compat` — Recorded divergence — Compatibility generation shares the in-memory native catalog. Existing authored and generated sidecars are read according to the selected mode.
- `mapinfo.load.map_settings` — Constant g_mapinfo_allow_unsupported_modes_and_let_stuff_break (0 / cvar absent)
- `mapinfo.load.loadmap` — Recorded divergence — Native reload creates a fresh GameWorld. The ending world restores mapinfo temporaries on its own cvar store before the next match loads. The console and campaign SettempCvars stack is separate from native mapinfo temporary settings.
- `mapinfo.list.allowed_maps` — Recorded divergence — The pinned Maplist_Init and gametype reset serialize the eligible catalog into g_maplist, subject to the QC string limit. Native resolves cvar, maplist.txt, then mounted catalog, with one map per buffer entry. The catalog fallback stays live and never writes itself into a cvar or file.
- `mapinfo.metadata.title_author` — Constant author separators (' by ' | ' (by ' | ' [by ' | 'Made By ' / ' by ' only (LastIndexOf, case-insensitive)) Constant titlestring ('^2bsp' | '^2title' | '^2bsp ^7// ^2title' via isRedundant / none (Title only; no composite titlestring))
- `mapinfo.metadata.cdtrack` — Recorded divergence — Base ships the cdtrack to the client inside MapInfo_Map_clientstuff ('cd loop "N"' stuffed at connect). The port's client reads the map's .mapinfo cdtrack line directly from its own VFS (NetGame.SetupMapMusic) — same file, same value, no wire round-trip; the worldspawn music/noise fallback comes from the in-process GameWorld.CdTrack. Equivalent for a listen server; a remote client with the same content also resolves it locally. The random autogen cdtrack (no cdtrack + no worldspawn music → random track from g_cdtracks_remaplist excluding g_cdtracks_dontusebydefault, mapinfo.qc:1110-1118) remains absent — such a map is silent instead of getting a random stock track. The .wav/.ogg suffix strip on a worldspawn music value (mapinfo.qc:1103-1106) is handled by the port's track resolver (ResolveCdTrack handles numbers/bare names/full paths per NetGame.cs:1368).
- `mapinfo.settemp.per_gametype` — Recorded divergence — The native host accepts typed assignments to existing writable gameplay cvars. The client resolves binary r_sky, r_bloom and r_drawfog defaults from mounted metadata for the active map and mode. Players can opt in to receiving and applying server suggestions for those three fields in Effects settings or through cl_accept_server_map_visuals. Explicit boot pins and client preferences take precedence. The effects stay in the render binding until map change or disconnect, leaving persistent settings intact. The existing typed ClientInit field supplies authoritative fog. The wider upstream client cvar scope and console settemp_restore integration remain open. Native ClientInit carries only typed values for the three visual switches. Both upstream sv_foginterval branches use the native initial typed fog delivery. The renderer uses its existing exponential approximation, without the full DarkPlaces start-distance, saturation or height-fade behavior. It respects the client's r_drawfog preference instead of forcing it every frame. Includes are one local .cfg level through the mounted VFS. Relative traversal, rooted paths and nested includes are excluded. Constant system ACL (deny g_mapinfo_*, rcon_*, _*, g_ban*; then allow / same plus the native gameplay and three client visual allowlists) Constant g_mapinfo_settemp_acl (+*; first exact or prefix match / sampled at load for mounted settings and checked live against cached server suggestions) Constant server suggestions (server-issued client settemp commands / per-peer opt-in controls the optional one-byte ClientInit tail for bloom, sky and depth fog)
- `mapinfo.sizes.minplayers` — Constant file format (maps/<map>.sizes: line1=min line2=max / no reader)
- `mapinfo.infra.cache_glob` — Recorded divergence — Behavior-free storage plumbing: the DP db/buf string caches, the glob handle bookkeeping, the MapInfo_Map_* global temp reset (ClearTemps) and the shutdown teardown are engine-memory management with no player-observable behavior. The port's equivalents are ordinary C# dictionaries (MapInfoBackend._cache, MapInfoCatalog._entries) and GC'd objects; sv-world-rules.yaml already records that MapInfo_Shutdown is not performed on world teardown (harmless — nothing to free).
- `mapobject-func.driver.calcmove` — SUB_CalcMovePause (door_blocked pauses an in-flight bezier controller when a non-linear door is blocked without reversing) is NOT ported — DoorBlocked omits the move_controller pause. Only affects eased (non-default) doors that get blocked; default doors are linear so unaffected.
- `mapobject-func.driver.pushmove` — PushEntity has no unstick pass yet (FLY checkstuck=false nuance noted in PhysicsToss); not exercised by these movers in normal play.
- `mapobject-func.reset` — func_breakable_reset's team_saved restore is not reproduced (no team_saved field at this layer) and func_breakable_look_restore is presentation-only (colormod); the core behave-restore/destroyed state IS reset. door_reset's CSQC SendFlags |= SF_TRIGGER_RESET is skipped (door networking latent/commented in Base).
- `mapobject-func.door.slide` — door_reset (snap to pos1, STATE_BOTTOM, active, re-init keys) IS ported (DoorReset, Doors.cs:209; assigned Doors.cs:73) and LIVE (dispatched by GameWorld.ResetMapObjects). (see mapobject-func.reset.) Residual: the CSQC SendFlags|=SF_TRIGGER_RESET is skipped (door networking latent/commented in Base). no CSQC ENT_CLIENT_DOOR networking/draw (latent in Base too — door_link Net_LinkEntity is commented out); brush syncs via engine entity state.
- `mapobject-func.door.rotating` — shares the (LIVE) reset + CSQC gaps of mapobject-func.door.slide; inherits its NOSPLASH fix + sounds-key fix + the live .message centerprint (door_rotating runs the shared DoorTouch -> MapMover.Centerprint) AND the live key-door cpid notifications. Only the CSQC ENT_CLIENT draw remains (latent in Base; door_link Net_LinkEntity commented out).
- `mapobject-func.door.keys` — key doors can't be damage-opened (faithful early-out in OnDeath: itemkeys!=0 keeps it alive) — the NOSPLASH branch and the itemkeys early-out are both independent OnDeath early-outs so order is immaterial.
- `mapobject-func.rotating` — Recorded divergence — Looping ambient noise (CH_AMBIENT_SINGLE) uses the sound facade Play, not QC's per-player soundto(MSG_ONE)/init_for_player resend — no client roster at this layer (project-wide reduction). Spin behavior is fully faithful. func_rotating_init_for_player (resend the ambient to a player who joins after spawn) is STILL not implemented; a late joiner may not hear an already-spinning rotator's loop. (Unchanged by Wave-1/2.) func_rotating_reset (re-apply STARTOFF active state on round restart) is LIVE — RotatingReset is assigned at MovingBrushes.cs:75 and dispatched by GameWorld.ResetMapObjects; STARTOFF spawn state is also applied at spawn-time and faithful. (CORRECTED 2026-06-25; prior 'STILL not wired' note was stale — see mapobject-func.reset.) RotatingSetActive does not start/stop the ambient on toggle (QC setactive plays/stops CH_AMBIENT_SINGLE) — only AVelocity is toggled. Cosmetic given the facade-only ambient. (Unchanged by Wave-1/2.)
- `mapobject-func.breakable` — Recorded divergence — func_breakable_damage realized via the Combat.Death hook (OnDeath) rather than a per-hit .event_damage shim: the pipeline drives HP below 1, then OnDeath restores HP so the kill early-out keeps it intact and either bounces (NOSPLASH/team-guard) or breaks. The killing-hit force (QC debrisforce) is not carried by the Death hook, but .debrisdamageforcescale defaults to 0 so QC's force contribution to debris is normally nil. Functionally equivalent for the modelled cases (NOSPLASH + team-guard now evaluated inside OnDeath before the break). func_breakable_colormod damage-indication (BREAKABLE_INDICATE_DAMAGE tint), waypoint sprites (WaypointSprite_*), and bot_attack/monster_attack registration are not ported (BehaveRestore/Destroyed don't toggle bot_attack or remove grappling hooks). func_breakable_reset (behave/destroyed-state restore) IS ported (BreakableReset, assigned Breakable.cs:82) and LIVE (dispatched by GameWorld.ResetMapObjects); spawn-time START_DISABLED/restore is also applied and faithful. Residual: team_saved restore + colormod look_restore not reproduced (minor; no team_saved field at this layer). (CORRECTED 2026-06-25; prior 'STILL not ported/wired' note was stale — see mapobject-func.reset.) mdl_dead wreck-model swap + debris MODEL rendering are presentation (debris entities spawn + fade but their visual is client-side); pointparticles break effect (.cnt/.count) not emitted. the killing-hit force isn't applied to debris (Death hook carries no force; near-zero effect at default debrisdamageforcescale 0).
- `mapobject-func.door_secret` — Recorded divergence — QC sets classname='door' and a per-entity .event_damage; the port uses a DISTINCT classname 'door_secret' (so it stays out of the shared door OnDeath/LinkDoors classname dispatch) and a real per-entity event_damage analogue (Entity.GtEventDamage, dispatched by DamageSystem.EventDamage). Slide mechanics, touch, blocked, use are otherwise identical. Forced by the shared-dispatch architecture. secret_reset round-restart re-arm is LIVE — SecretReset is assigned at TargetUtilities.cs:786 and dispatched by GameWorld.ResetMapObjects; spawn-time placement (oldorigin, shootable re-arm) is also applied and faithful. (CORRECTED 2026-06-25; prior 'STILL dead' note was stale — see mapobject-func.reset.) secret_blocked deals no damage (QC leaves the T_Damage commented out too — faithful). no CSQC ENT_CLIENT draw/networking (latent in Base); brush syncs via engine entity state.
- `mapobject-func.conveyor_ladder` — Recorded divergence — QC kept carried entities in global inventory lists (g_conveyed/g_ladderents) so the release pass sees an entity that left the sphere in one frame; the port has no IL, so each producer tracks its held set in a per-producer dictionary (faithful net effect). Overlap uses an AABB BoxesOverlap test rather than WarpZoneLib_ExactTrigger_Touch (exact brush-plane) — close approximation. The consumer (PlayerPhysics conveyor frame-of-reference + ladder climb + func_water waterlevel) is a separate already-ported physics unit. func_ladder bot waypoint tracetest (waypoint_spawnforteleporter_boxes) is bot-navigation only and is skipped — bots won't path up a func_ladder via this seam. overlap uses AABB BoxesOverlap not exact-trigger plane test (a non-axis-aligned conveyor/ladder brush could tag slightly outside its true volume). no CSQC ENT_CLIENT_CONVEYOR/LADDER draw/think (the client also runs the producer scan in Base for prediction).
- `mapobject-func.rainsnow.net_draw` — Recorded divergence — The dedicated ENT_CLIENT_RAINSNOW Net_LinkEntity wire (state/org/size/compressed dest/count/cnt/fade_end) is replaced by the shared-entity facade: WeatherSystem rescans Api.Entities.FindByClass(func_rain/func_snow) every 2s and reads the server entity fields directly (the same listen-server/demo seam as LaserRenderer). Per-frame te_particlerain/te_particlesnow spawning is replaced by one pooled GpuParticles3D per volume whose emission box is the same view-clipped effbox and whose budget is rate-matched to the QC density formula. whether Dest/ParticleCount/Cnt/FadeEndDist reach a REMOTE dedicated-server client over the generic entity wire is unverified (facade scan is exact only where server entities are locally visible — listen server/demo). per-frame emission is a continuous emitter sized perSec*lifetime, not DP's floor(mycount*drawframetime+random()) per-frame batch — rate-equivalent, not per-frame-exact (timing partial). visuals approximated: rain streak quad 0.5x24 vs DP spark-stretch, snow ±16/axis drift modeled as Spread 8, Quake palette reduced to the 16-gray ramp; MaxAmountPerVolume 4000 cap.
- `mapobject-func.stardust` — Recorded divergence — QC CSQCMODEL_AUTOINIT/AUTOUPDATE (per-entity Net_LinkEntity re-send each 0.25s) is subsumed by the port's shared-entity facade — the entity fields ARE the csqcmodel channel, so StardustThink is a reschedule-only heartbeat. The server contract (Effects=EF_STARDUST) is what matters and is set. presentation UNVERIFIED: the EF_STARDUST particle consumer is live for entities in the client _entityNodes pass (CsqcModelEffects.Apply called at ClientWorld.cs:1351), but whether an inline-BRUSH func_stardust prop enters _entityNodes (vs only item/player/monster models) was not traced — the sparkle field may never render over a brush stardust.
- `mapobject-func.driver.subs_helpers` — SUB_Friction is declared+defined in Base but has ZERO Base callers (grep: only subs.qh:5 decl + subs.qc:24 def) — not ported, faithful to its own deadness. SUB_NullThink not ported as a symbol — the port leaves Think null where QC parks SUB_NullThink 'for PushMove' (equivalent: MoveTypePhysics runs Push movers regardless of Think). Spiderbot.cs:562 carries a private duplicate FadeGib re-implementation of SUB_SetFade rather than calling MapMover.SubSetFade (cosmetic code-reuse nit, both faithful).
- `mapobject-func.contracts.headers` — door.qh sv_doors_always_open autocvar + Q3_DOOR_CRUSHER are q3compat — omitted per repo policy (documented intended divergence in the spec, not re-flagged). conveyor.qh g_conveyed / ladder.qh g_ladderents IntrusiveLists are replaced by per-producer held-set dictionaries (the mapobject-func.conveyor_ladder row's recorded intended divergence); ladder.qh .ladder_entity -> Entity.LadderEntity is ported+live there. door.qh CSQC .door_finished preload field has no port CSQC half (door networking latent in Base too).
- `mapobject-misc.laser.server_logic` — Recorded divergence — End effect is resolved by NAME (Entity.LaserEndEffect) instead of QC's particle-effect integer .cnt, and the detector latch uses a dedicated Entity.LaserHitLatch bool instead of QC's .count field (a mapper-set count no longer pre-latches). No shipped map uses a numeric cnt key. Net_LinkEntity/laser_SendEntity is dropped (the client reads the shared entity off the ambient facade). Documented in Laser.cs.
- `mapobject-misc.laser.beam_render` — Client trace runs at the server tick origin (no InterpolateOrigin_Do) — negligible since stock-map lasers are static; the beam ribbon is a two-crossed-quad stand-in for QC's Draw_CylindricLine.
- `mapobject-misc.dynlight.server_state` — Recorded divergence — The path-following branch reimplements a dynlight-specialised train_next/train_wait inline (PathNext/PathWait) on the public MapMover.CalcMove seam rather than calling MovingBrushes.TrainNext, because a dynlight is a zero-size point light that exposes none of the bbox/TRAIN_CURVE/TRAIN_TURN/NEEDACTIVATION keys the full func_train pather handles. Net behavior (travel corner chain at .speed, fire each corner's targets, honour per-corner .wait/.speed) matches QC's train_next reuse. Documented in DynamicLight.cs header.
- `mapobject-misc.dynlight.render` — Recorded divergence — One persistent Godot OmniLight3D per dynlight, driven each frame from the shared server entity (light_lev->OmniRange, color->LightColor/LightEnergy hue/energy split, origin->position so it follows path/FOLLOW/tag-attach). Shadows disabled by default (matches Base's commented-out PFLAGS_NOSHADOW default and the budget). avelocity spin is intentionally not applied (an omni point light has no orientation). Scans the ambient entity facade (Api.Entities.FindByClass) like LaserRenderer, so it lights up on listen-server/demo and idles on a pure --connect client — the established client-fx seam. Named .style lightstyle ANIMATION is applied only by modulating the dynlight's own radius (LightStyles.Sample at 10fps). DP additionally animates WORLD-BRUSH lightmaps for named styles; the port has no lightmap-modulation subsystem, so styled world geometry renders steady (only the dynlight node flickers). Constant style lightstyle animation (server/world.qc:882-920 style table @10fps a..z / LightStyles.Sample modulates radius (radius-modulation only, not lightmap))
- `mapobject-misc.keys.item_key` — Only the Q3COMPAT_COMMON QL +8z origin nudge is omitted (no Q3-compat global; documented). NOTE: the item_keys_names[lowestbit] save IS implemented (MapObjectsRegistry.cs:460 records this.netname per key bit; ItemKeysKeylist :386 builds the keylist string) and is LIVE — consumed by Doors.cs:537 and Triggers.cs:946 for the door/keylock 'You need <the SILVER key>' centerprints.
- `mapobject-misc.bgmscript.adsr` — Recorded divergence — Native listen matches read map-authored float fields and animate client-owned presentation nodes. Native snapshots carry no QC entity fields, and the native path avoids running the QC VM.
- `mapobject-misc.models.props_walls` — Recorded divergence — The g_clientmodel_* Net_LinkEntity (ENT_CLIENT_WALL) stream is omitted: the listen-server/demo client reads the shared entity off the ambient facade (the seam every client fx uses). No shipped stock map depends on the antiwall relay or bgmscript on these. Documented in MapModels.cs. Colormap value is stored on Entity.ColorMapOverride but render consumption (team-tinting the prop) is a follow-up seam — a colormapped prop is not actually recolored on screen yet (grep ColorMapOverride: only stored at MapModels.cs/MapObjectFieldsExtra.cs, no renderer consumer).
- `mapobject-misc.models.clientwall_fade` — Constant alpha = (alpha_min + alpha_max * bound(0,(fade_end-dist)/(fade_end-fade_start),1)) / 100 (yes / fields stored, render formula NOT applied) Constant single-distance cut (fade_end==fade_start) (alpha=0 past fade_start / not applied (render)) Constant PVS cull -> alpha 0 (!checkpvs => alph=0 / not applied (render))
- `mapobject-misc.pointparticles.server_logic` — Recorded divergence — Native listen clients consume the shared entity's state and live origin directly. This replaces Net_LinkEntity, SendFlags and pointparticles_think for the local path. The server stores the effect name and authored noise parameters. The client resolves assets on demand and emits the sound. Native standalone clients lack the point-particle entity stream. The server also omits QC's sound precache and separate numeric effect-id field on this path.
- `mapobject-misc.pointparticles.client_emit` — Recorded divergence — The native local renderer samples the live emitter once per frame, rebuilds placement when it moves, and forwards each accepted emission to EffectSystem.Spawn for the selected particle backend. It plays authored noise directly at the emission point on the existing client audio path. Constant brush containment (WarpZoneLib_BoxTouchesBrush; absolute retries while fail <= 64*n / unrotated brush-plane check; same retry rule; rotated brush uses box) Constant movedir projection (trace 4096 per sampled point; add normal × |movedir| / world-only trace 4096 per sampled point; add normal × |movedir|)
- `mapobject-misc.laser.wire_contract` — Recorded divergence — Net_LinkEntity/laser_SendEntity is deliberately dropped: the client reads the shared server entity off the ambient facade (Api.Entities.FindByClass scan in LaserRenderer), the seam every port client-fx uses — same rationale already recorded on mapobject-misc.laser.server_logic. The delta protocol itself is therefore intentionally unimplemented. The facade replacement only exists where the client shares the server process: on a pure --connect client there is no ambient facade, so laser state never reaches it and no beam renders — the one observable behavior the dropped stream would have provided. Constant SF_LASER_UPDATE_ORIGIN/TARGET/ACTIVE/EFFECT (BIT(0)..BIT(3) / no wire — client reads Origin/mangle/Active/effect off the shared entity every frame) Constant SF_LASER_NOTRACE/SCALE/ALPHA/FINITE (BIT(4)..BIT(7) optional-field presence bits / no wire — fields read directly)
- `mapobject-misc.keys.usekey_keylist` — Constant item_keys_names source (empty until an item_key spawns (keys.qc:240 records this.netname) / seeded with the 6 stock bit names, then overwritten per spawned key (MapObjectsRegistry.cs:379-387, :474))
- `mapobject-misc.models.wall_lod` — Constant loddistance wire clamp (bound(0, loddistance, 32767) / n/a) Constant LOD2 fallback (!lodmodelindex2 && lodmodelindex1 => lod2 = lod1 / n/a)
- `mapobject-movers-platforms.plat.spawn_setup` — draggable=drag_undraggable and effects|=EF_LOWPRECISION not set (no gameplay/networking impact in the port). q3compat-specific defaults (speed 200/lip 8/dmg 2/spawnflags 0/medplat/CPMA probe) live under the q3compat flag — see the dedicated plat.q3compat_defaults row (now LIVE; only the CPMA file-probe remains a gap).
- `mapobject-movers-platforms.plat.q3compat_defaults` — Constant CPMA sound_start/sound_end + pt1_strt/pt1_end probe (honored / ignored (documented map-pack file-probe gap))
- `mapobject-movers-platforms.plat.reset` — SendFlags |= SF_TRIGGER_RESET not set (CSQC reset networking; dead in stock Base — plat_link commented out — so no live impact).
- `mapobject-target.speed.use` — Recorded divergence — The Q3COMPAT launcher branch (which accumulates launcherspeed inside the per-axis loop — a deliberately-preserved Q3 bug) is not taken because the port has no Q3COMPAT flag; the faithful non-Q3 path is always used. Also the CSQC ENT_CLIENT_TARGET_SPEED net copy (for client prediction) is not ported — the server-side velocity edit is authoritative. On a Q3-compat map a launcher target_speed would apply a different (larger) speed in Base; the port always uses the non-Q3 magnitude. No CSQC-side velocity application, so a target_speed mid-air boost may visibly correct on the next server update rather than predict instantly.
- `mapobject-target.spawnpoint.use` — The .reset (target_spawnpoint_reset, re-asserts ACTIVE_ACTIVE) is a no-op trivially-satisfied invariant; no port .Reset hook installed but immaterial (Active is already ActiveActive).
- `mapobject-target.location.spawn` — QC NearestLocation falls back to the nearest g_items entity (findnearest checkitems=true) when no target_location wins; the port's reader only walks the location volumes (item fallback out of scope — documented at Chat.cs:648).
- `mapobject-target.changelevel.use` — The .gametype next-map gametype switch is wired (SwitchGameTypeHandler -> Cvars.Set("gametype", gt), GameWorld.cs:675), but it only sets the cvar for the NEXT changelevel boot — it does NOT live-switch MapInfo like QC's MapInfo_SwitchGameType. Faithful enough on the named-chmap path; a bare end-match (.chmap empty) carrying a .gametype changes the cvar but the immediate intermission/next-map still uses the current gametype context. Listen-server / authoritative: the CSQC side of the changelevel is not networked (no remote vote UI), but the rules-side fraction + map switch are server-authoritative and faithful.
- `mapobject-target.levelwarp.use` — Live only inside a campaign (g_campaign): outside a campaign the .use early-returns (matching QC). Campaign.LevelWarp itself is a real Unload/Load/Setup implementation — only reachable on the single-player campaign path.
- `mapobject-target.give.use` — QC iterates the g_items IL filtered by targetname; the port resolves by targetname over the whole entity index then filters to Item-flagged / item_* classnames — equivalent for placed items but would miss a non-standard item membership. The buff-item branch (GiveBuff + SND_SHIELD_RESPAWN) is folded into the GiveItemHandler pickup path; not separately verified. QC chooses CH_TRIGGER_SINGLE for powerups vs CH_TRIGGER for others; the port delegates the sound entirely to GiveItemHandler's pickup path (channel choice not re-verified).
- `mapobject-target.spawn.use` — Recorded divergence — The full $-field reflection templating (numentityfields / putentityfieldstring / the field DB, deferred replacement, self/activator/other/pusher/target/target2-4/killtarget/time substitutions, +offset/+random, helper spawnfuncs setmodel/setsize) and the *activator / FOREACH_ENTITY_STRING(target) EDIT modes have no port analogue and are intentionally cut (documented at GameWorld.cs:721-729). This wave wired the minimal viable CREATE path: a target_spawn with a `key value …` .message that carries a `classname` mints one fixed entity. The .count create-cap (target_spawn_cancreate) IS now honored — so create-mode is a faithful subset, edit-mode + templating are the deliberate cut. NEWLY LIVE this wave (was dead): SpawnEntityHandler is now assigned in the live GameWorld.Boot block (GameWorld.cs:730), so both the .use path and the ON_MAPLOAD deferred think now actually create an entity. The handler tokenizes .message into key/value pairs, extracts classname, mints an edict at .origin, runs ApplyDictFields (the same key→field path the BSP entity lump uses) and SpawnFuncs.TrySpawn. CREATE-ONLY: the *activator self-edit and the FOREACH_ENTITY_STRING(target) bulk-edit modes are still not modelled — a target_spawn that edits an existing entity (target set, or *activator) has no effect (intentional cut). The $-templating mini-language (field DB, deferred $-substitution, +offset/+random, setmodel/setsize helpers) is not reproduced — a .message that uses $-tokens spawns with the literal token text. origin parsing only supports the single-quoted-token form `origin "x y z"`; a three-bare-token origin would desync the 2-stride key/value loop (documented at GameWorld.cs:753-755).
- `mapobject-target.music.target_music` — Recorded divergence — The whole CSQC TargetMusic_Advance + TE_CSQC_TARGET_MUSIC net path is replaced by MusicPlayer.cs reading the live server entity state directly. On the listen-server (the primary play path) this is byte-for-byte the same audible result without a net round-trip; the priority stack (trigger > targeted target_music within lifetime > lifetime-0 default-slot claim > untargeted default > cdtrack) and lifetime/default-slot semantics are preserved. Fade default keeps the port's gentle 2s crossfade where QC's 0 means instant (no shipped map carries the fade keys). Listen-server ONLY: a remote/dedicated client receives no target_music override (the TE_CSQC_TARGET_MUSIC net message is not ported). target_music_use is still a GLOBAL override, not QC's per-activator (IS_REAL_CLIENT actor) + spectator (IS_SPEC && enemy==actor) override — every client hears the override. TargetMusicUse DOES guard `activator is null` + non-client, so a null/non-client relay no longer activates it, but the override is still applied globally rather than per-activator. QC fade 0 = instant; the port substitutes a 2s default crossfade. target_music_kill and TargetMusic_RestoreGame still have no analogue. Constant lifetime/fade_time/fade_rate (seconds (fade 0 = instant) / seconds (fade 0 -> 2s default))
- `mapobject-target.music.trigger_music` — Recorded divergence — Like target_music, the CSQC brush-overlap test (Ent_TriggerMusic_Think against the local view box) is replaced by a server-side Touch that stamps PushLTime, read by MusicPlayer with a freshness window. Equivalent on the listen-server. Listen-server ONLY (the ENT_CLIENT_TRIGGER_MUSIC net link to a remote client is not ported). On multiple overlapping trigger_music volumes the port takes the FIRST fresh one; QC's Ent_TriggerMusic_Think uses the LAST-touched. Could pick a different track where volumes overlap (code comment acknowledges this). Constant touch_freshness_window (per-frame Ent_TriggerMusic_Think while inside / TouchFreshnessWindow PushLTime window)
- `mapobject-target.voicescript.sequence` — play2 (per-player audible) maps to the shared sound facade on the Voice channel rather than a true per-client message — acceptable on the listen server but a remote client may hear another player's script.
- `mapobject-target.teleport_dest.spawn` — QC objerrors (refuses to link) a destination with no targetname; the port just registers it (a teleporter would fail to find it anyway). The ENT_CLIENT_TELEPORT_DEST net link (so CSQC can predict the teleport) is not ported; consumed only server-side.
- `mapobject-target.position.spawn` — Behavioral detail (target_push_init: SOLID_NOT passive point carrying origin/angles) is owned by the jumppad/trigger_push unit; this row only records that both classnames are registered and reached as arc destinations. Not independently re-traced for arc consumption here.
- `teleporters.trigger.active_gate` — Vehicle-teleportable check and turret exclusion are still approximated by 'IsCreature' rather than the explicit player.teleportable / player.vehicle.teleportable / IS_TURRET checks (minor — no turret/vehicle on the common path).
- `teleporters.trigger.touch_and_targets` — No EXACTTRIGGER_TOUCH brush-face precision test (box overlap only) — teleport.qc:56.
- `portals.entity.lifecycle` — Constant g_balance_portal_health (200 / none (indestructible warpzone; cvar unused)) Constant skins (0=in/1=out/2=waiting+broken / none (transparent warpzone window)) Constant effects (EF_RED / EF_STARDUST|EF_BLUE / EF_ADDITIVE / none)
- `portals.teleport.transform_and_amazing` — Constant planeshift_bound (+/-48 (s/t bound onto exit plane) / warpzone transform (not the +/-48 plane-shift))
- `portals.weapon.cleanup` — Death/reset teardown (PortalClearAll) is dispatched only for the player's CURRENTLY-held weapon slot (DamageSystem/SpawnSystem dispatch WrPlayerDeath/WrResetPlayer on the active weapon), whereas Base's Portal_ClearAll fires irrespective of held weapon — so portals could outlive the owner if they died holding a different weapon. (Mitigated: ClientManager.PutObserverInServer / ClientDisconnect also call PortalClearAll directly.) The 15s timeout is a hard removal — Base's SUB_SetFade(time,0.5) 0.5s visual fade-out on expiry is absent (cosmetic; see portals.entity.lifecycle).
- `mapobject-triggers.multi.trigger_multiple` — q3compat explicit-wait-0-means-forever edge not distinguishable (both 'unset' and 'set to 0' arrive as 0f, so the port applies the 0.5/0.2 default rather than wait-forever) — negligible, rare q3 map pattern
- `mapobject-triggers.heal.trigger_heal` — Base trigger_heal_touch only runs EXACTTRIGGER_TOUCH when is_trigger (targetname==""); the port path does no exact-trigger clip (warpzone clip out of scope) — net effect on stock maps is none
- `mapobject-triggers.secret.trigger_secret` — secrets_found / secrets_total are counted (MapObjectsState) but the count is never surfaced as a 'N secrets found' HUD line (Base also lacks a built-in surface — latent)
- `mapobject-triggers.relay.trigger_relay` — delay_use in Base captures trigger as goalentity; the port DelayUse sets GoalEntity=null (the trigger arg is dropped at the 2-arg .use boundary) — harmless since no downstream .use reads the trigger arg
- `mapobject-triggers.gamestart.trigger_gamestart` — Recorded divergence — game_starttime defaults to 0 in the headless gametype (the host applies the real match countdown upstream via GameWorld.GameStartTime, which the now-wired LogicGates.GameStartTimeProvider reads); the no-wait branch uses a same-frame think instead of QC's InitializeEntity(INITPRIO_FINDTARGET) because the port has no priority-init pass for this family. Both are deliberate port-infra adaptations, faithful on a real countdown start.
- `mapobject-triggers.magicear.trigger_magicear` — strdecolorize is a no-op (chat color codes minimal) so MAGICEAR_NODECOLORIZE is largely inert
- `mapobject-triggers.keylock.trigger_keylock` — PS(toucher) per-stats key store: the port reads toucher.ItemKeys directly (no PS indirection) — equivalent on the server path
- `mapobject-triggers.teleport.trigger_teleport` — Recorded divergence — TELEPORT_MINSPEED/MAXSPEED STAT clamps (and the KEEP_SPEED branch that skips them) are out of scope: those stats default to 0 in stock physics, so omitting the clamp is a no-op on stock maps. TeleportRoundGateSuppressed() is a seam-stub returning false: the !g_race/!g_cts + round-pre-start telefrag suppression needs a gametype/round-state accessor not reachable from this file (faithful on non-race stock play). Bot waypoint probing and CSQC send/recv also deferred. TELEPORT_MIN/MAXSPEED magnitude clamps not implemented (no-op on stock maps where the stats are 0) telefrag !g_race/!g_cts + round-pre-start suppression stubbed (TeleportRoundGateSuppressed() returns false) — over-telefrags only in race/cts/round-pre-start, faithful otherwise
- `mapobject-triggers.music.target_music` — Recorded divergence — QC fade_time/fade_rate == 0 means INSTANT (state step = 1.0); the port's Fades()/EnsureSource substitute DefaultFadeTime (2.0s) for an UNSET/0 map key (MusicPlayer.cs:325-327,347-348), keeping the established gentle default crossfade. No shipped map carries the fade_time/fade_rate keys, so nothing regresses; when a map DOES set them the ramp uses the real values byte-for-byte. The ramp math itself honors the 0->instant branch (FadeTime>0 ? dt/FadeTime : 1f); only the unset DEFAULT differs.
- `mapobject-triggers.viewloc.trigger_viewlocation` — Recorded divergence — ADR-0005 and ADR-0011 carry resolved region anchors in authoritative native snapshots. Stock uses DarkPlaces CSQC entity links. Entry and exit latency between authoritative snapshots remains unmeasured. The FREEAIM cursor uses the configured art, size and color. Its separate draw path still omits stock crosshair fades and feedback animations.
- `mayhem.score.per_kill` — Recorded divergence — Port routes the per-kill recompute through the Combat.Death bus (OnDeath) and suppresses the +1 frag by running Scores in non-owning mode (OwnsScore=false) rather than subscribing to a GiveFragsForKill hook. Observable result (no ±1 frag; score == the recompute) matches Base; the kills/teamkills/suicides aux columns are still maintained by the obituary so the recompute reads them.
- `mayhem.loadout.start_items` — Warmup-only: Mayhem's 200/200 + full ammo is NOT applied during warmup. ApplyWarmupLoadout fires SetWeaponArena (so the arsenal IS correct) but never fires SetStartItems, so warmup HP/armor/ammo come from g_warmup_start_* cvars; Base's mayhem handler also sets warmup_start_*. warmup ships off by default so this is dormant in normal play.
- `mayhem.loadout.weapon_arena` — Recorded divergence — The SetWeaponArena seam is now LIVE on the real spawn path (Wave-2). The single residual divergence is the *_available variants: Base intersects the arena set with weapons present on the map (weaponsInMapAll), but the port has no per-map weapon set, so most_available collapses to weapons_most — exactly the fallback QC itself uses when a map has no placed weapons. Only differs on maps that actually place weapon pickups; deliberate (documented) until a per-map weapon set exists. most_available does not intersect with map-present weapons (no weaponsInMapAll equivalent); uses the full weapons_most set. Only observable on maps with weapon pickups placed. Constant most_available_map_intersection (weapons_start | (weaponsInMapAll & weapons_most) when map has weapons, else weapons_most / weapons_most only (no per-map weapon set))
- `mayhem.combat.regen_rot` — Recorded divergence — The port's PlayerRegen hook surface is narrower than QC's — it returns a single 'disable' bool instead of separately zeroing the regen arg (M_ARGV 2) and the rot arg (M_ARGV 3). With the stock defaults (both off) QC's `return (!regenerate && !rot)` is exactly the port's `return !regenerate && !rot`, so the observable result is identical; this is a deliberate narrower seam, not a defect. It would only diverge if one of g_mayhem_regenerate / g_mayhem_rot were enabled independently (non-default). If g_mayhem_regenerate XOR g_mayhem_rot were set (non-default), the port cannot independently zero one arg — it returns a single disable bool. Non-default config only; faithful in every shipped config.
- `mayhem.items.filter` — Item-class detection is by hardcoded classname string lists (item_strength/_shield/_invincible powerups; item_* ammo; weapon_* pickups) instead of the QC itemdef.instanceOfPowerup/Ammo/WeaponPickup flags — a custom/modded item could be misclassified.
- `menu-core.vm.lifecycle_toggle` — Recorded divergence — The QC menu is an immediate-mode VM redrawn every frame (m_draw); the port is a Godot Control scene graph (MenuRoot screen stack). The architecture swap is deliberate; behavior-level flows (toggle semantics, m_goto hide-on-close vs keep-open, escape handling) are ported on top of it. On disconnect Base reopens the menu and runs 'menu_cmd directmenu Welcome RESET' after 1 s (m_draw). Shell clears cached Welcome data during teardown and returns to the main menu. The g_campaign-reset+m_sync on disconnect is handled elsewhere (LEAVEMATCH_CMD analogue). No menu fade-in/out (menuAlpha 5/s, _menu_alpha cvar export for engine dimming) and no intro logo fade (menuLogoAlpha). menu_slowmo, cl_capturevideo frametime override, and menu_mouse_absolute/menu_mouse_speed handling are absent (Godot supplies OS mouse handling — partially intended). Constant MIN_DISCONNECTION_TIME (1 s (disconnect reset debounce) / none) Constant menuAlpha fade rate (5/s in, 5/s out; logo 2/s / no menu fade)
- `menu-core.vm.menu_music_sounds` — Constant menu_sounds (0 (1=click, 2=click+hover) / cvar bound in Audio dialog, never read by a sound player) Constant focus sound debounce (0.25 s / none)
- `menu-core.vm.tooltips` — Constant menu_tooltips (1 (0=off, 1=standard, 2=advanced w/ cvar info) / cvar bound in Misc dialog, not consulted by tooltip display) Constant tooltip fade (in 5/s, out 2/s, MAX_TOOLTIP_LINES 16 / Godot default tooltip)
- `menu-core.vm.draw_primitives` — Recorded divergence — QC's software draw layer (pictures, border-images, text with color codes, clip stack) is replaced wholesale by Godot Controls + a Theme built from the same skin assets (MenuSkin). Only observable output parity matters; the skin background/border/cursor art is loaded from the same gfx/menu/<skin> files. Multi-layer background (SKINGFX_BACKGROUND _l2.. layers with per-char alignment string) and the ingame background variant (SKINGFX_BACKGROUND_INGAME alpha) are simplified to one crop-covered texture. Constant MENU_ASPECT (1280/1024 canvas / MenuMetrics.ConWidth/ConHeight + DesignHeight 1080 scaling)
