- `menu-core.toolkit.item_widgets` — Recorded divergence — The retained-mode QC widget classes (Item/Container/ListBox/Dialog/Nexposee/ModalController + the anim/ keyframe-easing framework) are replaced by Godot's Control toolkit; the port re-implements only their cvar-binding contract (loadCvars/saveCvars ↔ CvarControls two-way binding) and navigation semantics. Widget-internal fidelity is out of scope by design.
- `menu-core.toolkit.xonotic_widgets` — Recorded divergence — These files skin/bind the generic item/* widgets (cvar hookup, skin constants, COMMANDBUTTON flags, special sliders like decibels/picmip/resolution). The port's CvarControls + MenuSkin theme carry the same responsibilities on Godot Controls. Special-slider VALUE DOMAINS (decibel formatting, resolution enumeration, picmip range) are audited where they surface — the settings dialogs (menu-dialogs unit).
- `menu-core.nav.gamemenu` — Constant LEAVEMATCH_CMD (defer 0.4 disconnect; wait; g_campaign 0; menu_sync / Disconnect hook + campaign cvar handling in Shell)
- `menu-core.nav.uid2name` — Recorded divergence — Approved records.nickname_prompt retains First Run and Profile for native local rankings. The player can open Uid2Name explicitly in a match. Pinned CSQC can ask on entry when the preference is undecided.
- `menu-core.browser.serverlist` — Constant menu_slist_maxping gate (only when >0 and !showLaggy / fallback 300 when cvar <=0 (Base skips the mask instead)) Constant focusEnter auto-refresh throttle (10 s / one-shot refresh on first show only)
- `menu-core.create.maplist` — Constant menu_use_default_hostname (1 → hostname "<name>'s Xonotic Server" / not implemented)
- `menu-core.create.gametypelist` — Constant menu gametype order (dm tdm ctf ca ft mayhem tmayhem ka tka kh lms dom nb ons as surv (+hidden rr cts inv when menu_create_show_all) / GameTypes registry order — not verified equal)
- `menu-core.keys.keybinder` — Constant MAX_KEYS_PER_FUNCTION (2 (3rd bind evicts all) / single bind per action (capture replaces)) Constant forbidden capture keys (ESC cancels; CAPSLOCK/NUMLOCK re-arm / ESC cancels; no CAPSLOCK/NUMLOCK special-case)
- `menu-core.sp.campaign_screen` — Constant instant-action LMS timelimit (-1 (LMS rolls set timelimit_override -1, unlimited) / 10 (port sets TimeLimit=10 for every roll incl. lms, SingleplayerScreen.cs:313))
- `menu-core.media.demolist` — Constant cl_autodemo recording (cl_autodemo records client demos / cvar is configurable through the checkbox; no native client recorder)
- `menu-core.media.guide.description` — Recorded divergence — Native clears the pane when a filter has zero matches and immediately describes the highlighted entry whenever there is a match. Stock can keep showing the previous description when an index stays zero or the list empties.
- `menu-core.media.guide.entries` — Recorded divergence — Native remembers the latest selected entry ID per topic and restores it when available. When the remembered entry is filtered out, native highlights the first match as a temporary selection. Explicit row choices, including a click on the temporary selection, replace the remembered ID. Stock can recover a name after zero matches but can carry a stale description and does not retain selections per topic.
- `menu-core.media.guide.topics` — Recorded divergence — Each topic lists shared entries in the pinned Guide order and appends native-only entries. Gametypes ends with Map Editor after Team Mayhem.
- `menu-core.misc.credits` — Constant scroll rate (1 item-height/s ((time - scrolling) * itemHeight) / 28 px/s fixed)
- `menu-core.util.updatecheck` — Constant update URL (https://update.xonotic.org/checkupdate.txt / none) Constant cl_startcount (incremented each boot before the query / not maintained)
- `menu-dialogs.settings.video` — Constant vid_vsync domain (0/1 checkbox / 0 off / 1 on / 2 mailbox (default) / 3 adaptive) Constant cl_maxfps auto override (value honored as-is (DP default 256) / 0 or 256 -> max(144, refresh); explicit menu values honored)
- `menu-dialogs.settings.effects` — Constant picmip cvar (gl_picmip via slider_picmip (VRAM auto-clamp) / gl_picmip plain TextSlider, no reader)
- `menu-dialogs.settings.audio` — Constant volume slider scale (decibels -40..0 (slider_decibels.qc) / linear 0..1 with % readout (same cvars))
- `menu-dialogs.settings.game_host` — Recorded divergence — QC's topic LIST + swappable ScrollPanel (SettingSource over the Settings registry) is replaced by a nested Godot TabContainer with a fixed page set. The one dynamic registrant in Base (damagetext's REGISTER_SETTINGS from common/mutators/mutator/damagetext/ui_damagetext.qc) is hand-included as the seventh tab, so the visible result matches; a future QC-side registry addition would need manual porting. Tab order differs slightly (QC: Crosshair, HUD, Messages, Model, View, Weapons, Damage Text; port puts Messages after Weapons) — cosmetic.
- `menu-dialogs.settings.game_crosshair` — Constant crosshair_enabled radios (0 none / per_weapon / 2 custom / same trio (per_weapon writes crosshair_per_weapon=1, not the makeMulti crosshair_enabled poke))
- `menu-dialogs.settings.game_hud` — Constant scoreboard fade slider (slider_sbfadetime.qc (fadeinspeed with tuned readout) / plain 0-10 slider on hud_panel_scoreboard_fadeinspeed)
- `menu-dialogs.settings.game_model` — Constant cl_nogibs values (1 None / 0.75 Few / 0.5 Many / 0 Lots / same values bound — but no reader) Constant cl_ghost_items_color presets (5 named R G B strings / same strings bound — no reader)
- `menu-dialogs.settings.game_weapons` — Constant apply command (sendcvar cl_weaponpriority / same string — inert in MenuCommand (no sendcvar case))
- `menu-dialogs.settings.input` — Constant m_pitch invert (checkbox value 1.022 (sign flip keeping magnitude) / InvertMouseCheckBox flips sign, preserves magnitude, default |0.022|... registered default '1')
- `menu-dialogs.settings.misc` — Constant menu_tooltips domain (0/1/2 (advanced shows cvar+command) / same values bound; tooltip system ignores the cvar (menu-core.vm.tooltips))
- `menu-dialogs.mp.serverinfo` — Constant qcstatus field keys (P pure / S slots / F flags / T tos / M modname (colon-tokenized) / subset parsed — depth unverified)
- `menu-dialogs.mp.create` — Constant per-gametype frag cvar map (gt.m_configuremenu per gametype (e.g. ctf capturelimit_override, lms g_lms_lives_override, nb g_nexball_goallimit) / hand-built ModeMenus table (66-88) — spot-matches ctf/lms/nb/race; full diff not done)
- `menu-dialogs.mp.create_mutators` — Constant buffs off-value (g_buffs cvarOffValue -1 / unverified)
- `menu-dialogs.mp.profile` — Constant apply command (color -1 -1; name "$_cl_name"; playermodel $_cl_playermodel; playerskin $_cl_playerskin / verbatim string (ApplyCommand) — every verb unhandled by MenuCommand -> inert) Constant name forbidden chars (\r\n\\\"$ maxLength -127 bytes / unverified on the port input box)
- `minigames.framework.registry` — Recorded divergence — The QC string-event dispatcher (minigame_event(mg,"start"...)) becomes C# virtuals (Start/Join/Part/Move/End); the port also registers an 8th original game (Snake) that Base does not ship. Both deliberate. The extra 'snake' entry appears in `cmd minigame list` / the Create menu — a Base client would never see it (cosmetic-only divergence, flagged so cvar/asset diffs don't trip on it). Constant registered_games (7 (nmm ttt c4 pong ps pp bd) / 8 (adds Snake, an original non-Base game))
- `minigames.framework.tile_math` — minigame_prev_team has no port counterpart (also unused by all 7 Base games — QC dead code).
- `minigames.framework.msle_networking` — Recorded divergence — The port deliberately replaces the per-entity MSLE delta protocol with a per-peer whole-session snapshot (Envelope carries netname + the peer's own team). Reconstruction-complete for grid games + Pong; simpler and reliable-channel safe. Session.Extra state is NOT serialized except Pong: NmmState ('nmm') and BdState ('bd') never reach the client, so NMM and Bulldozer boards are invisible remotely (see the per-game presentation rows). Spectator peers who merely watch a session get snapshots only via Participants(); QC additionally let any client join as SPECTATOR_TEAM and receive entities — port does seat spectators (team 255) so envelopes flow, but a non-participant never sees boards (QC ditto: CheckSend gates to players) — parity OK, noted for clarity. Constant send_model (per-entity delta (SendFlags byte + MSLE fields), gated per-client by minigame_CheckSend / whole-session snapshot per participating peer on a reliable channel, dirty-flag driven)
- `minigames.framework.infra_files` — Recorded divergence — Behavior-free build glue: the genmod.sh include chains and one-line '#pragma once' game headers. C# assemblies replace the QC include model; nothing to port. Cited so the coverage ledger counts every scoped file.
- `minigames.sv.session_lifecycle` — GameLogEcho ':minigame:start/join/part/end/invite' event-log lines are downgraded to Log.Trace — they never reach the sv_eventlog game log (Base writes them there).
- `minigames.sv.client_command` — QC re-tokenizes the tail and forwards ('cmd' event returns handled?); the port's ForwardMove forwards the whole tail string — equivalent for all shipped games, but the QC CENTER_JOIN_PREVENT_MINIGAME centerprint on a failed join is only a plain print here, and CENTER_JOIN_PLAYBAN on the playban gate is likewise print-only.
- `minigames.cl.session_activation` — Recorded divergence — QC detects 'this is MY player_pointer' from the networked slot to self-activate; the port's per-peer envelope carries LocalTeam explicitly. Same observable result (board opens on join, closes on leave/end). QC auto-opens the minigame MENU on activation (auto_close_minigamemenu) and re-runs CurrentButton on deactivate; the port opens only the board overlay — the menu must be toggled manually ('M').
- `minigames.cl.cmd_forwarding` — The Join list is scraped from ServerPrint lines of 'list-sessions' (a heuristic single-token-with-underscore filter) instead of QC's direct entity walk — a session named oddly or a print race could miss entries.
- `minigames.cl.turn_prompt` — Constant notify icon (minigames/<game>/icon_notif / none)
- `minigames.cl.hud_command_toggle` — Recorded divergence — Base ships NO default bind for the minigame menu (players type 'hud minigame'); the port binds 'M' for usability. Deliberate, noted in NetGame comment. No 'hud minigame' console-command path (users of the Base muscle-memory/console can't open it). QC refuses to open in demo playback (isdemo()) and force-closes during map vote (mv_active in HUD_MinigameMenu); the port has neither gate. Constant invocation (console 'hud minigame' (no default bind) / hardcoded 'M' key toggle; no console command)
- `minigames.cl.draw_helpers` — Constant board square (centered square = min(w,h) of the panel / centered square = min(vp)*0.7 of the full screen)
- `minigames.game.ttt.rules` — Recorded divergence — QC runs the singleplayer AI CLIENT-side (ttt_aimove in CSQC, moves applied locally); the port runs it server-side inside Place so there is one rules path. Same policy, same observable play. QC multiplayer next-match needs BOTH players to confirm (TTT_TURN_NEXT handshake); the port's NextMatch resets immediately on the first 'next' (no two-player confirmation phase). QC join REFUSES (return false -> 'Cannot join') into a singleplayer-vs-AI session (ttt.qc:160-162); the port's Join doesn't check the AI flag — the AI occupies the second seat so PlayerCount>=MaxPlayers and a second human is seated as SPECTATOR (team 255) instead of the join failing (TicTacToe.cs:44).
- `minigames.game.c4.rules` — No rematch path at all: QC c4 has no 'next' either (Base parity), but QC lets you re-create; noted only that the win state is terminal in both.
- `minigames.game.c4.presentation` — Constant board art (c4/board_under + pieces + c4/board_over overlay / single 'c4/board' lookup (misses the under/over split))
- `minigames.game.pp.presentation` — Constant piece tint (team1 '1 .3 .3', team2 '.3 .3 1', team5 = pp/piece_taken pic / generic TeamColor; team 5 renders as white disc)
- `minigames.game.bd.rules` — Constant sv_minigames_bulldozer_startlevel ("level1" (cvar, loads minigames/bulldozer/storage_level1.txt) / "level1" set by the shipped minigames.cfg:18 but NO code reads it; no level ever loaded)
- `minigames.port.snake` — Recorded divergence — An ORIGINAL port addition (documented in Snake.cs header): Base ships no Snake. Tracked so parity diffs don't flag it as an unmapped Base feature; it has no Base spec to diverge from.
- `monster-framework.spawn.setup` — DRIFT FIX (2026-06-26, Wave-8e): g_fullbrightplayers->EF_FULLBRIGHT (512) and g_nodepthtestplayers->EF_NODEPTHTEST (8192) are now applied at setup (MonsterAI.cs:282-285, QC Monster_Spawn:1484-1485); both default 0. Only g_monsters_edit->grab=1 remains unported — intentional (the port has no .grab field on a plain Entity; grab/drag is the SandboxObject path), so the residual is a single cheat-editor toggle, not a presentation defect. view_ofs = 0.35*maxs.z, the drop-to-floor tracebox + nudgeoutofsolid, and FL_FLY/FL_SWIM-from-type are folded into each descriptor's Spawn rather than the shared Setup (faithful effect, different placement).
- `monster-framework.spawn.miniboss` — DRIFT FIX (2026-06-27): the MonsterCheckBossFlag mutator hook (lets a mutator veto miniboss status, clearing MINIBOSS) is now WIRED + LIVE — Setup (MonsterAI.cs:285) calls MutatorHooks.FireMonsterCheckBossFlag and clears MonsterFlag_Miniboss on veto, exactly as QC Monster_Miniboss_Setup:531. No stock mutator uses the hook (same as Base), so the chain is wired for parity but never fires today. No residual gaps remain for this row.
- `monster-framework.target.validtarget` — DRIFT FIX (2026-06-26b): the PVS coarse cull is now PORTED + LIVE — ValidTarget (MonsterAI.cs:714) calls Api.Trace.CheckPvs(eye, targCenter) (gated on Api.Services, like the turret port) BEFORE the fine traceline, exactly as QC checkpvs(eye, targ). Headless tests with no compiled BSP PVS fall through to the traceline (deterministic). Closes the long-standing checkpvs omission. DRIFT FIX (2026-06-26b): the alpha<0.5 fade gate is now PORTED + LIVE — ValidTarget (MonsterAI.cs:700) rejects targ.Alpha != 0 && targ.Alpha < 0.5 using the live Entity.Alpha (DamageEntityState.cs:149, default 1=opaque; dropped to the invisibility alpha by PowerupsMutator.cs:383 / BuffsMutator.cs:957 / Cloaked composition). EnemyCheck (MonsterAI.cs:857) drops a chased enemy on the same gate. A cloaked/invisible target is now correctly un-targetable. DRIFT FIX (2026-06-26b): the vehicle-vs-melee rejection is now PORTED — ValidTarget (MonsterAI.cs:669) rejects targ.VehicleDef (a target driving a vehicle, VehicleCommon.cs:31) for a non-ranged monster (!Def.IsRanged), and the dead/notarget gates carry the matching !IS_VEHICLE guard (:676/:679). Mirrors QC Monster_ValidTarget:108-114. DRIFT FIX (2026-06-25): the typefrag/chat gate is now PORTED + LIVE — ValidTarget (MonsterAI.cs:684) rejects a chatting target when g_monsters_typefrag==0 via targ.ButtonChat, the live input mirror written by PlayerPhysics every physics frame. Matches QC default-1 (gate inactive).
- `monster-framework.target.find_enemycheck` — DRIFT FIX (2026-06-25): crouch 75% range scaling is now LIVE — FindTarget (MonsterAI.cs:720) scales trange by 0.75 for a crouching client via IsCrouching(it) = e.ButtonCrouch, the live input mirror written by PlayerPhysics.cs:242 (player.ButtonCrouch = input.ButtonCrouch). A crouching target now gets the QC stealth/range benefit. QC scans the g_monster_targets intrusive list (anything with .monster_attack); the port scans FindInRadius for players (+ other monsters only in teamplay). Behaviorally equivalent for the stock target set. (UNCHANGED.) DRIFT FIX (2026-06-26b): the alpha<0.5 enemy drop is now PORTED + LIVE — EnemyCheck (MonsterAI.cs:857) drops a chased enemy when en.Alpha < 0.5 && en.Alpha != 0 using the live Entity.Alpha (DamageEntityState.cs:149), so a chased enemy that activates invisibility/cloak is released, matching the now-live alpha gate in ValidTarget. The LOS-retrace + frozen drops were already faithful.
- `monster-framework.attack.dispatch` — QC selects the attack TYPE (melee vs ranged) in Monster_Attack_Check from enemy distance and passes it into monster_attackfunc; the port's AttackCheck only gates on enemy+cooldown and re-derives melee/ranged inside each descriptor's Attack. Equivalent outcome, different split. (UNCHANGED.) The infront-facing gate in Attack_Check (autocvar_g_monsters_target_infront || MONSTERFLAG_INFRONT) is not re-checked in AttackCheck (it is checked in ValidTarget at acquisition). (UNCHANGED.)
- `monster-framework.attack.primitives` — TraceToss is a stepped-traceline approximation of QC tracetoss (deterministic, ~4s/80 steps) rather than the engine's exact ballistic toss; close enough to gate 'will I land on my enemy', not bit-exact. (UNCHANGED.)
- `monster-framework.damage.pain_death` — Recorded divergence — damageforcescale double-apply (generic apply-push in DamageSystem.ApplyKnockback + the MarkPain self-push) is intentionally retained because QC does the same double-add for a pushable monster (recon section 8); removing either would halve monster knockback vs Base. event_heal/Monster_Heal as a per-entity delegate is also intentionally not modeled — the mage heals directly via HealPulse. DRIFT FIX (2026-06-25): the pain cue now routes through SoundChannel.PainAuto (QC CH_PAIN, -6) (MarkPain MonsterAI.cs:1587), not the earlier SoundChannel.Body — the channel now matches QC. Death is CH_VOICE in QC vs SoundChannel.Voice in the port (matches). The audio residual is now only the SND_BODYIMPACT1 placeholder below. The spamsound SND_BODYIMPACT1 body-impact placeholder on every non-drown/fire hit is still NOT implemented, and the take>50/>100 extra gib splashes are not implemented (client VFX). (UNCHANGED — this is why audio stays partial.) QC restores target2 = oldtarget2 on death for a redirected-patrol respawn; the port has no .oldtarget2 (documented minor deferral). (UNCHANGED.) DRIFT FIX (2026-06-27): WaypointSprite_UpdateHealth on each hit + WaypointSprite_Kill on death are now PORTED + LIVE — MarkPain (MonsterAI.cs:1817) calls UpdateHealthbar(st, health) and MarkDead (:1909) calls WaypointSprites.Kill(st.Sprite), both no-ops only when healthbars are off (Sprite null). The WP_Monster sprite now exists (see healthbar row). QC Monster_Dead swaps to mdl_dead corpse model + CSQCModel_UnlinkEntity + clears dphitcontentsmask BODY; the port sets DeadState/Solid.Corpse but the model-swap is presentation (deferred). (UNCHANGED.)
- `monster-framework.damage.loot_drop` — DRIFT FIX (2026-06-27): the MonsterDropItem mutator hook (QC sv_monsters.qc:51 — lets a mutator rewrite the itemlist) is now WIRED + LIVE — DropItem (MonsterFramework.cs:219) calls MutatorHooks.FireMonsterDropItem(self, itemList, attacker) and aborts if the returned list is empty, exactly as QC. InstagibMutator registers a live handler (InstagibMutator.cs:742 OnMonsterDropItem) that rewrites the loot for instagib, so the hook fires on a real gameplay path. No residual gaps remain for this row.
- `monster-framework.move.straight_2d` — DRIFT FIX (2026-06-27): Monster_Move_2D is now PORTED — Move2D (MonsterAI.cs:1255) is a line-for-line port of QC sv_monsters.qc:1161: forward 32u trace + reverse-on-wall/monster (not on a player) + ledge down-trace (32u, reverse unless allow_jumpoff) + the game_stopped/campaign/spawn_time movement gate (MatchHalted/CampaignMovementHeld) + walk/idle anim via DriveAnimFrame + movelib_brake_simple(0.6) on halt. It is a public Base API with NO caller in stock Xonotic (declared sv_monsters.qh:111, never invoked) — used only by mapper/custom monsters — so the port likewise has no live caller (liveness: na, matching Base's latent state). The omission is gone; the only remaining 'gap' is the latent no-caller, which mirrors Base exactly.
- `monster-framework.lifecycle.respawn_reset` — Respawn re-stamp is a subset of Monster_Spawn (health/solid/takedamage/spawnshield/think) and does not re-run monster_setupcolors (the difficulty colormap is not re-derived on respawn); it DOES re-apply miniboss EF_RED (see spawn.miniboss). Minor — a respawned monster keeps its first-life tint, which matches QC (monster_setupcolors is gated to !RESPAWNED in QC too).
- `monster-framework.spawn_drivers.stats` — editmob spawn disabled-message divergence: QC prints 'Monster spawning is disabled' when g_monsters_max<=0 || g_monsters_max_perplayer<=0; the port instead falls through to the count checks (same gating outcome — per-player 0 = off — different message text). Command-layer cosmetic. (UNCHANGED.)
- `monster-framework.presentation.healthbar_sounds_anim` — Recorded divergence — CSQC per-frame model frame playback (Monster_Anim frame groups, mr_anim, CSQCMODEL_AUTOUPDATE) is intentionally render-deferred — the headless authority port maintains only the server-side animation PHASE (idle/walk/run/attack/pain/death) that drives timing, the same authority/presentation split used by the turret and vehicle ports. The healthbar sprite is now IMPLEMENTED + LIVE + NETWORKED + rendered (no longer a gap). The monster sound system is wired (gate + throttle + per-monster SoundCues cue-gate + per-monster SoundDir/SoundExt/variant suffix) but resolves the path STATICALLY from the descriptor instead of parsing the .sounds file at runtime via GlobalSound + skin-keyed Monster_Sounds_Update — that static-path approximation is a deliberate fidelity simplification (no runtime per-skin sound packs ported), not a missing API. RE-VERIFY (Wave-16): the SoundCues set + SoundCueCount mirror the .sounds presence/variant test so a Base-silent cue stays silent and a multi-variant cue randomises — ALL FIVE monsters now match Base (Zombie/Golem cue sets; Spider/Mage/Wyvern empty-set fully silent, the old Mage/Wyvern==null is fixed). The quake_resize VISUAL scale*=1.3 is render-deferred (the collision bbox resize landed Wave-16); the Wave-8e server-side anim-variant/death-variant rolls are part of the same intended authority/presentation split (the server picks the animation GROUP; the per-frame playback is render-deferred). DRIFT FIX (2026-06-27): Healthbar waypoint sprite (WP_Monster) is NOW IMPLEMENTED + LIVE + NETWORKED — SpawnHealthbar (MonsterAI.cs:446) spawns a real WaypointSprite ('Monster', maxs.z+15, RADARICON_DANGER, team/red color, lifetime 0, maxdist 1024, hideable, INVINCIBLE-gated) when g_monsters_healthbars!=0 (gated at SpawnFromMap:604 + Respawn:2166); UpdateHealthbar refreshes the bar on every hit (MarkPain:1871) and on corpse damage; WaypointSprites.Kill removes it on death (MarkDead:1963) and on Monster_Remove (:727). The sprite serializes per-peer via ServerNet (SendWaypoints) and the client WaypointSpriteLayer renders the floating health bar via DrawHealthBar (game/client/WaypointSpriteLayer.cs:207, drawn when wp.Health>=0). This closes the baseline's #1 worst gap. RESIDUAL: the WaypointSprites infra defaults Health to a pre-normalized 0..1 fill (QC normalizes client-side by max_health) — equivalent display, different normalization placement. RE-VERIFY DRIFT FIX (2026-06-27, Wave-16 b3a9c06): ALL FIVE monsters now carry an explicit SoundCues set so every voice cue matches Base — Zombie={death,sight,idle}.ogg, Golem={death,sight,melee,pain,idle}.wav (per-cue SoundCueCount variant counts), Spider/Mage/Wyvern=EMPTY set (fully silent). The baseline's 'Mage/Wyvern still SoundCues==null (play-all)' divergence is CLOSED — Mage.cs:62 + Wyvern.cs:62 both now `new HashSet<string>()`, matching Base which ships NO mage/wyvern .sounds (only zombie/golem). So MonsterSound no longer attempts to play port-invented monsters/<dir>/<cue> samples for those three. RESIDUAL audio gap: per-cue resolution is STATIC-path (descriptor-derived), NOT the runtime .sounds-file GlobalSound parse + skin-keyed Monster_Sounds_Update (a deliberate fidelity simplification); SND_BODYIMPACT1 body-impact placeholder still absent. DRIFT (2026-06-26, Wave-8e) CSQC anim got closer (still the intended-divergence render-deferral): the server now maintains per-phase animation VARIANT indices (st.AnimVariant) and a death-variant roll (Def.RollDeathVariant() at MarkDead, e.g. zombie die1/die2, golem melee2/3 + pain1/2), driven into the descriptor's AnimFrame(phase,die2,variant) by DriveAnimFrame so the headless authority picks the same animation group QC would. The actual per-frame CSQCMODEL_AUTOUPDATE frame playback remains client-deferred. RE-AUDIT (2026-06-28, Wave-16 b3a9c06): g_monsters_quake_resize (MONSTER_SIZE_QUAKE x1.3) is PORTED but LATENT (no caller) — ApplySize (MonsterAI.cs:487-499) scales the COLLISION bbox mins/maxs *= 1.3 when def.SizeQuake && !respawned && g_monsters_quake_resize!=0 (default 1), BUT grep confirms ApplySize has ZERO call sites (src/+game/): the five monsters call Api.Entities.SetSize directly in their own Spawn (Spider.cs:102 etc.), with the explicit comment 'No Quake-resize: Xonotic-native, SizeQuake==false'. So the applier is dead-on-arrival, the SAME latent pattern as Move2D (liveness na). def.SizeQuake (EntityClasses.cs:106) defaults false so the stock five are unaffected and there is NO behavioral gap; the earlier 'PORTED + LIVE' claim was inaccurate (it is ported-but-not-wired). The matching VISUAL scale*=1.3 also stays render-deferred (no networked Entity render-scale field). RE-VERIFY DRIFT FIX (2026-06-27, Wave-13 5427e74): g_monsters_playerclip_collisions (DPCONTENTS_PLAYERCLIP, default 1) is NOW PORTED + LIVE — Setup (MonsterAI.cs:244-246) stamps e.DpHitContentsMask=SOLID|BODY|BOTCLIP|MONSTERCLIP and ORs in PLAYERCLIP when g_monsters_playerclip_collisions!=0; TraceService.GenericHitMask (TraceService.cs:562-563) honors a nonzero Entity.DpHitContentsMask on the monster's OWN movement trace, so monsters now clip playerclip brushes. Closed.
- `monster-golem.brain.think` — AttackCheck still does not honor the g_monsters_target_infront / MONSTERFLAG_INFRONT facing gate (QC Monster_Attack_Check:460); benign for golem at default cvars (no INFRONT flag, default off).
- `monster-golem.attack.lightning` — Constant projectile_scale (2.5 (gren.scale, golem.qc:145) / 1x (Entity carries no networked render-scale field))
- `monster-mage.attack.select` — Recorded divergence — Port re-derives melee/ranged from enemy distance inside Mage.Attack and calls Push/Teleport/FireSpike directly, rather than routing through WEP_MAGE_SPIKE.wr_think / OFFHAND_MAGE_TELEPORT.offhand_think. The weapon-entity wrapper is not modeled; the branch chances and gating are behaviorally equivalent.
- `monster-mage.attack.teleport` — RandomRelocate is an approximation of QC's MoveToRandomLocationWithinBounds builtin: 32 biased samples + ground-drop + bbox-fit test, not the engine's content-mask sampler (maxtries 256, jitter/dist params). Behaviorally equivalent (place at a random clear grounded spot within +/-1200) but not bit-exact — a LOGIC approximation, not a presentation gap.
- `monster-mage.think.decide` — QC mr_think scans BOTH clients (FOREACH_CLIENT) and monsters (IL_EACH g_monsters) for need_help; port NeedsHelpNearby uses one FindInRadius pass over all entities in heal_range, which conflates the two scans but covers both populations.
- `monster-spider.identity.registration` — Constant spawnflags (MON_FLAG_MELEE|MON_FLAG_RANGED|MON_FLAG_RIDE / not modeled (no per-flag field))
- `monster-spider.attack.web_projectile` — Muzzle origin differs: QC sets origin to CENTER_OR_VIEWOFS(this) then W_SetupProjVelocity; port uses owner.Origin+ViewOfs+dir*14 (SpawnProjectile). Minor spawn-point offset. QC bot_dodge / MIF_SPLASH|MIF_ARC / damagedbycontents flags not modeled (bot dodge-rating 0 anyway; affects bot avoidance + contents detonation).
- `monster-spider.web.monster_slow` — Recorded divergence — QC implements this as a MonsterMove mutator hook; the port folds the same 0.5 multiplier directly into MonsterAI.Move's speed computation (no separate mutator-hook chain for monster movement). Same observable effect; structurally inlined.
- `monster-spider.attack.player_wielded` — Constant impulse (9 / none) Constant player melee attack_range (60 / none) Constant player web prepareattack delay (g_monster_spider_attack_web_delay (3) / none)
- `monster-spider.menu.describe` — Recorded divergence — Base surfaces describe() on a dedicated MENUQC monster-book/page widget, which the port lacks. Following the DialogMutators pattern, the port rides the describe prose on the DialogMonsterTools spawn-radio tooltip (the closest faithful surface). Same text, different host widget.
- `monster-zombie.identity.def` — Class flags (UNDEAD/MELEE/RIDE) are documented in comments but not stored as spawnflags; MON_FLAG_RIDE (monster riding) is unported across the whole monster family. Constant spawnflags (MONSTER_TYPE_UNDEAD|MON_FLAG_MELEE|MON_FLAG_RIDE / doc-only (no flags int))
- `monster-zombie.pain.mr_pain` — Sound DELIVERY machinery (GlobalSound .sounds-file table, random sample, skin-keyed Monster_Sounds_Update) still unported — moot for the zombie since pain is correctly silent, but the path-form/sample-table seam is absent (latent).
- `monster-zombie.death.mr_death` — Sound DELIVERY uses literal monsters/zombie/death.ogg (SoundExt='.ogg') with no GlobalSound table lookup / random sample; the death cue IS a real shipped asset so this is audible/faithful in effect, but the table machinery is latent.
- `mutator-bloodloss.enable.registration` — Base REGISTER_STAT(BLOODLOSS) networks the threshold to CSQC for crouch/jump prediction; the port has no STAT(BLOODLOSS) and no CSQC mutator registration. Faithful in effect (server-authoritative) but the prediction path is absent.
- `mutator-bloodloss.rot.tick` — No runtime/automated test confirms the rot tick cadence or that the rot damage actually removes health in a live match (static + cross-file trace only; no bloodloss test in tests/).
- `mutator-bloodloss.rot.vehicle_eject` — Liveness depends on the rot tick reaching a vehicle-occupying player below threshold; not exercised by any test, and the port's vehicle mode is itself only partially live.
- `mutator-bloodloss.move.forced_crouch` — CSQC variant (bloodloss.qc:58-62) adds a STAT(HEALTH)>0 guard so a corpse isn't predicted-crouched. The port has no CSQC prediction at all, so crouch is server-authoritative only (no local prediction); behaviourally matches SVQC, but is not locally predicted.
- `mutator-bloodloss.move.jump_block` — CSQC variant (bloodloss.qc:63-67) adds STAT(HEALTH)>0; the port has no CSQC prediction, so jump-block is server-authoritative only (no local prediction).
- `mutator-bloodloss.presentation.active_mutator_signalling` — Constant active-mutator id (MUT_BLOODLOSS (g_bloodloss > 0) / none (no MUT_BLOODLOSS / mut_set_active / active_mutators surface))
- `breakablehook.remove.drop_chain` — Recorded divergence — Port's RemoveHook is private to Hook.cs; the mutator removes the chain by invoking the grapplinghook's own ProjectileDamage callback (Hook.cs:258: (self,attacker) => RemoveHook(actor.WeaponState(slot))), the same encapsulated path used when the chain is shot down normally. Behaviorally equivalent to QC's direct RemoveHook(frag_target): removes the chain entity and clears the owner's weapon hook slot.
- `buffs.item.spawnfunc` — Constant bot_pickupbasevalue (1000 / none)
- `buffs.item.touch_pickup` — The autoreplace preference is read from the server cvar rather than replicated per player.
- `buffs.item.needkill_respawn` — QC's ITEM_TOUCH_NEEDKILL is the engine's NODROP/sky contents test; port re-derives it with a zero-length trace at the item and checks DpHitContents&NODROP | DpHitQ3SurfaceFlags&SKY. Equivalent in practice; QC's mask also covers DPCONTENTS_SLIME/LAVA/SKY/BODY/DONOTENTER but NODROP is what lava/slime brushes carry.
- `buffs.item.respawn_cooldown` — Constant g_buffs_pickup_anyway (0 / none)
- `buffs.item.random_location` — Constant g_buffs_random_location_attempts (10 / not read (folded into RandomMapLocation))
- `buffs.item.new_type_weighting` — Constant weight (max(0.2, 1/seencount) / uniform)
- `buffs.item.available` — Port derives the start_items IT_UNLIMITED_AMMO state from g_use_ammunition (the dominant source of that start flag) rather than the literal start_items bitmask, so an unusual start_items source for unlimited ammo would be missed — but the common case (g_use_ammunition 0) is covered.
- `buffs.ammo` — Port fills each slot's clip via WeaponState.ClipSize/ClipLoad + SetWeaponLoad (FillSlotClip) on apply AND each tick, and restores the saved pre-buff clip loads on remove (RestoreAmmoClips) — matching QC's clip_load = weapon_load[switchweapon] = clip_size with buff_ammo_prev_clipload save/restore. Reachability of the clip path depends on the WeaponState clip fields being populated for the live weapon set.
- `buffs.magnet` — QC selects buff-vs-item range by it.buffdef; port selects by classname == 'item_buff' (equivalent in practice; item_buff items now spawn on maps, so the buff-range branch is reachable).
- `buffs.flight` — On removal QC restores gravity from trigger_gravity_check.enemy.gravity when standing in a gravity zone, else the saved old gravity; port mirrors this (actor.GravityCheck?.Enemy.Gravity, else BuffFlightOldGravity), so the gravity-trigger interaction is preserved where GravityCheck is populated.
- `buffs.carrier_model` — Constant light_lev (200 / none)
- `buffs.waypoint` — Per-viewer hide-if-already-held is modelled: SpawnBuffWaypoint sets wp.VisibleForPlayer = viewer => !Active(viewer, buff) (BuffsMutator.cs:607), consumed on the live per-peer send path (ServerNet.WaypointVisible:1297). The waypoint is killed on pickup (BuffTouch:425), re-attached + re-tinted on respawn (ScheduleRespawn:480-482), and pinged + colour-resynced on relocation (BuffRespawn:559-561). Residual: the waypoint colour is the per-buff Color triple via BuffColor, but the 'build-finished' arming is collapsed (the marker is spawned at activation, not on a separate WP build timer); minor vs Base's buff_Waypoint build-finished sequence.
- `buffs.drop` — Port omits the IS_INDEPENDENT_PLAYER guard on the INFO_ITEM_BUFF_LOST broadcast (sends AllExcept unconditionally); independent-player modes (e.g. some duel/race variants) are an edge case not modelled.
- `buffs.notifications` — Pickup: ITEM_BUFF_GOT center (picker) + INFO_ITEM_BUFF (others) + misc/shield_respawn sound all fire (BuffTouch). Replace: INFO_ITEM_BUFF_LOST (others) fires. Drop: ITEM_BUFF_DROP center + INFO_ITEM_BUFF_LOST + SND_BUFF_LOST all fire (OnPlayerUseKey). Tokens ITEM_BUFF/ITEM_BUFF_GOT/ITEM_BUFF_LOST/ITEM_BUFF_DROP all defined in NotificationsList.cs. SND_STRENGTH_RESPAWN on item (re)activation IS now emitted (ActivateBuff:336 + ScheduleRespawn:462 SoundSystem.PlayOn(item,"STRENGTH_RESPAWN")). SND_BUFF_LOST on a *replace* (autoreplace path) is commented out in QC too, so omitting it is faithful. SND_KA_RESPAWN is wired for swapper + item relocation.
- `buffs.port_extra_speed_invisible` — Recorded divergence — BuffsMutator still carries speed/invisible handler branches and fields, but the catalog registers NO buff_speed/buff_invisible def (only the 13 QC buffs are registered as buffs; 'speed'/'invisibility' are POWERUP defs, not buffs). QC speed/invisibility are PowerupsMutator powerups. So Active(player,"speed"/"invisible") resolves buff_speed/buff_invisible which are unregistered -> Has() false; the branches are inert dead code that can never fire. Harmless, but should be removed for clarity. Inert: no buff_speed/buff_invisible StatusEffectDef exists, so OnPlayerPhysics:674 (speed) and OnPlayerPreThink:797 (invisible) are unreachable. Constant g_buffs_speed / g_buffs_invisible (do not exist (speed/invisibility are POWERUPS) / _speedSpeed 1.5 / _invisibleAlpha 0.5 fields)
- `buffs.defs.qh_autocvar_fallbacks` — Only divergence is unreachable on a stock install: if mutators.cfg were NOT executed, Base luck would crit at x3 (the .qh autocvar fallback) while the port falls back to x2. With the shipped cfg both are 2 — effective behavior identical. Constant g_buffs_luck_damagemultiplier (code fallback, cfg absent) (3 (luck.qh) / 2 (field default))
- `mutator-bugrigs.player_physics.prevangles` — Recorded divergence — player.disableclientprediction = 2 is intentionally NOT written. In Base it is a DP server->client engine flag (dpextensions) that tells the native DarkPlaces client to skip its built-in movement prediction for the rig; no QC reads it. VERIFIED inert in the port: the client-prediction carrier (game/net EntityMovementStep -> PlayerPhysics.Move -> CallPmPhysics) runs the SAME RaceCarPhysics through the shared static MutatorHooks.PMPhysics chain on the shared Api collision world (PlayerPhysics.cs:364/446-449), so the predicted rig is already in lockstep with authority — there is nothing for a prediction-disable flag to suppress. Writing to a field nothing reads would be dead code. Same stance as Race's freeze. Constant disableclientprediction (2 / not set (verified inert))
- `mutator-bugrigs.racecar.drive_model` — Recorded divergence — Planar branch adds an explicit Api.Entities.SetOrigin(self, neworigin) because the port engine does not auto-integrate a NOCLIP entity's trace-derived origin the way DarkPlaces MOVETYPE_NOCLIP does; this reproduces the same net displacement Base gets from the engine push. Pitch-convention is per-call-site: FixedVecToAngles for the planar surface-align (bugrigs.qc:242-248 vectoangles), plain VecToAngles2 for the final body pitch/roll (bugrigs.qc:271-278) and the smoothing pass (bugrigs.qc:303), matching the QC vectoangles/vectoangles2 originals (the intermediate body angle is re-vectored by the smoothing pass, so the un-negated form is faithful).
- `mutator-bugrigs.clientconnect.chase_camera` — Recorded divergence — Base stuffcmds 'cl_cmd settemp chase_active 1' to the connecting client; the in-process listen server has no networked stuffcmd channel, so the faithful analogue is a direct Api.Cvars.Set('chase_active','1') (the client view reads chase_active each frame via NetGame CameraMode). The caller is gated to !p.IsBot because bots have no view (matches the human-only observable effect of the QC stuffcmd) and to bugrigsConnect.Added (mirrors QC ClientConnect only firing while the mutator is active).
- `campcheck.realclient.gate` — weaponLocked gate is the freeze subset only: FrozenStat || STATUSEFFECT_Frozen is checked (matching WeaponFireDriver.WeaponLocked), but the broader QC weaponLocked() pieces (player_blocked, the LockWeapon mutator hook) are not modeled.
- `campcheck.prematch.reset` — Recorded divergence — The !warmup_stage && time>=game_starttime portion of the LIVE gate collapses to !GameStopped — a sim-wide convention shared by sibling PlayerPreThink mutators (TouchExplode, Bloodloss), since the headless sim's only match-live signal is GameStopped. The separate RESET block is now ported in full (all three OR terms). The round-handler / campaign-bot seams are mode-scoped: RoundGateBlocks() returns false outside round modes (provider null), exactly matching QC where round_handler_IsActive() is false there; campaignWait is false outside campaigns — so the reset is inert exactly where Base is inert. Constant live gate (!game_stopped && !warmup_stage && time >= game_starttime / !VehicleCommon.GameStopped only)
- `campcheck.lifecycle.misc` — Recorded divergence — The CopyBody clone-origin copy has no port analogue because the port's respawn architecture REUSES the live player edict (SpawnSystem.cs:585 comments this explicitly) rather than spawning a separate corpse clone — there is no clone entity to carry campcheck_prevorigin onto, and PlayerSpawn re-inits campcheck_prevorigin/nextcheck/traveled anyway, so behavior matches. The PlayerDies centerprint-clear and BuildMutatorsString advertise ARE both ported and live. CopyBody clone's campcheck_prevorigin not copied — but architecturally moot in the port (no corpse clone; the edict is reused and PlayerSpawn re-inits the fields). Intended divergence, not a behavior gap. Constant CopyBody clone prevorigin (clone.campcheck_prevorigin = player.campcheck_prevorigin / no CopyBody hook in the port; the live edict is REUSED on respawn (no corpse clone), and PlayerSpawn re-inits the fields)
- `mutator-cloaked.alpha.default_alpha_consumers` — STILL OPEN (secondary) — no exterior/held weapon-entity alpha-follow: Base weaponsystem.qc:116-117,170-171 makes the held weapon model track default_weapon_alpha when the owner is at the default player alpha; the port has no such consumer. Minor vs the now-working player body alpha. STILL OPEN (secondary) — no vehicle-exit alpha restore (Base sv_vehicles.qc:814 resets player.alpha = default_player_alpha when leaving a vehicle). Constant spawn: exteriorweapon.alpha = default_weapon_alpha (client.qc:790 / none (no exterior-weapon entity alpha-follow)) Constant weapon entity follows owner alpha (weaponsystem.qc:116-117,170-171 / none) Constant vehicle-exit restores player.alpha = default_player_alpha (sv_vehicles.qc:814 / none)
- `mutator-cloaked.report.mutators_pretty_string` — STILL OPEN (minor) — no MUT_CLOAKED=7 bit (util.qh:62) / util.qc:302 bitfield mapping ported. Cosmetic mutator-bitfield only; the live pretty-string path does not consume it. Constant MUT_CLOAKED bit (7 (util.qh:62) / none)
- `mutator-cloaked.menu.metadata` — MINOR (intended infra divergence) — there is no dedicated in-game per-mutator describe-PAGE widget; the QC METHOD(MutatorCloaked, describe) text rides on the create-game checkbox tooltip instead (the port-wide pattern). The actual describe wording is now carried verbatim, so the content gap is closed; only the page-vs-tooltip presentation surface differs.
- `damagetext.sv.first_hit_bitset` — Constant bitset width (11 ints x 24 bits = 255 clients / HashSet<Entity> (unbounded))
- `damagetext.net.wire_and_visibility_tiers` — Recorded divergence — The port collapses the CSQC Net_LinkEntity temp-entity replication into an in-process host-local queue (DrainPending), consumed only on the host path (NetGame.cs 'if (_server is not null)'). Matches the port's general 'host drives the local HUD directly' model; the local attacker (sv_damagetext 2) is the common single-player/listen-host case. Documented in the DamagetextMutator header (lines 70-72). A pure remote client (no local server) gets no damage text at all (the drain is guarded by 'if (_server is not null)' at NetGame.cs). The tier-1 'spectator following the attacker' arm is dormant: the host has no follow-spectate, so ShouldShowTo's viewerSpectatesAttacker is never passed true from NetGame (free-fly observer arm IS live). Constant PRECISION_MULTIPLIER fixed-point wire (Short/Int24 by BIG_* flags) (128 + WriteShort/WriteInt24_t; armor omitted on NO_ARMOR, potential on NO_POTENTIAL / no net packet; un-multiplied amounts passed in DamageTextEvent, x128 + NO_*/BIG_* reconstruction in DamageTextLayer.Add)
- `damagetext.cl.placement_2d_3d` — QC tests entcs.has_origin for can_use_3d; the port always has a worldPos so can_use_3d is effectively always true (same as the common case). Edge: QC's 'return nothing' branch (no entcs origin AND 2D-disabled-or-observer) cannot occur in the port — a number always renders as 3D world or 2D screen. Minor; matches Base for all reachable states. view_origin: QC uses the eye position; the port uses Camera.GlobalPosition (the host camera node). Close-range distance is computed in metres (QU*0.0254) which is dimensionally faithful but the camera origin vs QC eye origin may differ by a small view offset.
- `mutator-dodging.input.double_tap` — Minor: the pressed-key refresh (DodgingMutator.cs:214-221) seeds Forward/Backward/Right/Left/Jump/Crouch but omits the KEY_ATCK/KEY_ATCK2 bits Base sets in PM_dodging_GetPressedKeys (sv_dodging.qc:291-292). Those bits are not consulted by the detector's four direction branches, so behavior is unaffected, but the pressedkeys field is not a complete mirror of Base.
- `mutator-dodging.input.delay_state_gate` — The `if (cvar != 0) field = cvar` guards mean explicitly setting one of the *_threshold/delay cvars to 0 is ignored and the hardcoded fallback is used instead. Harmless with the shipped cfg (no stock value is 0). NOTE: MaxSpeed/AirMaxSpeed are read WITHOUT the !=0 guard (DodgingMutator.cs:117-118), so a 0 there is honoured correctly (= no cap, matching Base).
- `dynamic_handicap.compute.update_handicap` — Apply pass: Base sets a handicap on EVERY connected client (FOREACH_CLIENT(true), incl. observers); port iterates players only. Harmless functionally (observers take no damage) but a behavioral diff if an observer's stored handicap is ever read. Score representation: Base scores are float-typed (PlayerScore_Get), port GameScores.Get returns int. Equivalent in normal play (score deltas are integers), but a fractional Base score would differ. Constant mean_pool (FOREACH_CLIENT(IS_PLAYER) for mean; FOREACH_CLIENT(true) for apply / FindByClass(player)+Client flag, mean excludes IsObserver, apply iterates all clients) Constant score_type (PlayerScore_Get -> float (stored in float field) / GameScores.Get -> int)
- `dynamic_handicap.apply.damage_scaling` — Base short-circuits Handicap_GetTotalHandicap entirely under HANDICAP_DISABLED() (returns 1); the port checks CTS/RACE inside VoluntaryHandicapProvider (returns 1) and relies on the mutator not activating in CTS/RACE so the forced fields stay 1. Equivalent in practice, but the FORCED part of HandicapTotal (DamageSystem.cs:899-906) has no explicit HANDICAP_DISABLED gate — if a forced handicap were ever set in a CTS/RACE match by some other path, the port would still apply it. Latent, not reachable today (Handicap.SetForcedHandicap itself early-returns when disabled).
- `dynamic_handicap.apply.invalid_value_guard` — Redundant tolerance on the READ path: Handicap.GetForcedHandicap (Handicap.cs:61) coerces a stored forced<=0 to 1 rather than faulting. The authoritative WRITE path (Handicap.SetForcedHandicap, Handicap.cs:72-73) matches Base's error() via Log.Fatal, so this read-path softness is latent only (the +1 / 1/(|h|+1) fold keeps written values strictly positive, so neither the guard nor the fallback is hit in normal play).
- `mutator-hook.enable.predicate` — Theoretical-only ordering divergence: the port's ExprToken tests >= <= == != === !== then > <, whereas Base's BINOP macro order is >= <= > < == != === !==. strstrofs/IndexOf both find the FIRST occurrence, so for every realistic token (a bare var, a single comparison like 'var>=1', the instahook literal '1') the two orders return identical results. They diverge only for a contrived token containing BOTH a > or < AND an == (e.g. 'a>b==c'), which never occurs in cvar expressions. Not a real gap; flagged for completeness.
- `mutator-hook.onadd.free_reel` — Recorded divergence — The port has no per-weapon ammo_factor; instead OnPlayerPreThink (line 107-108) tops the player's fuel up to Primary.Ammo+1 before each WrThink so the hooked-fuel TakeResource (Hook.cs) is a no-op. Functionally equivalent for the offhand path; the ONROLLBACK_OR_REMOVE ammo_factor=1 restore is moot because there is no ammo_factor to restore (Unhook just unsubscribes the handlers). Equivalence holds only because the offhand path is gated on NOT carrying the real WEP_HOOK as main-hand; if the same Hook were main-hand the top-up would also free its primary fire (an edge case that does not arise here). Cosmetic-equivalence divergence, not a behavioral gap. Constant WEP_HOOK.ammo_factor (1 -> 0 on add, restored to 1 on rollback/remove / no ammo_factor; fuel topped to Primary.Ammo+1 each think)
- `mutator-hook.client.gameplay_tip` — Recorded divergence — Base cl_hook builds this tip CSQC-side into the connecting player's welcome dialog. The port has no CSQC layer, so it surfaces the identical text per-player via the chat sink (Commands.ChatToPlayer -> ServerNet.SendChatToPlayer reliable svc_print) on the client-connect path — same audience (the one joining client, NOT a broadcast) and same text/keybind, different transport. Bots are skipped (no HUD).
- `mutator-hook.precedence.blaster_override` — The precedence is now observable on the live path (the offhand mechanism it arbitrates is no longer input-dead). Both PlayerSpawn handlers run; the registry NetName sort makes blaster the LAST OffhandWeapon writer ('grappling_hook' < 'offhand_blaster' ordinally), so blaster wins when both are on — matching Base 'overridden by offhand_blaster'. No remaining gap.
- `mutator-instagib.enable.predicate` — ONADD item-flag unblocking of VaporizerCells/Invisibility/Speed via the g_instagib_items IntrusiveList is not literally ported; the port instead leaves those items un-MutatorBlocked and gates them per-match through FilterItemDefinition (InstagibItems.cs documents this as a deliberate equivalent).
- `mutator-instagib.loadout.start_items` — No explicit warmup_start_* twins in StartLoadout; relies on the single value mirroring QC warmup (equal in Base, so behaviorally faithful). Port reads g_instagib_ammo_start but keeps default 10 when the cvar reads 0 (`if (start != 0f)`); Base would set cells to 0. Edge case only (default is 10).
- `mutator-instagib.items.filter` — The port splits QC's single FilterItem hook into FilterItemDefinition (pure deletes, pre-spawn) + FilterItem (entity-level replacements). Deliberate port mechanics, not a behavior gap — both seams are live and together reproduce the QC switch.
- `mutator-instagib.items.touch` — Port omits Base's `if(MUTATOR_RETURNVALUE) return false;` early guard (sv_instagib.qc:381) — minor; only matters if a prior subscriber already claimed the pickup.
- `mutator-instagib.items.defs` — VaporizerCells botvalue 2000 is documented but the port AmmoPickup has no bot-value field (recorded as a comment in InstagibItems.cs). ExtraLife waypoint blink-2 noted but the port has no waypoint blink system. Both are bot-nav flavor, not gameplay.
- `mutator-instagib.lifecycle.stop_countdown` — No dedicated MatchEnd hook chain in the port; the QC MatchEnd FOREACH_CLIENT(instagib_stop_countdown) is approximated by the per-tick game_stopped stop condition in AmmoCheck (reads real VehicleCommon.GameStopped), which stops the countdown within ~1 tick of the match freezing. Behaviorally equivalent for the visible centerprint, but not a literal match-end FOREACH_CLIENT sweep.
- `mutator-invincibleproj.client.active_mut_flag` — Constant MUT_INVINCIBLE_PROJECTILES (5 / none)
- `mutator-itemstime.producer.times_table` — Recorded divergence — Base updates it_times event-driven (SetTime/UpdateTime called at each item pickup / respawn-schedule in items.qc). The port instead re-scans all live world items every server frame (OnStartFrame -> Recompute) reading each item's ScheduledRespawnTime. The published value is identical (min cooldown across copies); only the trigger differs. Documented in ItemstimeMutator.cs comments.
- `mutator-itemstime.net.csqc_sync` — Recorded divergence — Base writes one IT_Write per (item-id, float) keyed by the CSQC item registry id; the port batches the whole table into a single reliable NetControl.ItemsTime message keyed by the HUD panel's item NAMES (plus a leading STAT(ITEMSTIME) tier byte), and dedups per-peer (resent only when that peer's resolved view changes) instead of Base's STATIC_INIT-seeded + per-event IT_Write stream. The delivered per-peer table content is equivalent.
- `mutator-itemstime.producer.per_player_tiers` — Recorded divergence — Base implements the per-player send tiers via discrete sync/reset hooks (MakePlayerObserver / PlayerSpawn / ClientConnect / reset_map_global call SetTimesForPlayer / ResetTimesForPlayer / ResetTimes). The port folds all of these into ServerNet.SendItemsTime: it evaluates the SAME send gate (warmup || IsObserver || sv_itemstime==2) for each peer every tick and resends (with per-peer dedup) only when that peer's resolved view flips — so the observable consequence of join/observe/spawn/reset (a gated-out live player gets the reset/-1 table, an observer/warmup player gets the full table) is reproduced without the named hooks. !IS_PLAYER is realized as Player.IsObserver. The discrete Base hooks (MakePlayerObserver / PlayerSpawn / ClientConnect / reset_map_global) are not present as named mutator-hook handlers; their effect is reproduced by the per-tick per-peer send gate + dedup in ServerNet.SendItemsTime. A gated-out live player receives the reset (-1) table via the dedup-driven resend rather than an explicit ResetTimesForPlayer call — equivalent in delivered state but not a 1:1 hook port.
- `mutator-itemstime.net.itemstime_stat` — Recorded divergence — Base registers STAT(ITEMSTIME) as a generic networked player stat (the stat system streams autocvar_sv_itemstime). The port has no generic stat registry; instead the tier is sourced on the server from ItemstimeMutator.Tier (= sv_itemstime) and carried as the leading byte of the dedicated NetControl.ItemsTime message, decoded into ClientNet.ItemsTimeTier. Same value, same liveness, carried on the itemstime channel rather than a general STAT block.
- `mutator-itemstime.hud.draw` — hud_panel_itemstime_hidebig (Base default 0/false) is not registered/consumed in the port: there IS an orphan menu checkbox for it (DialogHudPanelItemsTime.cs:80) but the cvar is neither registered in ItemsTimePanel.RegisterDefaults nor read by DrawPanel, so toggling it has no effect (the catalog always shows Big Health/Armor). Matches the stock default false, but a user setting hidebig 1 is silently dead. PRE-EXISTING; unchanged at this drift check (grep hidebig across src+game -> only the dialog checkbox + comment). The _hud_configure HUD-editor preview (Base Item_ItemsTime_GetTime returns fake times: ArmorMega+8, ArmorBig+0, HealthMega+0, Strength+4) is not ported; the port panel has no hud-configure preview path, so the items-time panel shows nothing in the HUD editor. PRE-EXISTING; unchanged at this drift check.
- `mutator-itemstime.hud.feed_host` — Recorded divergence — Two feed paths: the listen host pushes ItemstimeMutator.CurrentTimes straight into ItemsTimePanel (no serialisation round-trip), while a pure remote client receives the per-peer networked table (ClientNet.ItemTimes from NetControl.ItemsTime) — the C# analogue of IT_Write per real client. Same table content via two transports; both gated by ShouldDraw so the per-player send semantics hold.
- `mutator-itemstime.waypoints.spectator_sprites` — Recorded divergence — Base gates the SPRITERULE_SPECTATOR respawn waypoint CLIENT-side via autocvar_g_waypointsprite_itemstime (1=spectating, 2=+warmup/sv_itemstime==2; default 2) in Draw_WaypointSprite. The port enforces the same spectator/warmup/sv_itemstime==2 audience SERVER-side in ServerNet.WaypointVisible (the SpriteRule.Spectator case) and does NOT read the client g_waypointsprite_itemstime cvar (which is present in xonotic-client.cfg but dead) — so a client cannot override the gate. The delivered visibility (only spectators in a live round, everyone in warmup / sv_itemstime==2) matches; only the gate location and the client override differ. Also: QC plays SND(ITEMRESPAWNCOUNTDOWN) per visible client via soundto(MSG_ONE); the port emits a single positional CH_TRIGGER cue on the item (ATTEN_NORM falloff) since the Common layer has no per-client soundto seam — slight over-broadcast to nearby live players. The client-side g_waypointsprite_itemstime cvar (xonotic-client.cfg:518, default 2) is present but UNREAD — a client overriding it (e.g. to 1 or 0) has no effect; the spectator/warmup audience is enforced server-side in ServerNet.WaypointVisible instead. Documented in src/VortexArena.Server/Cvars.cs:578-584. The per-second ITEMRESPAWNCOUNTDOWN countdown cue is broadcast positionally on the item (CH_TRIGGER, ATTEN_NORM) rather than soundto(MSG_ONE) per waypoint-visible client, so it can leak to nearby live players for a SpectatorOnly item (no per-client soundto seam in the Common layer). Constant g_waypointsprite_itemstime_default (2 (xonotic-client.cfg:518) / cvar present in Base/data/.../xonotic-client.cfg:518 but UNREAD (no client draw-side gate); the spectator visibility is enforced server-side by ServerNet.WaypointVisible instead)
- `mutator-kick_teamkiller.action.punish` — Constant g_kick_teamkiller_severity (0 (unset->0; default=play-ban) / read into Severity; default branch implemented, sev1/2 unenforced) Constant g_kick_teamkiller_bantime (0 (unset->0) / read into BanTime but unused (KickBanClient never called)) Constant g_ban_default_masksize (3 (xonotic-server.cfg:425) / 3 (Cvars.cs:337) but not consumed (no IP-ban path))
- `melee_only.enable.predicate` — Arena exclusion is checked against the g_weaponarena CVAR (==0f) rather than Base's MapInfo_LoadedGametype.m_weaponarena (the LOADED-gametype arena flag). Equivalent for the common case (arena driven by the cvar), but a gametype that sets m_weaponarena without the cvar (or vice-versa) would diverge. Minor; the instagib/overkill exclusions and the string expr_evaluate read are faithful. Constant exclude weaponarena (!MapInfo_LoadedGametype.m_weaponarena / g_weaponarena cvar == 0f)
- `melee_only.loadout.warmup` — The port's warmup spawn path (SpawnSystem.ApplyWarmupLoadout) does NOT call SetStartItems — it runs the SetWeaponArena seam (forced 'off' by melee_only -> no arena, SetWeaponArena.Call at SpawnSystem.cs:1101) then falls into the g_warmup_allguns branch (ALL non-hidden weapons, default true) + 30 shells, so the melee_only SetStartItems hook is bypassed during warmup. Faithful end-state is salvaged ONLY because the port-only OnPlayerSpawn (PlayerSpawn hook, fired AFTER PutPlayerInServer on every spawn incl. warmup) re-clears to shotgun + 0 shells. Remove OnPlayerSpawn and warmup melee_only would break.
- `melee_only.loadout.player_spawn_reapply` — Recorded divergence — Port-only PlayerSpawn handler with no Base counterpart. Base enforces the loadout purely through the start_*/warmup_start_* globals computed once at config; the port's SetStartItems seam covers only the non-warmup spawn path, so this per-spawn re-application is the mechanism that (a) enforces melee_only during WARMUP (where the warmup loadout bypasses SetStartItems and would otherwise grant all-guns+shells) and (b) guarantees the shotgun-only set on every respawn. Same observable loadout as Base, achieved by a port-specific code path.
- `melee_only.arena.force_off` — PARTIAL. The SetWeaponArena hook is LIVE on the LOADOUT path: SpawnSystem.ComputeStartItems (SetWeaponArena.Call at SpawnSystem.cs:877) and ApplyWarmupLoadout (SpawnSystem.cs:1101) both call SetWeaponArena.Call, so melee_only's 'off' correctly prevents an arena weapon loadout from replacing the shotgun start set. HOWEVER the item-suppression gate still reads the RAW cvar, not the hook result: StartItem.cs:344 (HavePickupItem) and GiveItems.cs:161 test g_weaponarena (GetFloat != 0f) directly. So melee_only forcing the resolved arena to 'off' does NOT clear that gate — with melee_only + a configured g_weaponarena, the port still wrongly suppresses every medium/large health/armor and ammo world-pickup that Base keeps (Base's 'off' would re-enable them). Real bug, edge config (host must set both g_melee_only and g_weaponarena).
- `mutator-midair.enable.predicate` — Port tests `GetFloat("g_midair") != 0` instead of QC `expr_evaluate` (string expr). Equivalent for the stock 0/1 cvar; non-numeric expressions would differ, but that is not a stock config.
- `mutator-multijump.counter.networking` — Constant count sync (multijump_count is a networked stat; CSQC copies the server value into the local field every PlayerPhysics tick before the reset / MultijumpCount is a plain entity field; no explicit stat-replication or CSQC-style copy)
- `mutator-nades.throw.input_wire` — No latency gap for humans (re-verified 2026-06-27, post Wave-17): OffhandFirePressed is now published BEFORE the PlayerPreThink hook in the SAME tick for human players — GameWorld.OnClientMove (GameWorld.cs:2225-2234) reads InputProvider(p).ButtonHook and sets p.OffhandFirePressed = hookPressed && Vehicle is null right before the offhand_think runs, so the +hook offhand reacts same-tick (matching QC W_WeaponFrame's same-frame dispatch). Bots keep the WeaponFireDriver PostThink publish (WeaponFireDriver.cs:69), a 1-tick stale read — but bots never bind +hook so the offhand/nade path is moot for them. The earlier '1-tick latency for all players' note is superseded. The QC PreThink (sv_nades.qc:733) calls offhand_think only when (offhand != OFFHAND_NADE || hasHook); the port calls OffhandThink when OffhandWeapon == "nade". This is the correct headless inversion: in QC, when the nade IS the offhand and no hook is held, the engine weapon-frame dispatches offhand_think itself (NOT the PreThink hook), whereas the headless port has no engine offhand dispatch so it drives it from PreThink. No double-drive (OffhandThink has exactly one caller). g_nades_override_dropweapon read in OnForbidThrowCurrentWeapon but the cvar is not in the default cvar table (defaults to 0 via GetFloat fallback — faithful at defaults).
- `mutator-nades.prime.select` — g_nades_client_select / g_nades_bonus_client_select not honoured: the port always uses the server g_nades_nade_type / g_nades_bonus_type, never per-client cl_nade_type/cl_pokenade_type (the headless sim has no per-client cvar plumbed). PlayerSpawn's bonus_client_select bonus-type set is also omitted.
- `mutator-nades.throw.charge_force` — Recorded divergence — DESIGN 2026-08-02: Vortex throws nades CENTERED on the crosshair. Base hardcodes a rightward velocity bias in both throw paths — v_right * 0.05 on weapon_drop (3.81 deg off-axis) and v_right * 0.1 on offhand (8.13 deg) — which, paired with the leftward g_nades_throw_offset (see mutator-nades.toss.projectile), means a Xonotic nade crosses the aim line exactly once and never travels along it. Origin: Mario, 2013-06-05 (66318bb5b "Begin making nades a separate mutator"); carried verbatim ever since, through k9er's 2025 file split (34e6c26b9) and otta8634's 2025 reformat (f748474fed). No upstream commit ever states a rationale for the right component. The weights are now CVARS rather than literals, as (forward, right, up) in the view basis, defaulted by the D8 config layer (data/core.pk3dir/vortex-server.cfg — the upstream mutators.cfg stays pristine), so the Base feel is one config line away: g_nades_throw_dir "0.75 0.05 0.2"; g_nades_throw_dir_offhand "0.7 0.1 0.2". The C# fallbacks in NadeThrow (used when no cfg tree is mounted) match the layer's values. Zeroing the right term barely touches range — the weight vector's length (which scales the throw force) drops 0.2% on weapon_drop and 0.9% on offhand; the minforce/maxforce ramp itself is untouched and still matches Base exactly.
 Constant dir_weights_weapondrop (fwd0.75 up0.2 right0.05 (hardcoded sv_nades.qc:629) / g_nades_throw_dir "0.75 0 0.2" (vortex-server.cfg)) Constant dir_weights_offhand (fwd0.7 up0.2 right0.1 (hardcoded sv_nades.qc:678) / g_nades_throw_dir_offhand "0.7 0 0.2" (vortex-server.cfg))
- `mutator-nades.toss.projectile` — Recorded divergence — DESIGN 2026-08-02: g_nades_throw_offset now defaults to "0 0 0" (centered) instead of Base's "0 -25 0", which spawns the nade 25qu to the thrower's LEFT (v_right is genuinely rightward — AngleVectors gives (0,-1,0) at zero angles — so a negative y is leftward). Paired with the rightward velocity bias (see mutator-nades.throw.charge_force), Base's nade leaves left of the crosshair and flies right of it. Origin: Mario, 2013-06-15 (56cc3610e "Move thrown nades to the side a bit more", which raised a hardcoded v_right*-10 to v_right*-25); Mario 2015-03-17 (1e10293f6) then added the g_nades_throw_offset cvar at "0 -20 0" WITHOUT removing the hardcoded -25, so Base shipped a 45qu offset until Martin Taibr folded the two together in 2017-08-07 (2416db2a5 "cleanup nade offset"), landing on today's -25 with a TODO noting the value is a floor-edge-bounce compromise for OK promode. The cvar is unchanged in behavior — only its default moved, via the D8 config layer (data/core.pk3dir/vortex-server.cfg; the shipped mutators.cfg is left pristine and still says "0 -25 0") — so `g_nades_throw_offset "0 -25 0"` restores Base exactly. Note the port's spawn base has always been the EYE, not Base's w_shotorg, so the port never carried the gun-alignment lateral term (tracing.qc:73-77) on top.
 Newton-style 0 is implemented as pure absolute _velocity rather than W_CalculateProjectileVelocity(e, e.velocity, _velocity, true); for non-default g_projectiles_newton_style this diverges from Base's velocity blending (style 0 is the shipped default, so identical at defaults). Constant g_nades_throw_offset ("0 -25 0" (mutators.cfg:198) / "0 0 0" centered (vortex-server.cfg overrides the shipped mutators.cfg)) Constant newton_style (0 absolute / 1 relative / 2 velocity-only (mutators.cfg:213) / 0/1/2)
- `mutator-nades.touch.bounce_pickup_detonate` — ITEM_TOUCH_NEEDKILL (lava/void/nodraw content) delete branch is omitted (content-trace engine concern); a nade in a kill volume is not destroyed-to-normal-boom.
- `mutator-nades.damage.launch_destroy` — Blaster pure-force launch (damage=0): the DamageSystem only dispatches event_damage when damage>0 or damageforcescale!=0, and the nade keeps damageforcescale 0, so a 0-damage blaster hit may not reach NadeDamage — documented in NadeProjectile.cs header.
- `mutator-nades.boom.monster` — Gated by g_monsters (faithful — QC also requires g_monsters; nades_CheckTypes falls back to NORMAL when monsters off).
- `mutator-nades.lifecycle.cleanup` — DropSpecialItems (the dedicated special-item-drop toss hook, sv_nades.qc:906) has no port HookChain. The hook's common case — the death-toss — IS handled by the PlayerDies hook (OnPlayerDies tosses the held nade with '0 0 100'); the unported residual is the non-death special-item-drop toss ('0 0 0'), which has no live trigger in the port today. Minor.
- `mutator-nades.presentation.client` — Constant orb presentation (MDL_NADE_ORB skin 1, quadratic growth, square-root fade, accelerated spin / shield.md3 skin 1 with additive texture and lifetime estimated from first sight)
- `mutator-nades.registry.type_catalog` — m_icon is not carried in NadeDef — the per-type HUD icon mapping lives client-side (AmmoPanel DrawAmmoNades cell, see presentation.client row), so the catalog split is port-structural, not a behavior gap. Nade_FromProjectile (nades.qh:65, resolve a nade type from a CSQC projectile id) has no direct port analogue — the client classifies by the ServerNet string key instead (ProjectileCatalog.cs:337 ClassifyNade/HasNadeClass); equivalent surface, different key. The CSQC half of NADES_SMALL (cl_nades.qc:46 sets the client projectile hull 16/32 for prediction) is moot in the port: nade positions come server-authoritative over the entity stream (ProjectileCatalog nades use CollisionMode.None).
- `mutator-nades.presentation.projectile_trails_wire` — Constant normal-nade trail (EFFECT_NADE_TRAIL(team): red/blue/yellow/pink by thrower team, neutral otherwise / no trail (TrailEffect '')) Constant napalm trail (EFFECT_TR_ROCKET (burn: SPIDERBOT_ROCKET_TRAIL) / FIREBALL/FireSmall approximation) Constant spawn/heal/monster/entrap/veil/ammo/darkness trails (NADE_TRAIL_YELLOW/RED/RED/YELLOW/NEUTRAL/BROWN/PURPLE (+_BURN variants) / none (TrailEffect '' for all 7)) Constant burn-variant swap (nade_burn_spawn switches to m_projectile[1] when the nade is first shot/armed / wire key never changes (no burn token))
- `mutator-nades.presentation.explode_effect` — Constant explode effectinfo (nade_neutral_explode tinted expcol_min..expcol_max (normal boom tinted by nades_PlayerColor of the thrower) / EXPLOSION_MEDIUM/EXPLOSION_BIG/ICEORGLASS/HEALING/AMMO_REGEN tinted by NadeDef.Color (min==max; normal untinted white))
- `mutator-nades.select.client_type_replication` — Constant cl_nade_type (3 (xonotic-client.cfg; replicated to the server per client) / absent) Constant cl_pokenade_type ("zombie" (replicated) / absent)
- `mutator-nades.net.orb_wire` — Constant orb payload (type, origin, expiry, total lifetime, radius / type, origin, expiry, radius)
- `mutator-nades.bonus.cross_unit_awards` — Constant g_nades_bonus_score_high (60 (mutators.cfg:242; ctf capture / kh capture) / KeyHunt bare fallback 15 (KeyHunt.cs:1212))
- `mutator-new_toys.weapons.unblock` — Port omits the QC ONADD 'time > 1' runtime-add guard (error) and the ONREMOVE 'cannot be removed at runtime' refusal (return -1); the port's Hook/Unhook just clears/restores the flags. Cosmetic — the live boot path adds once before play. Constant ONADD time>1 runtime guard (error('cannot be added at runtime') / none (no time-guard))
- `mutator-new_toys.startitems.rearrange` — No start_weapons_defaultmask: the port rewrites the whole StartLoadout.Weapons set (l.Weapons.Clear() + union rebuild) rather than only the bits in the default mask. Unobservable with the stock blaster-only default; would diverge if a server set a partial start_weapons_defaultmask. Constant applies under start_weapons_defaultmask (newdefault &= start_weapons_defaultmask / operates on whole StartLoadout.Weapons (no defaultmask concept))
- `mutator-new_toys.mapreplace.set_weaponreplace` — QC's >=2-token replacement links the extra weapons as one --internalteam team-item group (the engine shows ONE of the set); the port has no team-item select group at this layer, so each extra token spawns its OWN world weapon (ItemSpawnFuncs.cs WeaponSpawn token loop). The faithful SET of weapons is present; the single-show grouping is the deviation (already noted in StartItem). weapon_defaultspawnfunc's WEP_FLAG_MUTATORBLOCKED map-spawn rejection is intentionally NOT enforced in WeaponSpawn (ItemSpawnFuncs.cs:310 NOTE) — a blocked weapon placed directly on a map still spawns. Deferred as a separate broad-impact parity item; does not affect the New-Toys replacement chain itself. Constant WEP_FLAG_MUTATORBLOCKED map-spawn rejection (yes (startitem_failed in weapon_defaultspawnfunc) / none (ItemSpawnFuncs.cs:310 NOTE — deferred as a broad-impact separate item))
- `mutator-new_toys.pickup.roflsound` — Off by default (g_new_toys_use_pickupsound 0) — the roflsound only plays when the cvar is set, matching Base. Port gates on item.Pickup.IsWeaponPickup + IsNewToy(item.NetName) (the weapon-pickup def carries its weapon as NetName) rather than QC's item.weapon int; equivalent selector.
- `mutator-new_toys.menu.create_game_controls` — The Base checkbox dependency is the full weapon-arena/instagib compatibility test; the port approximates it as a plain not-instagib dependency (DialogMutators.cs comment notes the approximation). Edge-case behavior under arena combos may differ slightly.
- `mutator-new_toys.menu.priority_list_suffix` — Recorded divergence — Port marks mutator-blocked weapons in the priority list with a ' *' suffix plus a '(* = mutator weapon)' header instead of the per-row '(Mutator weapon)' text. Same meaning (driven by WEP_FLAG_MUTATORBLOCKED), compacter presentation choice for the list widget. Constant suffix on mutator-blocked weapons ('(Mutator weapon)' / ' *' (header notes '(* = mutator weapon)'))
- `mutator-nix.enable.gate` — Recorded divergence — QC's expr also requires !MUTATOR_IS_ENABLED(mutator_instagib) && !MUTATOR_IS_ENABLED(ok) && !MapInfo_LoadedGametype.m_weaponarena. The port has no cross-mutator MUTATOR_IS_ENABLED helper, so the exclusion is expressed against the underlying cvars g_instagib/g_overkill/g_weaponarena (NixMutator.cs:48-50) — the same stand-in NewToys/Vampire/instagib/melee_only use. Behaviorally equivalent: a direct `g_nix 1` can no longer double-activate alongside instagib/overkill/weapon-arena.
- `mutator-nix.choose.canchoose` — Omits the `g_weaponarena` sub-branch (keep only g_weaponarena_weapons). Moot because NIX cannot coexist with a weapon arena (enable-gate now excludes g_weaponarena). Note: because Normal-flag is required, superweapons (Fireball etc., which lack WEP_FLAG_NORMAL) never enter the NIX pool — see mutator-nix.spawn.loadout.
- `mutator-nix.filter.items` — Recorded divergence — Keys off classname tags (item_health*/item_armor*/item_strength|shield|invincible) instead of the registry instanceOf{Health,Armor,Powerup} flags, until the item-class registry exposes those flags. Behaviorally equivalent for stock items.
- `offhand_blaster.fire.offhand_think` — Residual (minor): the ForbidWeaponUse mutator-hook half of Base weaponUseForbidden (weaponsystem.qc:430) is not modeled — WeaponUseForbidden only covers the round-grace half. No port mutator currently forbids weapon use, so this is a non-issue in practice. The round-grace half (Base weaponsystem.qc:428: round_handler_IsActive() && !round_handler_IsRoundStarted()) IS now enforced live (see verification).
- `offhand_blaster.tips.gameplay_hint` — Surface differs intentionally: Base builds this client-side (CSQC BuildGameplayTipsString -> welcome dialog); the port has no CSQC layer, so it emits the tip as a per-player chat line (Commands.ChatToPlayer) to the connecting non-bot only — same audience/scope as Base (not a broadcast). The mut_is_active(MUT_OFFHAND_BLASTER) bitfield isn't networked, but the server gates on the mutator's own Added state, which is equivalent.
- `offhand_blaster.menu.describe` — Minor: the entry-list label is Humanize(NetName)='Offhand Blaster' rather than Base's localized `message` ATTRIB (the port has no per-mutator localized display-name field). Cosmetic; text is identical for English.
- `overkill.weapons.unblock` — Constant g_overkill_weapons (0 / none)
- `overkill.blaster.nullify_damage_force` — Recorded divergence — Port checks IsPlayer(target) only and drops Base's explicit (IS_VEHICLE || IS_TURRET) target branch; vehicles/turrets route their own damage and are largely unported, so the player subset is the faithful core. The force-zero keeps the self/frozen/dead guard, the damage-zero is unconditional under keepdamage=0 — both matching Base.
- `overkill.loot.drop_player` — Minor: the Base "random" keyword (drop any allowed normal item) is stubbed to fall back to armor_small (the port's item registry has no filtered normal-item group). The "" (disabled) sentinel IS handled (RandomFromList returns null on empty). Default loot is armor_small, so this only affects servers that set g_overkill_loot_player "random".
- `overkill.loot.drop_monster` — Minor: same "random" keyword fallback stub as the player-loot row (RandomFromList resolves "random"→armor_small); default g_overkill_loot_monster is armor_small so only affects servers that set it to "random". Monster types themselves are only partially ported, but the hook chain + handler are now present and faithful.
- `overkill.countdown.blaster` — Minor: Base iterates all weapon slots and runs each slot's m_weapon.wr_think(...,2); the port runs the secondary WrThink for slot 0's current weapon only (the port's inventory carries a single active weapon on slot 0). Functionally equivalent for OK loadouts. The QC weaponLocked() is approximated by the reachable freeze subset (FrozenStat || STATUSEFFECT_Frozen), same stand-in as WeaponFireDriver/CampcheckMutator.
- `overkill.filter.items_and_powerup_replace` — Minor: Base copies the original item's full field set (Item_CopyFields) onto the replacement and sets ok_item + lifetime=-1; the port spawns a fresh weapon pickup at the same Origin via StartItem.Spawn with PickupAnyway + the superweapon respawn time. The replacement weapon is allowed back through FilterItem via the classname fall-through (it is not item_strength/shield) rather than via an ok_item flag — functionally equivalent, no recursion. ok_item flag itself is unmodeled (ItemIsLoot stands in for the loot case).
- `overkill.itemwaypoints.respawn` — Minor: Base matches on item.itemdef (ITEM_HealthMega/ArmorMedium/ArmorBig/ArmorMega); the port matches on the live entity's ClassName (item_health_mega/armor_medium/armor_big/armor_mega) — same stand-in the FilterItem hook uses since the port's item registry isn't fully type-mapped. End set is identical.
- `overkill.naming.mod_strings` — Minor: the client-side cl_overkill cvar_settemp(g_overkill,1) sync (cl_overkill.qc REGISTER_MUTATOR ONADD) is still absent — there is no client mutator-registration seam to settemp g_overkill on the joining client. Server-side reporting (machine token, pretty string, modname) is now fully live; this is the only remaining hole and is client-cosmetic.
- `overkill.okhmg.nade_selfdamage_scale` — Recorded divergence — Base registers this as a SEPARATE always-on mutator (okhmg_nadesupport, predicate `true`) rather than gating it on the ok mutator; the port folds the handler into OverkillMutator, so it is only active while Overkill is enabled. The OK-HMG superweapon can only be obtained/used under Overkill in practice, so the effective behavior matches; the difference is the registration vehicle, not the rule (guard on weapon==okhmg + 10%-max-health damage scaling is bit-faithful).
- `overkill.oknex.charge_velocity` — Recorded divergence — Base registers oknex_charge as a SEPARATE always-on mutator (oknex.qc:32, predicate true) whose GetPressedKeys hook adds velocity-charge while m_weapon == WEP_OVERKILL_NEX; the port folds the same per-tick accumulation inline into OkNex.WrThink (the fire driver only calls WrThink for the held weapon, so the per-tick condition is identical — the same stand-in Vortex.cs uses for vortex_charge). The chargepool / wr_glow remain unported but are charge-gated and inert at the shipped balance. Minor: the chargepool drain and wr_glow visual on the charge path remain unported; both are charge-gated (g_balance_oknex_charge default 0) so inert at the shipped balance. The velocity-charge accumulation itself is now present and bit-faithful.
- `mutator-physical_items.registration.ode_gate` — Recorded divergence — HasPhysicsEngine() unconditionally returns false (after emitting the exact QC revert Log.Trace), so the mutator self-disables. This reproduces QC's own MUTATOR_ONADD revert path when DP_PHYSICS_ODE is unavailable — the port has no ODE rigid-body integrator wired to gameplay entities, so the only faithful behavior on a non-ODE engine is to revert to classic items, which is what stock DarkPlaces also does by default. Constant physics_ode (engine cvar, required for activation) (0 (off; needs DP_PHYSICS_ODE) / no autocvar_physics_ode / no ODE engine)
- `mutator-physical_items.item_spawn.ghost_entity` — Constant real item hide (EF_NODRAW + MOVETYPE_FOLLOW + aiment=ghost + setSendEntity func_null / EffectFlags.NoDraw + MoveType.Follow + Aiment=wep (PhysicalItemsMutator.cs:141-145); setSendEntity has no port equivalent (visibility via EF_NODRAW only)) Constant settle (DropToFloor_QC_DelayedInit when !cnt (map item) / no explicit DropToFloor; relies on TOSS/Physics integrator settling next step (PhysicalItemsMutator.cs:132-134 comment))
- `mutator-physical_items.item_callbacks.think_touch_damage` — Constant colormap/colormod/glowmod copy (copy owner.colormap/colormod/glowmod for map items / not ported (presentation fields; PhysicalItemsMutator.cs:172 comment))
- `mutator-pinata.death.offhand_slots` — Recorded divergence — The port models a single weapon slot (no dual-wielding in normal play; MaxWeaponSlots=2 exists but is not driven for the main game), so QC's slot>0 offhand loop has no extra weapons to scatter. g_pinata_offhand is read in Hook() for cvar parity but has no behavioral effect.
