Not player-observable in stock play; should be revisited only if/when dual-wielding is implemented. With g_pinata_offhand 1 and a dual-wield loadout, Base would also scatter the off-hand slot's non-held weapons; the port has no second slot to iterate (OnPlayerDies hardcodes WeaponSlot(0)), so this branch never does anything. Inert in stock play (no dual-wielding). Constant slots scanned (0..MAX_WEAPONSLOTS-1 (=2), break after slot 0 unless offhand / single WeaponSlot(0) only)
- `mutator-pinata.menu.metadata` — Minor: the port has no dedicated describe-page widget, so the describe text rides on the checkbox tooltip rather than a PAGE_TEXT panel. The incompatibility binding is approximated as the dominant 'not InstaGib' gate (Dependent.Bind(pinata, "g_instagib", 0, 0)) rather than QC checkCompatibility_pinata's full set (instagib/nix/overkill/melee_only/weapon-arena).
- `powerups.strength.fire_sound` — Per-player anti-spam stamps live in Dictionary<Entity,float> _prevStrengthSound/_prevStrengthSoundAttempt rather than entity fields; functionally equivalent but the maps are only cleared on Unhook (no per-entity eviction on disconnect — minor leak, not a behavior gap).
- `powerups.countdown_beep` — Base gates play_countdown on IS_REAL_CLIENT (real humans); the port gates the PreThink on EntFlags.Client (incl. bots), so a bot near expiry triggers the server-side beep too. Negligible (sound to a bot is harmless).
- `powerups.drop.ondeath_onuse` — Frozen drop uses the freezeTimer closure flag as the timer-frozen marker rather than Base's RES_ARMOR=1 marker on the loose item (logically equivalent: the think skips the bar update for a frozen drop, matching QC's !GetResource(RES_ARMOR) gate). The dropped-powerup waypoint renders the generic 'Item' def (magenta) — it has no wp_extra-driven per-powerup icon (QC sets wp.wp_extra = itemdef.m_id). Minor cosmetic-only divergence.
- `powerups.hud.timer_bars` — Strength/Shield m_active CSQC override (force-show in _hud_configure) is the editor-only path; not separately verified.
- `random_items.engine.type_weighted_pick` — On a prefix where NO type has a positive probability cvar the port breaks out via the chosenType==None guard (:340) and returns empty; Base logs a per-cvar warning and returns empty too — same end result, the warnings are diagnostics only (not flagged).
- `random_items.engine.item_prob_by_property` — Item_IsDefinitionAllowed is approximated by def.ItemDef.IsAllowed, which only checks the MutatorBlocked spawnflag (not the broader QC allow-list / gametype gates). Adequate for the stock pools but a latent narrow divergence under mutator-restricted item sets.
- `mutator-rocketminsta.damage.zero_laser_self_round` — Recorded divergence — Round-not-started branch: a mutator can't reach the gametype-owned round handler, so the port approximates round_handler_IsActive()&&!IsRoundStarted() with game_stopped (GameStopped) — a faithful superset for the not-yet-live-round case. The self-damage (attacker==target) branch is exact. Documented in the class comment. Round gate approximated by game_stopped (intended divergence); may diverge from the exact round-handler edge in round-based modes mid-round. Constant round condition (round_handler_IsActive() && !round_handler_IsRoundStarted() / VehicleCommon.GameStopped)
- `mutator-rocketminsta.secondary.laser_barrage` — Forced-reload-for-laser branch (wr_think:290-303: when reload_ammo && WEP_CVAR_PRI(WEP_BLASTER,ammo) && clip_load < min(...), force wr_reload + reset hagar_load) is not modeled — dormant at defaults (Vaporizer reload is not on the instagib path; no clip_load/reload_ammo handling in the port).
- `mutator-rocketminsta.laser.projectile_render` — Per-team effectinfo variants (red/blue/yellow/pink) are not registered — only the neutral trail (rocketminsta_laser_neutral) exists; Base also only registers neutral live, so the team tint is carried by the colormod (faithful).
- `running_guns.alpha.set_default_alpha` — Map-restart re-fire: Base SetDefaultAlpha() runs at worldspawn AND map restart (world.qc:954,1730); the port fires FireSetDefaultAlpha once at worldspawn (GameWorld.cs:618). Inert because the seed is recomputed deterministically per map (re)load from the same mutator set + cvars.
- `mutator-sandbox.core.registration` — Fully wired: NetGame.WireSandbox (live setup path, NetGame.cs:793) assigns Store (file IO under ~/XonData/sandbox/storage_*.txt), so MUTATOR_ONADD autoload genuinely reads the per-map storage file at activation. Base REGISTER_MUTATOR(sandbox) has NO instagib/arena/ok exclusion (unlike NIX) — port IsEnabled = g_sandbox != 0 is faithful.
- `mutator-sandbox.object.touch_material_fx` — WIRED + LIVE: ObjectSpawn assigns e.Edict.Touch = (self,other)=>ObjectTouch(e, other.Velocity) (SandboxMutator.cs:204), the port equivalent of Base settouch(e, sandbox_ObjectFunction_Touch) (sv_sandbox.qc:180). The object carries a real MOVETYPE_TOSS edict; the engine physics loop dual-dispatches e1.Touch on collision, so the velocity-scaled impact SOUND fires on the live match path. Touch averaging (object+toucher vel), >=min gate, offset, *factor clamp(0,1), 0.1s rate-limit all bit-faithful. presentation: FIXED (was missing) — the four impact effects are now REGISTERED in EffectsList.cs:303-306 (Effects.Register("IMPACT_METAL","impact_metal") + stone/wood/flesh, point effects). ObjectTouch's EffectEmitter.EmitByEffectInfoName("impact_<mat>") (SandboxMutator.cs:410) now resolves a real Effect via Effect.ByEffectInfoName (matches NetName ordinal, Effect.cs:87-93) → Emit(effect,…) with a non-null Effect (EffectEmitter.cs:240-242) → networked by ServerNet.WriteEffect (no longer the null-Effect drop). Count = ceil(intensity*10) ∈ 1..10 (>0, survives Emit's point-effect count==0 guard, EffectEmitter.cs:241). Base effectinfo.txt has impact blocks for exactly metal/stone/wood/flesh (effectinfo.txt:5529+); an unknown material renders nothing on both ends, matching Base's __pointparticles(-1) fallthrough. CAVEAT: this is CODE-verified (registered → networks by id, no longer dropped); the in-game particle render was NOT visually re-confirmed this pass (no kind:visual) — consistent with the sibling casing/beam rows' bar. (NB: the SandboxMutator.cs:405-407 source comment still claims by-name fallback works 'regardless of which effects are registered' — that comment is now stale but harmless; the live path takes the registered-Effect branch.) audio: sound playback uses the right cue (object/impact_<mat>_N.wav, channel TriggerAuto, vol VOL_BASE*intensity, atten ATTEN_NORM) and is reachable; precache of the 5 variants is deferred to lazy Play (intentional).
- `mutator-sandbox.object.edit_get` — WIRED + LIVE: Trace seam assigned (SandboxTraceProvider — crosshair forward TraceService, resolves a hit object by ReferenceEquals(o.Edict, tr.Ent)) + reachable via CmdSandbox → HandleCommand, so edit-target selection works on the live path.
- `mutator-sandbox.object.attach` — WIRED + LIVE: object_attach get/set/remove reachable via CmdSandbox → HandleCommand. FIXED (re-audit 2026-06-27): the setattachment seam is NO LONGER a TODO — ObjectAttachSet calls Api.Models.SetAttachment(e.Edict, parent.Edict, tag) (SandboxMutator.cs:298-299) and ObjectAttachRemove clears it (SandboxMutator.cs:315-316). EngineServices.SetAttachment (EngineServices.cs:796-803) sets e.Aiment=parent + records the tag; the child is set MOVETYPE_FOLLOW=12, and FlyMove.PhysicsFollow (SV_Physics_Follow, FlyMove.cs:483) rigidly tracks the parent's origin each server step — so the child genuinely follows the parent on the live physics path (same FOLLOW seam vehicles/heads use). Residual nuance: bone-precise tag OFFSET application is a renderer detail (SetAttachment stores the tag name for the renderer; the server follow tracks the parent ORIGIN), not runtime-verified in-game — but the attach is no longer a no-op.
- `mutator-sandbox.object.remove` — WIRED + LIVE: object_remove reachable via CmdSandbox → HandleCommand; now deletes a real edict (Api.Entities.Remove).
- `mutator-sandbox.object.claim_info` — WIRED + LIVE: claim/info reachable via CmdSandbox → HandleCommand; CryptoIdfpProvider(=PersistentId)/NetNameProvider/PrintTo(=SendChatToPlayer) assigned in NetGame.WireSandbox, so claim/owner-name/info-print all work. FIXED (re-audit 2026-06-27): object_info mesh tag enumeration (FOR_EACH_TAG) is NO LONGER stubbed — NetGame.WireSandbox:958-961 wires sandbox.MeshTagNamesProvider from ModelService.TryGetModel(...).Tags.Keys, and HandleCommand(object_info mesh) iterates it (SandboxMutator.cs:890-893) to print the ^3tag^7, … list. The mesh subcommand now prints the real tag (bone) list, not just the header.
- `mutator-sandbox.storage.persist` — WIRED + LIVE: NetGame.WireSandbox:877 assigns IObjectStore = SandboxObjectStore (real file IO via UserPaths.Resolve("sandbox/storage_<name>_<map>.txt") under ~/XonData). DatabaseSave writes, DatabaseLoad reads — so the 5s autosave tick (OnStartFrame on the live SvStartFrame) + autoload at activation genuinely persist objects per map. Sandbox_SaveAllowed mutator-hook veto IS ported + fired: FireSandboxSaveAllowed() at DatabaseSave head (SandboxMutator.cs:543); chain MutatorHooks.cs:1035, no subscriber (Base-faithful empty chain).
- `mutator-sandbox.hooks.gating` — WIRED + LIVE: HandleCommand is reachable (CmdSandbox), so the g_sandbox_readonly gate (commands rejected with the read-only notice) fires on the live path; g_sandbox_info gates LOG verbosity faithfully. The three Sandbox_* veto hooks (DragAllowed/SaveAllowed/EditAllowed) are FULLY PORTED as MutatorHooks hook chains (MutatorHooks.cs:1121/1142/1165) and fired at all three Base-equivalent call sites: FireSandboxEditAllowed at the command gate (SandboxMutator.cs:620), FireSandboxSaveAllowed at DatabaseSave (:543), FireSandboxDragAllowed at the think grab branch (:346). No subscriber currently exists — identical to Base, whose MUTATOR_CALLHOOK returns 0 with no handler — so the gating is bit-faithful, not 'readonly-only'.
- `mutator-sandbox.menu.tools_dialog` — WIRED + LIVE: MenuCommand has a `sandbox` case (MenuCommand.cs:232) that expands the cfg alias (`sandbox … → g_sandbox …`) and routes through SendGame → the live-match channel. So the dialog's action buttons genuinely drive the SandboxMutator backend (CmdSandbox → HandleCommand) when g_sandbox is enabled. Object_duplicate COPY still inert (clipboard stuffcmd gap). No in-game entry point: the Shell `"sandbox"` factory key (Shell.cs:188) is reached ONLY via the dev/CI `--menu-screen sandbox` flag. No menu button or keybind opens it in a normal match (Base uses the `menu_showsandboxtools` keybind, no port equivalent).
- `mutator-spawn_near_teammate.registration.cvar_gate` — MINOR/cosmetic: ExprToken tests the comparison operators in the order >= <= == != === !== > < whereas Base BINOP source order is >= <= > < == != === !==. The '> / <' before '== / !=' difference only changes the split point for a pathological token that contains BOTH a '>'/'<' and an '=='/'!=' (e.g. 'a>b==c'); no real ruleset/cvar value has such a token, so the live result is identical. The code comment overclaims 'EXACTLY QC's BINOP source order'.
- `mutator-spawn_near_teammate.relocate.eligibility` — MINOR/subset: WeaponLocked (line 468) ports only the reachable freeze subset (Entity.FrozenStat != 0 || STATUSEFFECT_Frozen) — the same reachable piece as CampcheckMutator.WeaponLocked. QC's weaponLocked also covers game_starttime/sv_ready_restart_after_countdown, game_stopped, player_blocked, and the LockWeapon mutator hook, none of which have a port equivalent on the spawn path. No observable divergence in a normal live match (spawns only fire mid-match).
- `mutator-spawn_unique.state.su_last_point` — Recorded divergence — Base stores the last spawnpoint in a per-edict field (.entity su_last_point); the port holds it in a ConditionalWeakTable<Entity, StrongRef> keyed on the player entity instead of adding a per-mutator field to Entity/Player. GC-safe (entry drops when the player is collected) and behaviorally identical (a per-player Entity?). Same pattern stale_move_negation / vampirehook use.

- `smneg.damage_calculate_hook` — Recorded divergence — Per-attacker per-weapon weight stored in a ConditionalWeakTable keyed by the attacker entity instead of QC's per-CS() .float x_smneg_weight[] field. Behaviorally identical: server-only, non-networked, lives for the match; chosen to avoid adding an Entity field. Deathtype is a string (project-wide) vs QC int+DEATH_WEAPONMASK; WeaponNetNameOf empty is the faithful analogue of DEATH_WEAPONOF==WEP_Null. The Base FOREACH `it != WEP_Null` guard is automatically satisfied: this port registers NO Null weapon, so Weapons.All holds only real weapons and the loop's `other.RegistryId == wid` skip mirrors `it != w`.
- `status_effects.framework.registry` — StatusEffectDef now carries DisplayName/Icon/Color (m_name/m_icon/m_color data-parity), but the live HUD still reconstructs label/icon/color from a separate PowerupMeta table in PowerupsPanel rather than reading them off the def. Cosmetic; the def-carried values are unused by the HUD. The behavioral methods (RemovalSound/PersistentCheck/OnTick/OnRemove/OnApply) are all carried and live.
- `status_effects.framework.apply` — The per-effect m_apply override path is now ported (Apply runs def.OnApply on the !wasactive transition) and frozen carries OnApply=Hook.RemoveGrapplingHooks, so a freshly-frozen entity DOES now drop its grappling hook (live via FreezeTag/NadeIceBoom/ApplyFrozen). The only residual frozen.m_apply omission is Frozen_ice_create (the MDL_ICE block) — tracked under status_effects.frozen.
- `status_effects.framework.remove` — The per-effect OnRemove override is wired for spawnshield (clear EF_ADDITIVE|EF_FULLBRIGHT), stunned (clear EF_SHOCK), and burning (clear EF_FLAME). Only frozen's ice-block teardown (Frozen_ice_remove) is NOT ported (frozen has no ice block — covered under status_effects.frozen).
- `status_effects.framework.tick` — Tick scope is still live players only (skips observers/dead), not all damagedbycontents entities. Monsters tick burning/spawnshield through their own MonsterFramework path; a non-player non-monster ignite would not tick. (Acceptable: players + monsters cover the real victims.) Constant tick scope (all g_damagedbycontents ents with .statuseffects, not MOVETYPE_NOCLIP / live, non-observer, non-dead players only (per-player Tick in GameWorld:2393); monsters tick burning via MonsterFramework)
- `status_effects.framework.persistent` — The explicit SetPersistent(Entity,def,bool) setter still has no external caller — but it is redundant: the per-tick PersistentCheck recompute in Tick is the live m_persistent mechanism and it DOES set the networked PERSISTENT bit. The bespoke 999s SuperweaponTimeout/SuperweaponPass re-apply is retained but is not the persistence source.
- `status_effects.framework.removeall_lifecycle` — Base's SpectateCopy effect-aliasing (client.statuseffects = spectatee.statuseffects) still has no port analogue — intentional N/A: there is no separate statuseffects_store entity for spectators, and remote viewing of a spectatee's burning/frozen overlay is covered by the per-entity NetEntity blob.
- `status_effects.net.entity` — Recorded divergence — Port writes a full per-entity status-effect snapshot each changed tick (gated by a _dirty set mirroring SendFlags=0xFFFFFF) instead of delta-encoding against a separate statuseffects_store entity. The major/minor bit layout and per-effect (float time, byte flags) payload are identical; per-effect delta-encoding was deemed optional. Bandwidth cost only. Constant delta vs statuseffects_store entity (delta (only changed effects sent) / full per-entity snapshot each changed tick)
- `status_effects.hud.powerups_feed` — Infinity glyph is now driven by the networked PERSISTENT flag (PowerupsPanel:222), matching Base. The held-bit fallback rows (jetpack/fuelregen/unlimited-superweapons via items bits, PowerupsPanel:246-251) remain a port extension because those are .items bits, not status effects, in this port — but for an active superweapon status effect the flag-derived glyph now applies.
- `status_effects.burning` — Fire TRANSFER (FireApplyDamage boxesoverlap + FindInRadius re-ignite) uses a radius search on the entity center rather than QC's g_damagedbycontents iteration; behaviorally equivalent for overlapping players/monsters but could miss/over-include in edge cases. Minor.
- `status_effects.frozen` — Recorded divergence — Frozen is shown via an icy-blue per-model colormod on the frozen player's mesh (ClientWorld.cs:1104) so every frozen player reads as frozen map-wide, rather than Base's local-only full-screen blue CSQC drawfill self-flash. Deliberate presentation choice (visible to all observers, not just the frozen viewer). No MDL_ICE block entity spawned around a status-frozen entity (Frozen_ice_create/think/remove); no SVQC lava/STAT_FROZEN auto-extinguish for the frozen def itself (it has no OnTick — burning/stunned DO self-extinguish while frozen, but frozen itself doesn't auto-clear in lava). Base's local full-screen blue freeze flash (CSQC drawfill) for the frozen viewer is not implemented (only the model tint). Constant m_color ('0 0.62 1' / Color=(0,0.62,1) on def; ClientWorld uses FrozenColormod per-model tint) Constant ice model (MDL_ICE block at origin-'0 0 16', alpha 0.5, random frame 0..20, think follows owner / none) Constant SVQC extinguish in lava/STAT_FROZEN (yes (m_remove NORMAL) / no per-effect tick for the frozen def itself (frozen has no OnTick)) Constant CSQC self overlay (full-screen drawfill '0.25 0.90 1' * hud_colorflash_alpha*0.3 additive / per-model colormod on the frozen player's mesh)
- `status_effects.spawnshield_stunned_superweapon` — Player spawnshield's authoritative damage-block remains the bespoke Entity.SpawnShieldExpire timer (read by DamageSystem.HasSpawnShield); the SpawnShield status effect is mirrored from it (applied at spawn + kept in lockstep each tick) purely to drive the EF_ADDITIVE|EF_FULLBRIGHT shimmer + StatusEffects_active(SpawnShield) readers. Structural divergence (two state sources kept in sync) but behaviorally faithful. Minor.
- `superspec.state.flag_bitfields` — Recorded divergence — Option state is held in a static Dictionary<Player,Options> side-table rather than as .fields on Player, because this port file may not edit Player.cs (documented in the class summary; promoting to real Player fields for networking is a todo). Constants are bit-identical. The store is now WRITTEN on every live path: the superspec/autospec/itemfilter commands (HandleCommand), the ClientConnect options-file load (OnClientConnect), and read by ItemTouch/PlayerDies/follow* — and persisted across reconnect via OnClientDisconnect (see persistence row). Residual: state is server-side only (not networked to the client as real .fields).
- `superspec.spectate.transfer` — Recorded divergence — The port has no engine Spectate() target-switch primitive, so SpectateTransfer sets the follow-cam state directly (Player.Spectatee = target, Player.SpectateeStatus = target.PlayerId) — the faithful observable result of Spectate(this, targ). Now reached on EVERY live caller: the followkiller PlayerDies body, the ItemTouch auto-follow triggers (ASF_SSIM/powerup/mega/flag), and the followpowerup/followstrength/followshield commands. No remaining un-wired caller.
- `superspec.persistence.options_file` — Recorded divergence — IsLocalProvider is left unwired by the host, so the listen-host local-file fast path (IS_LOCAL -> superspec-local.options regardless of UID) is never taken; the port keys EVERYONE by crypto_idfp (dedicated-server behaviour). Net effect: an authenticated client persists; an unauthenticated remote client and a listen host's local fast-path do not. The bit-faithful load/save logic is in place behind the seam if IsLocalProvider is later wired. Load on connect (magic-checked, crypto_idfp-keyed) + save on disconnect are now LIVE via the host Store. Residual divergence: the IS_LOCAL listen-host fast path is not wired (see intended_divergence), so a listen host's local options aren't saved/restored under a fixed filename — they persist only if the host carries a PersistentId.
- `superspec.notify.missing_uid` — Recorded divergence — The port has no per-entity think scheduler reachable from a Common-layer mutator, so the new_pure(...).nextthink delayed-hello entity is stood in for by a (Player, When) pending-list drained off the per-frame MutatorHooks.SvStartFrame chain. Observable timing (fire ~5s after connect if still UID-less) is faithful. Fires on the live path: scheduled at time+5 in OnClientConnect, drained by OnStartFrame on the pumped MutatorHooks.SvStartFrame chain, sent NOTIF_ONE_ONLY to the UID-less client. Cleared on disconnect before firing if the client leaves early.
- `superspec.msg.helper` — Both output forms are now wired and reach the spectator: the console line via PrintTo (host SendChatToPlayer) and the centered overlay via SendCenterRaw -> MsgType.CenterRaw, consumed live at HudNotifications.cs:146 (the same raw-centerprint channel Chat.cs and map triggers use). The silent/verbose/spamlevel gating is bit-faithful.
- `superspec.itemfilter.match` — FilterItem is a faithful port (empty filter = match all; else tokenized classname allowlist against item.ClassName) AND now has a LIVE caller: OnItemTouch invokes it for each spectator with SSF_ITEMMSG set when an item is touched. The filter string is settable via the wired superspec_itemfilter command.
- `superspec.itemtouch.autospec_and_msg` — Fully ported and live: the new MutatorHooks.ItemTouch chain is fired from ItemPickupRules.ItemTouch (after the QC pickup gate, before the give, so the item still carries its raw powerup timers — faithful to items.qc:706). For each spectator/observer: the filtered SSF_ITEMMSG pickup message + the ASF_SSIM auto-follow, then the Strength/Shield/Mega-Armor/Mega-Health/flag-grab auto-follow with observer_only suppression. Minor faithful mapping: QC item.netname (m_name) -> item.Pickup.DisplayName (fallback NetName).
- `superspec.playerdies.followkiller` — Subscribed to the live PlayerDies chain (fired at DamageSystem.cs:589) and the body is reachable: ASF_FOLLOWKILLER is settable via the wired autospec command, so a spectator following the victim auto-switches to the killer. The show_what announcement now reaches the spectator on BOTH the console (PrintTo) and centered (SendCenterRaw) channels.
- `superspec.commands.parse` — All 6 verbs ported faithfully (bit accumulation, clear, on/off, help, OPTIONINFO state lines) and routed live via the server command bus (Commands.CmdSuperspec, gated on Added + spectator-only). Both message forms now reach the client (console via PrintTo, centered via SendCenterRaw). followfc [red|blue] lives in the CTF gametype (sv_ctf.qc:2658), out of scope.
- `superspec.ui.mutator_strings` — Both overrides exist with the exact Base tokens and BOTH are LIVE. MACHINE token ':SS' — MutatorActivation.BuildMutatorsString (gated on mut.Added) called from GameWorld.cs:1068 at gamelog init (sv_eventlog-gated, faithful to Base server/gamelog.qc:50 GameLogInit). PRETTY label ', Super Spectators' — MutatorActivation.BuildMutatorsPrettyString now has two live callers that write the result into the 'modifications' serverinfo: GameWorld.cs:1079 (match-start, faithful to Base PutClientInServer server/client.qc:1107) and ServerNet.cs:607 (BuildServerInfo, the DP server-browser infoResponse). Residual nuance: the port computes the pretty string at match-start / on info request and stuffs it into the 'modifications' cvar/infostring rather than recomputing it per client-join, but the content is identical (mutators don't change mid-match) and the leading ', ' is stripped exactly as Base substring(s,2,...).
- `touchexplode.scan.prethink` — IS_INDEPENDENT_PLAYER guard is a no-op: Entity.IsIndependentPlayer is hardcoded false; equivalent today since the port has no independent-players mode, but it won't gate if one is added.
- `touchexplode.list.activemutators` — Constant list_label ('Touch explode' (when g_touchexplode > 0) / none)
- `mutator-vampire.enable.gate` — Minor edge divergence: Base excludes vampire when the instagib MUTATOR is enabled, and instagib itself is gated on `g_instagib && !m_weaponarena`. The port's `g_instagib == 0` term keys on the cvar, not the resolved mutator state — so on a weaponarena gametype with g_instagib 1 (instagib mutator OFF) Base would permit vampire but the port suppresses it. The common `g_vampire 1; g_instagib 1` double-activation (the actual baseline defect) is correctly prevented.
- `mutator-vampire.report.mutator_strings` — Minor presentation form divergence (not a defect): Base's describe() renders a dedicated 2-paragraph mutator info page; the port carries the same text (both paragraphs, %s resolved to 'Vampire') on the g_vampire checkbox tooltip instead, following the established Cloaked / New Toys / Piñata / Hook tooltip pattern. Content is faithful.
- `mutator-vampirehook.latch.aiment_follow` — Minor structural omission vs SetMovetypeFollow (util.qc:2129-2147): the port does NOT set solid=SOLID_NOT, MOVETYPE_FOLLOW, v_angle, aiment_classname, or aiment_deadflag. Instead it keeps MoveType.None (set on latch) and manually re-positions the chain to follow each think (Hook.cs:330-331). These fields are unused by the vampirehook drain and the reel pull, so behavior is equivalent for this unit; flagged only for completeness.
- `mutator-vampirehook.drain.engine` — Damage attribution: Base passes `WEP_HOOK.m_id, DMG_NOWEP` (hook id flagged no-weapon) (sv_vampirehook.qc:30); port passes DeathTypes.FromWeapon("hook") only (VampireHookMutator.cs:112) — the DMG_NOWEP flag is not modeled (no NOWEP concept in the port's deathtype string; DeathTypes.cs:157 has only FromWeapon), so a drain kill is attributed as a normal hook-weapon kill rather than weaponless. Minor death-message/stats nuance.
- `mutator-vampirehook.hitsound.accumulator` — Recorded divergence — VampireHookMutator does not write the hit-confirm accumulator on a drain tick (VampireHookMutator.cs:24-25 documents the omission as cosmetic-for-now). The victim still takes/flinches from the normal Damage path; only the attacker's per-tick hit-confirm ding for the drain is absent. NOTE: the accumulator field itself now EXISTS in the port (DamageSystem.cs:1004 Entity.HitsoundDamageDealtTotal, networked via NetEntity Feedback flag, and written by InstagibMutator.cs:206-236) — so wiring it here is now a one-line add; it is simply not yet done for vampirehook. No hit-confirm ding for the owner on a vampire-hook drain tick (HitsoundDamageDealtTotal not accumulated). Cosmetic. Observable (drain body is live). The accumulator seam now exists (DamageSystem.cs:1004, used by InstagibMutator) but VampireHookMutator does not write to it.
- `mutator-walljump.predict.shared_impulse` — Residual (out-of-scope for this unit): BootClientFacade traces the predicted move against a flat test floor, not the replicated server BSP — so a remote-client wall jump predicts against approximate local geometry until client-side map collision lands. The mutator wiring itself is correct; this is a general client-collision-replication gap noted in NetGame.cs:988-994.
- `mutator-walljump.fx.sound` — Minor channel divergence: Base plays on CH_PLAYER; SoundSystem.PlayPlayerSound hardcodes SoundChannel.VoiceAuto (CH_VOICE, SoundSystem.cs:342). Audible and per-model-correct otherwise; channel only affects which slot the cue occupies/cuts. UNCHANGED. Constant channel (CH_PLAYER / SoundChannel.VoiceAuto (CH_VOICE/-2))
- `waypoints.registry.defs` — Constant superweapon Weapon / Item blink (spritelookupblinkvalue, wp_extra-dependent) (2 / item.m_waypointblink / Weapon def blink=1; not wp_extra-driven)
- `waypoints.gametype.collect` — RE-AUDIT 2026-06-27 (post-W16/17): TEN live CollectWaypoints producers now — CTF (flag base/dropped/carrier), Domination (control points), FreezeTag (Frozen/Reviving + thaw-progress bar), Keepaway (KaBall/KaBallCarrier), TeamKeepaway (team-colored carrier/ball), Key Hunt (KeyDropped + KeyCarrier<team>/Finish), LMS (LmsLeader show-window-gated), AND now Race (Start/Finish/StartFinish/Checkpoint fixed markers, Race.cs:607), Onslaught (generators WP_OnsGen/Shielded + control points WP_OnsCP/Defend/Attack via the teamplay triple, Onslaught.cs:1063), Nexball (WP_NbBall on carrier-or-loose + WP_NbGoal shown to the enemy team, Nexball.cs:1292). Each is an override on the active GameType, called via _world.GameType?.CollectWaypoints in ServerNet.SendWaypoints per tick — live whenever that mode runs. DRIFT FIX: Onslaught/Nexball/Race were listed as MISSING in the prior row but are now ported + live. Still NOT CollectWaypoints producers: turret + vehicle markers. (Weapon_whereis, MONSTER healthbars, BUFF markers (BuffsMutator.SpawnBuffWaypoint, NEW 2026-06-28), and the CTF flag-CARRIER health-bar marker (Ctf.EnsureCarrierWaypoint, NEW 2026-06-28) are ported as MANAGER producers — not via CollectWaypoints. Assault + itemstime + Seeker + powerup-drop also emit via the manager, merged in SendWaypoints alongside CollectWaypoints.)
- `waypoints.ctf.flag_markers` — FIX (2026-06-28): flag-carrier HEALTH BAR is now PORTED + LIVE. Ctf.EnsureCarrierWaypoint (Ctf.cs:579) attaches the own-team WP_FlagCarrier via WaypointSprites.AttachCarrier, sets max = 2*healtharmor_maxdamage (CarrierMaxHealth, effHP approximated as health+armor) + per-tick UpdateHealth (RefreshCarrierWaypoint:596), serialized at ServerNet.cs:1240 and drawn by WaypointSpriteLayer.DrawHealthBar (:206). The QC armor-block-percent is approximated as the unblocked HP+armor sum (documented headless proxy). FIX (2026-06-28): auto-helpme on carrier hit is now LIVE — OnDamageCalculate (Ctf.cs:1366-1384) flashes HelpMePing on the live carrier waypoint when effHP < g_ctf_flagcarrier_auto_helpme_damage and the antispam window (g_ctf_flagcarrier_auto_helpme_time) elapsed, matching QC Damage_Calculate. FIX (2026-06-28): stalemate + self-carry enemy-FC reveal is now PRODUCED — CollectWaypoints (Ctf.cs:499-535) emits WP_FlagCarrierEnemy<team> on the carrier during Stalemate or self-carry, gated to live enemy-team viewers and hidden while the carrier holds Invisibility (ctf_Stalemate_Customize), plus the WP_FlagReturn self-carry marker (carrier-only).
- `waypoints.deploy.personal_here_danger` — Origin nuance: the port routes ALL personal/here/danger variants (_here/_crosshair/_death) to the player's feet origin (p.Origin). Base distinguishes them — _crosshair uses the crosshair world-trace endpoint, _death uses the death spot. Minor partial: the marker lands at the player instead of the aimed point for the _crosshair/_death verbs. UNCHANGED. Personal waypoint visibility is owner-only via VisibleForPlayer predicate (matches QC showto=own).
- `waypoints.deploy.helpme` — The MUTATOR_CALLHOOK(HelpMePing) extension point (mutators overriding helpme, e.g. nades) is not modeled — the port attaches WP_Helpme unconditionally in teamplay. UNCHANGED.
- `waypoints.visibility.rules` — All three rules match Base WaypointSprite_visible_for_player: personal predicate (VisibleForPlayer), SPRITERULE_SPECTATOR sv_itemstime gating (hide when ==0, show in warmup or when ==2), SPRITERULE_DEFAULT team-restriction + live-player gate. Powerups CustomizeWaypoint hook now modeled: an enemy carrier holding Invisibility is hidden from enemy radar (ServerNet.cs:1090-1096). FIX vs baseline — this hook was not represented in the row before.
- `waypoints.visibility.three_image_rule` — Recorded divergence — Port resolves the per-audience image (QC model1=enemy / model2=own-team / model3=spectator) on the SERVER, per peer, in SendWaypoints via WaypointSprite.SpriteFor(viewerTeam, viewerIsSpectator), rather than networking the model1/2/3 triple to the client and selecting there. Because the port already serializes the waypoint set per peer, the right image is chosen for each viewer with no wire-format change; the visible result matches QC's Draw_WaypointSprite SPRITERULE_TEAMPLAY image swap. WaypointSprite_UpdateSprites(e,m1,m2,m3) maps to the SpriteName/SpriteNameOwn/SpriteNameSpec triple. RE-AUDIT 2026-06-27 (post-W16/17): DRIFT FIX — the three-image teamplay swap is now IMPLEMENTED + LIVE. WaypointSprite carries SpriteName (model1/enemy) + SpriteNameOwn (model2) + SpriteNameSpec (model3); WaypointSprite.SpriteFor(viewerTeam, viewerIsSpectator) (Waypoints.cs:203) picks the right one per viewer, with null falling back to SpriteName so non-teamplay producers are unaffected. SendWaypoints calls SpriteFor((int)peer.Team, peer.IsObserver) per peer (ServerNet.cs:1229), and WaypointVisible skips a viewer only when the chosen image is empty (QC `if(spriteimage=='') return`). Live producers setting the triple: Assault (Defend/Destroy/Destroy via UpdateSprites), KeyHunt (KeyCarrier<team> / KeyCarrierFriend|Finish / KeyCarrier<team>), TeamKeepaway (Tka<team> / KaBallCarrier / Tka<team>), Onslaught CP (OnsCPAttack / OnsCPDefend / OnsCP). Residual: the QC client-side per-def blink that rides each of the three images (e.g. OnsCPDefend 0.5 / OnsCPAttack 2) IS carried because the chosen sprite NAME resolves the def + its blink client-side; only the wp_extra-driven per-instance blink override (waypoints.registry.defs) remains unmodeled. The mechanism (server-side per-peer resolution vs networking the triple) is a deliberate divergence, not a gap.
- `waypoints.net.serialize` — Recorded divergence — Port re-serializes the full peer-visible waypoint set each tick as a flat record list (id/origin/team/sprite/radaricon+pingbit/color/health/fade/helpme/maxdist/hideable) instead of QC's delta-bit SendEntity edicts. Equivalent payload; simpler wire format suited to the port's snapshot net model. Capped at 255/peer. The radar-icon byte packs the ping pulse in bit 7 (QC cnt|BIT(7)), so the ping ring IS carried. No SendFlags delta encoding (always sends all fields); QC sends only changed sub-fields. Bandwidth-only difference. UNCHANGED. wp_extra (item/weapon/buff id used for client text/color/blink lookup) not networked — fine while wp_extra-dependent producers (superweapon pickups) are absent. UNCHANGED.
- `waypoints.client.draw` — Constant SPRITE_ARROW_SCALE (1 / approx (custom triangle arrow))
- `waypoints.client.cvars_inert` — All g_waypointsprite_* cvars (including the distance/edge/crosshair fade cvars) + cl_hidewaypoints are READ LIVE by WaypointSpriteLayer._Draw via Api.Cvars (gated on HasCvars). Residual: g_waypointsprite_iconcolor not read (default white only). Minor.
- `waypoints.radar.icons` — Radar PING ring pulse is now IMPLEMENTED: server stamps bit 7 of the radar byte while IsPinging (ServerNet.cs:1234), client stamps a per-id ping time, RadarPanel.DrawPingPulse draws the expanding additive gfx/teamradar_ping ring. Live producers: itemstime respawn countdown (ItemPickupRules.cs:843) + buff respawn (BuffsMutator.cs:559). FIX vs baseline (was no-op).
- `waypoints.keyhunt.key_markers` — Dropped key → WP_KeyDropped on the key entity (KH_KEY_WP_ZSHIFT offset, SPRITERULE_DEFAULT + DroppedKeyWaypointVisibleFor tracking gate); carried key → WP_KeyCarrier<team> riding the carrier (head offset, SPRITERULE_TEAMPLAY + CarrierWaypointVisibleFor), flipped to WP_KeyCarrierFinish when one team owns all keys. Per-viewer predicates mirror kh_*_waypointsprite_visible_for_player (tracking-device + warmup/spec/same-team/invisibility).
- `waypoints.misc.lms_seeker_powerup` — LMS: WP_LmsLeader attached to each in-game leader (FFA, no team gate), shown only in the periodic leader show-window and never to a spectator of that leader (lms_waypointsprite_visible_for_player). Live via CollectWaypoints (LastManStanding.cs:836). Seeker: type-1 tag spawns a WP_Seeker following the tagged target for tag_tracker_lifetime (RADARICON_TAGGED), refreshed/killed with the tracker (Seeker.cs:494-540). Live via the tag Touch handler. Powerup drop: a WP_Item countdown follows the dropped powerup, the bar fills toward its remaining time, killed at 0 / expiry (PowerupsMutator.cs:541-578). The generic WP_Item def is used (no wp_extra per-powerup icon — minor cosmetic gap).
- `waypoints.weapon.whereis` — Constant marker lifetime / radar (-2 (instant, no fade) / RADARICON_NONE (in-world only, not on radar) / WhereisLifetime 2.5 (re-spawned each press) / radarIcon 0 (RADARICON_NONE, in-world only))
- `waypoints.monster.healthbar` — Constant wp_extra = monsterid (wp.wp_extra = this.monsterdef.monsterid / WpExtra NOT set on monster sprite)
- `mutator-weaponarena_random.registration` — Recorded divergence — Port gates IsEnabled on the two arena cvars instead of QC's always-added + per-hook !g_weaponarena_random early-return; behaviorally equivalent (QC hooks no-op when random off), avoids subscribing inert handlers. Registered via [Mutator] source-gen into Mutators.All; Apply() live at GameWorld.cs:535.
- `mutator-weaponarena_random.w_randomweapons` — Recorded divergence — RNG is the deterministic Prandom (ADR-0010 project-wide determinism rule) instead of engine random(); distribution is uniform either way, so the only observable difference is reproducibility, not balance. Constant rng_source (engine random() (nondeterministic) / Prandom.RangeInt (deterministic, ADR-0010))
- `mutator-weaponarena_random.arena_weaponset_dep` — Recorded divergence — The '*_available' arena keywords intersect the arena set with the weapons actually placed on the map (weaponsInMapAll). The port has no map-weapon inventory, so it resolves all_available/most_available/devall_available to the full all/most/devall set — which is exactly QC's fallback when the map carries no weapons (world.qc:1953/1966/1979 else-branch). Only diverges on maps that DO carry a weapon subset; plain numeric/all/most/list arenas (the common case, and all weaponarena_random configs) are bit-faithful. '*_available' arena keywords can't intersect with map-present weapons (no weaponsInMapAll port model); they fall back to the full set. Intended divergence — only affects maps with a partial weapon set under an *_available arena. Constant *_available map intersection (weaponsInMapAll & weapons_{all,most,devall} / no map-weapon set -> full set fallback (QC's own no-map-weapons fallback))
- `net-entity-state.csqcmodel.change_mask` — Recorded divergence — Unified NetEntityState delta-codec replaces QC's per-entity SendEntity/ReadEntity funcs (csqcmodel + ent_cs + wepent + CSQCProjectile + generic stream) with one change-masked table; the port has no QCVM SendEntity machinery, and DP's own entity delta already works this way. Wire bit-layout differs (32-bit mask, NetPrecision.Low coords) but the only-changed-fields contract and precision class match. Constant mask_width_bits (24 (WriteInt24_t) / 32 (uint)) Constant origin_quantization (WriteVector 13.3 coord / NetPrecision.Low) Constant angle_quantization (WriteAngle 8-bit / NetPrecision.Low)
- `net-entity-state.snapshot.delta_baseline` — Recorded divergence — Explicit C# per-client ack/baseline ring replacing DP's engine-side EntityFrame; required because the port has a real client/server split with no shared QCVM. Behavioral contract (deltas vs last acked baseline; lost packet never corrupts; full snapshot when baseline lost) matches DP.
- `net-entity-state.csqcmodel.teleport_bit` — Recorded divergence — Base sets the bit explicitly at the teleport/respawn site (EF_TELEPORT_BIT); the port re-derives it from a one-tick origin-jump past a distance threshold in the snapshot builder. Same observable effect (client snaps instead of lerping) and it also clears the antilag ring (parity with PutPlayerInServer antilag_clear). Constant tick_jump_threshold (EF_TELEPORT_BIT (explicit on teleport/respawn) / TeleportTickDistance (origin-jump detect))
- `net-entity-state.csqcmodel.force_updates` — Recorded divergence — The port broadcasts a full owner block + entity deltas every server broadcast tick, which is denser than 4 origin sends/sec, so the symptom the keepalive prevents (stale replay buffer / vanishing tag) does not arise on the live path. No explicit keepalive constant is ported. No out-of-PVS teammate GPS: Base keeps sending a teammate's coarse ent_cs origin out of PVS (radar blip persists); the port's relevance cull drops the entity entirely, plus there is no CSQCPLAYER_FORCE_UPDATES keepalive — relies on the dense per-tick broadcast instead. Constant force_updates_per_sec (4 / none (full owner block + deltas every broadcast tick)) Constant CL_MAX_USERCMDS (128 / n/a)
- `net-entity-state.entcs.radar_shownames_slice` — Recorded divergence — ent_cs is folded into the unified NetEntityState (Origin/Health/Armor/Colormap/Model) rather than a separate GPS sender; the radar + shownames read it via ClientNet.TryGetRemoteState/SampleRemote. Coding differences (full short vs /10 byte) are cosmetic precision, not a behavioral gap. Constant health_coding (RES_HEALTH /10 byte, bound 0..255 / full short) Constant armor_coding (RES_ARMOR /10 byte / full short) Constant angles_y_coding (/(360/64) byte / NetPrecision.Low full angles) Constant entcs_think_hz (64 (0.015625s) / per broadcast tick)
- `net-entity-state.entcs.privacy_mask` — Recorded divergence — FIXED (Wave-6c ba3ae47): ServerNet.RelevantEntitiesFor now applies the ent_cs enemy-privacy gate per-recipient, mirroring _entcs_send's break-condition (radar_showenemies || SAME_TEAM || recipient-not-active-player => send private; else strip). It zeroes the HEALTH/ARMOR resource slice for an enemy recipient of a live remote Player entity. Intended divergence on ORIGIN/ANGLES: Base marks those private too, but in QC the visible player MODEL is a SEPARATE csqcmodel channel sent (PVS-gated) regardless of the entcs mask — the mask only hides the out-of-PVS radar GPS. The port folds model+entcs into one state whose origin IS the render position and already PVS-culls the whole entity when out of view, so an enemy's origin is governed by PVS exactly as QC's csqcmodel origin is; freezing it here would wrongly stall a visible enemy's model. So only HEALTH/ARMOR (no csqcmodel counterpart, otherwise leaked even for visible enemies) are stripped. The residual out-of-PVS coarse-GPS degrade remains its own tracked gap (csqcmodel.force_updates). Origin/angles privacy not separately enforced: Base's ENTCS_PRIVATEMASK also covers origin/angles_y (the out-of-PVS radar GPS), which the port governs by whole-entity PVS culling instead of a coarse stripped-GPS send. Anti-cheat critical HEALTH/ARMOR leak is now closed; the GPS-degrade nuance is folded into the csqcmodel.force_updates gap.
- `net-entity-state.wepent.active_weapon` — Recorded divergence — Local viewmodel weapon rides the full-precision owner block (ActiveWeaponId) rather than a per-slot wepent sender; remote players' held weapon rides NetEntityState.Weapon and is attached third-person by ViewEntityRenderer. Same observable result (correct viewmodel locally, correct held weapon on remotes).
- `net-entity-state.wepent.charges_clip_heat` — Recorded divergence — FIXED (uncommitted wave, working tree 2026-06-28): the wepent charge/clip/heat fields are now NETWORKED and LIVE across all clients, closing the prior host-only gap. ONE authoritative resolver (WepentResolver.Resolve, engine-free in .Common — mirrors QC client/hud/crosshair.qc 471-557 + Arc_GetHeat_Percent) feeds THREE sinks that previously drifted: (1) the per-player WepentView entity-state block (EntityField.WepentView BIT20, ×255 unit byte / signed-short clip, decoded onto Entity.WepentView at ClientEntityView.cs:157) so a SPECTATOR following another player draws that player's rings; (2) the owner-block OwnerWeaponRings (9 floats appended to WriteOwnerState, read by ClientNet→LocalWeaponRings) so a PURE remote / dedicated-server client draws its OWN rings; (3) the host slot-scratch branch (LocalServerPlayer) unchanged. NetGame.UpdateCrosshairWeaponRings now picks the right source per role. Intended divergence (consistent with the unit's other coding divergences): the port codes every [0,1] charge/pool/heat at ×255 uniformly (Base codes some at ×16); HagarLoadMax/MineLimit caps stay owner-only (per-player client clamps against the same CrosshairPanel defaults 4/3); ViewmodelFrame/BeamState fields are RESERVED on the per-player wire for a Phase-2 viewmodel/beam consumer (currently default 0 — Arc beam bit0 is the only BeamState bit set). ViewmodelFrame and BeamState (electro beam bit2) are reserved on the per-player WepentView wire but have no live consumer yet (Phase-2): only the Arc-beam BeamState bit0 is produced, and ViewmodelFrame stays 0 — the networked viewmodel-frame / electro-beam-active overlay is not yet rendered. The charge/clip/load/heat/arc rings themselves are fully live across host/remote/spectator. Constant oknex_charge_coding (x16 byte (networked) / WepentViewState ×255 byte (port uses ×255 uniformly, intended))
- `net-entity-state.wepent.switch_alpha_misc` — Recorded divergence — FIXED (Wave-14a): the wepent switch + exterior-weapon-alpha block is now NETWORKED (EntityField.Wepent BIT19) and LIVE. ServerNet (1970-1983) produces m_switchweapon/m_switchingweapon from the live slot-0 weapon state machine (Switch*WeaponId), derives a render-relevant WS_RAISE/WS_DROP phase from WeaponFireState, and sends the exterior-weapon alpha (QuantizeAlpha(DefaultWeaponAlpha)); ClientEntityView (148-153) decodes them and ViewEntityRenderer (92-143/194-218) renders the remote weapon raise/lower dip + per-instance gun transparency on the live Player snapshot path (ClientEntityView.cs:213). Intended divergence: the port carries a SINGLE compressed WepPhase byte rather than reproducing Base's exact viewmodel_draw raise/drop timing off m_switchingweapon, and the alpha wire is the port's x255+sentinel coding (vs Base x254). Residual MISSING (no server producer / not in struct): m_gunalign (GunAlign field defaults 0), viewmodel skin (ViewmodelSkin defaults 0), porto_v_angle_held, tuba_instrument. wepent m_gunalign not produced: the GunAlign field is networked in NetEntityState but no server producer sets it (cl_gunalign is a client-local concept the port doesn't model server-side), so it defaults 0 — the remote gun-align side is not reproduced. viewmodel skin not produced: ViewmodelSkin field exists but no server producer; the exterior weapon's .skin variant is not networked. wepent porto_v_angle_held (Porto held-angle viewmodel) and tuba_instrument are not in NetEntityState at all — Porto held-angle and @!#%'n Tuba instrument are not networked (low-impact, weapon-specific). Weapon-switch raise/lower timing is a port approximation: a single WS_RAISE/WS_DROP phase byte + a fixed-rate dip tween (ViewEntityRenderer SwitchRaiseTime/SwitchDropTime), not Base's m_switchingweapon-driven viewmodel_draw timing. Constant m_alpha (byte rint(bound(-1,254*a,254)+1)/254 (x254) / WepAlpha byte: 0=opaque(not sent)/255=hidden(-1)/else a*255 (x255 + sentinels)) Constant m_gunalign (byte (this.m_gunalign) / GunAlign byte field exists but NO producer -> default 0) Constant porto_v_angle_held (byte + WriteAngleVector2D / none (not in NetEntityState)) Constant tuba_instrument (byte / none (not in NetEntityState)) Constant skin (viewmodel) (WriteShort(this.skin) / ViewmodelSkin byte field exists but NO producer -> default 0)
- `net-entity-state.csqcmodel.appearance_hook` — Friend-color collision is checked against the LOCAL team only, not the full team list (port doesn't network the team list here) — a friend force equal to ANOTHER team's color isn't suppressed (self-documented in CsqcModelAppearance.cs:110). STILL OPEN.
- `net-entity-state.replicate.client_cvars` — Constant server_honored_set (all of the above consumed server-side / cl_autoswitch, cl_autoswitch_cts, cl_handicap(+given/taken), cl_weaponpriority(+N), cl_noantilag, cl_physics, cl_clippedspectating, g_xonoticversion, notification choices now have live consumers; only cl_autoscreenshot (driven client-locally off the networked intermission flag, not the replicated value) is allowlisted+stored but not consumed off the replicated value server-side)
- `net-entity-state.csqcmodel.anim_state` — Recorded divergence — FIXED (Wave-14b): the UPPER-body anim ACTION channel (animdecide getupperanim: SHOOT/MELEE/PAIN1/PAIN2/DRAW/TAUNT/DIE1/DIE2 + start time) is now NETWORKED + LIVE. The action is latched server-side at the real gameplay sites (WeaponFireGate fire commit, DamageSystem pain/death, WeaponFireDriver draw, SoundSystem taunt), ServerNet networks the expiry-RESOLVED id (AnimDecideUnit.GetUpperAnim with the action's numframes/framerate window), and PlayerModel plays it as a torso overlay over the velocity-derived legs (SelectTorsoAction), driven by the live ServerTimeProvider. Intended divergence remains on TWO sub-channels: (1) the discrete anim_state byte (BIT7) and (2) the per-player anim_lower_action split (BIT8) are NOT separately networked — the LEGS locomotion (run/walk/idle/jump/duck) is still reconstructed CLIENT-side from the networked velocity/onground/ducked (LocomotionBlend.SelectLegsDirectional), the port having no server-side animdecide_setstate machinery. AnimActionTime is a raw server-clock float (the death_time-class client-observed-time divergence) rather than QC's WriteApproxPastTime byte compression. So the torso ACTION overlay is now faithful+live; the legs split + anim_state byte stay client-reconstructed (values partial). anim_state byte (BIT7) and the lower-body anim_lower_action split (BIT8) are not separately networked: the LEGS locomotion (run/walk/idle/jump/duck) is reconstructed client-side from velocity/onground/ducked (LocomotionBlend.SelectLegsDirectional), not the QC animdecide_setstate networked state — so a remote player's lower-body locomotion is inferred, not authoritative. The UPPER-body action overlay IS now networked + live. AnimActionTime is a raw float (server clock) rather than QC's WriteApproxPastTime byte-quantized past-time — a deliberate death_time-class divergence (values: partial). Constant anim_state_coding (byte (per-player, BIT7) / none (not networked; locomotion reconstructed client-side)) Constant anim_lower_action_coding (char + WriteApproxPastTime (BIT8, non-local only) / none (lower-body action not separately networked; legs reconstructed from velocity))
- `net-entity-state.csqcmodel.alpha` — Recorded divergence — The csqcmodel per-entity render alpha is now networked in the unified NetEntityState.Alpha channel (not present at the last audit). Wire coding diverges deliberately: Base uses CSQCMODEL_PROPERTY_SCALED x254 over the -1..1 range and folds the default (1.0) into a normal value, whereas the port quantizes to a byte with two reserved sentinels (0 = fully opaque, NOT networked so an opaque entity costs nothing; 255 = the QC -1 'do not render' marker) and 1..254 = alpha/255. Observable result matches Base: transparent entities (Cloaked default_player_alpha 0.25, Running Guns invisible-player/visible-gun, the Invisibility powerup, death/spawn fades) render at the right alpha, and the -1 'hide model' sentinel is preserved as a distinct hidden state. Producers feed it live from p.Alpha (players) and e.Alpha (entities); the client maps it back to Entity.Alpha each decode. Constant alpha_quantization (WriteByte rint(bound(-1,254*alpha,254)+1) — -1..1 -> 0..255 (x254 + offset) / QuantizeAlpha: >=1 -> 0 (opaque, not networked); <0 -> 255 (hidden); else clamp(round(alpha*255),1,254)) Constant opaque_sentinel (alpha 1 (default, still in mask) / 0 = opaque, mask bit stays clear (costs nothing)) Constant hidden_sentinel (-1 -> wire 0 (rint(bound(-1,-254,254)+1)=0)... mapped (ReadByte-1)/254 / 255 = hidden(-1), DISTINCT from opaque)
- `net-entity-state.projectile.wire_channel` — Recorded divergence — The dedicated CSQCProjectile SendEntity channel is folded into the unified NetEntityState delta snapshot (same rationale as net-entity-state.csqcmodel.change_mask): a projectile is just an entity whose model field carries a catalog key instead of a model path, and the per-field delta mask replaces the hand-rolled sf byte. Origin/velocity/type/owner-team reach the client with the same observable result; the sf sub-flags that exist only to compress the QC wire (0x01 origin, 0x02 type, 0x40 onground) are subsumed by the delta codec. silent flag (sf bit 4, !sound_allowed) not networked: the client attaches the catalog fly-sound loop unconditionally, so a projectile the server intends silent still hums (low impact — sound_allowed is rarely false for projectiles). onground bit (0x40) not networked (Base uses it for the client mover's ground state; the port predictor doesn't model ground rest, a resting grenade just receives its unchanged origin). Constant type_coding (byte csqcprojectile_type (sf & 2) / catalog key string in the (otherwise unused) model field, classified by ProjectileCatalog.Classify) Constant silent_flag (sf bit 4 from !sound_allowed -> client suppresses fly loop / none — LoopSounds attaches the catalog fly loop unconditionally (ProjectileRenderer.cs:176-178)) Constant onground_flag (sf 0x40 (IS_ONGROUND) / none (not networked))
- `net-entity-state.projectile.clientanimate_prediction` — Recorded divergence — The port promotes EVERY projectile to the client-animated contract (snap to each authoritative origin, extrapolate locally by the held velocity, world-trace stop/bounce) instead of Base's two-class split — the unified snapshot networks velocity for all entities anyway, and the CSQC behaviour this reproduces (instant full-speed muzzle exit, no interp delay) is the reason the predictor was built (ProjectilePredictor header documents the QC path it ports). Gravity is deliberately NOT networked: the per-snapshot velocity refresh captures the trajectory's first order, bounding the between-snapshot sag to ~1u at normal rates. The projectile predictor has its own collision policy by projectile type. The local debris support for MatchTicrate in physics-movetypes.matchticrate.interp is a separate path. Ballistic arc between snapshots is straight-line (gravity not networked/applied client-side): a grenade/mortar shell renders as a chord of its true parabola between snapshots (~1u error at normal snapshot rates, larger under packet loss). No interpolated (non-clientanimate) projectile class: Base renders a server-moved projectile via iorigin1/iorigin2 two-snapshot interp; the port predicts everything, so a projectile whose server motion is NOT ballistic/linear (scripted movers) can wobble between snap corrections. Constant gravity_networked (WriteCoord gravity when sf 0x10 / none — straight-line extrapolation; per-snapshot velocity refresh bounds the error to the second-order gravity term (ProjectilePredictor doc)) Constant clientanimate_split (per-projectile flag: 0x80 = client-moved (Movetype_Physics_MatchServer), else two-snapshot interpolated (iorigin1/2) / ALL projectiles predicted (snap+extrapolate); no interpolated projectile class) Constant cl_projectiles_sloppy (1 in shipped xonotic-client.cfg, unchecked residual for MatchServer; 0 enables trace / none — predictor advances by raw render dt with a world-only trace for collide/bounce catalog types)
- `net-entity-state.projectile.fade` — Recorded divergence — Native snapshots carry current opacity in the existing Alpha field. Upstream sends a relative start offset and duration to drive the ramp client-side. The initial delay and duration must fit the original tick fields. The native shared snapshot uses spawn time, so a late observer or a later upstream resend can have a different start offset. Snapshot-driven alpha updates step at the snapshot cadence. The modern GPU trail also fades already-emitted particles as a group.
- `net-entity-state.projectile.team_color` — Constant ffa_coding (realowner.clientcolors byte when !teamplay / e.Team only (0 in FFA -> no tint))
- `net-entity-state.projectile.teleport_resend` — Constant no_trail_suppression (0x08 suppresses the trail segment across the jump / none — ProjectileRenderer.cs:341-350 emits the faithful trail segment from the pre-jump TrailPos to the post-jump position with no discontinuity gate)
- `net-entity-state.wire.message_registry` — Recorded divergence — The QC message registries exist to multiplex many gameplay messages through the engine's single svc_tempentity/SendEntity pipe. The port owns the transport, so the LIVE equivalent is the NetControl frame-kind byte + hand-written typed channels (snapshot/event/sound/matchstate/waypoints/...) dispatched in ServerNet.HandlePacket / ClientNet's switch — one enum instead of three renumbered registries. NetMessageId/NetChannel/NetDispatcher preserve the three-registry partition for a future source-generated message set (ADR-0003) but are NOT on the live path (sample-grade, self-documented). NetDispatcher + NetMessageId are DEAD scaffolding: grep shows no caller outside NetDispatcher.cs (NetProtocol.cs references them in docs only, and BuildParity folds NetMessageId.Temp_Explosion as a hash constant). The live dispatch is the NetControl switch — fine behaviorally, but the 'registry' half of the port exists without a live consumer. Constant temp_id_base (80 (TempEntities renumber) / 160 (NetMessageRanges.TempFirst)) Constant linked_id_base (1 / 32 (LinkedFirst)) Constant parse_guard (0xDEADBEEF net_guard_marker (NDEBUG-gated debug framing check) / none (BitReader bounds-checks; no per-message sentinel))
- `net-entity-state.wire.c2s_yenc` — Recorded divergence — Base must smuggle binary C2S messages through the engine's TEXT console-command pipe ('cmd c2s ...'), which is the sole reason yEnc exists (lib/yenc.qh's only consumer is net.qh's WriteByte/ReadByte). The port owns a binary transport (ENet channels), so C2S messages are plain BitWriter frames (InputFrame, ClientCommand = the DP clc_stringcmd analog) and the entire yEnc layer is intentionally without counterpart — nothing to encode around. Constant byte_encoding (yEnc (+42, '=' escape) so binary rides a console string / raw binary (BitWriter/ENet) — no text tunnel exists to escape for)
- `net-entity-state.wire.registry_checksum` — Recorded divergence — Same contract (a client and server whose ordered gameplay registries disagree must not play together), different mechanics: QC sends each registry's id+hash after connect and the client fatals on mismatch; the port folds the order-sensitive registry hashes + the wire protocol version into ONE uint carried in the very first handshake packet and rejects before admission — strictly earlier and cheaper, and NetProtocol.cs:128 documents itself as the registry_net.qh analog. Residual honesty: registries not yet folded (weapons, items) rely on the manual ProtocolVersion bump convention rather than a content hash. Hash coverage is narrower than Base: only Effects + Notifications are content-hashed; a client/server pair differing ONLY in another ordered registry (e.g. weapon registry order) passes the gate unless someone remembered the ProtocolVersion bump.
- `net-entity-state.stats.stat_registry` — Recorded divergence — The engine 256-slot stat array is a DP transport detail; the port replaces the MECHANISM with typed channels while keeping the CONTRACT per stat: MOVEVARS_* -> MoveVarsBlock (hash-gated, prediction-critical, with the absent-cvar default symmetry fix), TIMELIMIT/GAMESTARTTIME/WARMUP/OVERTIMES -> MatchState, per-player stats -> the owner block, per-entity stats -> NetEntityState fields. Individual gameplay STATs are audited in their owning units; this row owns the registry-level contract. STAT(FRAGLIMIT) (slot 235) has no located networked counterpart: the client cannot display the server's frag/point limit on the play path (Base HUD score panel shows it from the stat). TIMELIMIT made it into MatchState; FRAGLIMIT did not. No generic late-registration guard: QC's Stats_renumber + REGISTRY_CHECK make a stat-set mismatch a connect-time error; the port's typed channels are only guarded by ProtocolVersion bumps (see wire.registry_checksum gap). Constant slot_model (one 256-slot engine stat array, magic ids hard-coded, rest renumbered around holes / no stat array — each stat rides a typed channel (MoveVarsBlock positional vector / MatchState frame / owner block / NetEntityState field)) Constant FRAGLIMIT_235 (STAT(FRAGLIMIT) / none located — no networked fraglimit consumer in game/ (grep hits only menu/Shell cvar writes))
- `nexball.init.initialize` — PresentTeams unconditionally includes Red+Blue and only ADDS Yellow/Pink from goal entities; QC nb_spawnteams derives every present team purely from the nexball_goal ents (teamplay_bitmask). Behaviorally equivalent on the standard two-team map and on 3/4-team maps that include red+blue goals.
- `nexball.ball.spawn` — Constant ball model (models/nexball/ball.md3 scale 1.3 / models/nexball/ball.md3 set (scale 1.3 not applied))
- `nexball.ball.lifecycle` — InitBall does not WaypointSprite_Ping (the ball waypoint sprite itself IS now spawned via CollectWaypoints; only the QC ping/flash request on release is unported).
- `nexball.ball.football_kick` — FootballPhysics is a compile-time const 2 (the default), not read from g_nexball_football_physics at runtime; the -1/0/1 branches exist but are unreachable unless the const changes.
- `nexball.carry.perframe` — Constant g_nexball_viewmodel_scale (0.25 / none (client viewmodel scale; QC ball_customize is CSQC))
- `nexball.goal.sentinel_encoding` — Recorded divergence — Port shifts fault/out sentinels to -2/-3 (QC -1/-2) so they don't collide with the port's team-color/None numbering; mapping is consistent end-to-end and observable scoring is unchanged. The ball_redgoal->blue / ball_bluegoal->red compat swap is preserved exactly. Constant GOAL_FAULT (-1 / -2) Constant GOAL_OUT (-2 / -3)
- `nexball.ball.bounce_jumppad` — BounceFactor (0.6) / BounceStop (0.075) / Pushable (true) are hard-coded defaults in BallConfig.WithKindDefaults rather than read from the per-mode g_nexball_{basketball,football}_{bouncefactor,bouncestop,jumppad} cvars at spawn (QC reads them off the spawnfunc). Values equal Base at the gametypes-server.cfg defaults, so a non-default cvar override would not take effect.
- `nexball.waypoint.sprites` — InitBall/GoalTouch WaypointSprite_Ping (the ball-release/goal flash request) is not emitted (the sprite itself is drawn each tick via the pull model; only the one-shot ping/flash is absent). The SendWaypoint hook radar-bit mask (M_ARGV(2,int) &= ~0x80) is not reproduced (minor radar-icon detail).
- `onslaught.graph.power_propagation` — None on logic: the 3-phase algorithm (reset → flow power outward through same-team captured/linked neighbors → unshield nodes adjacent to a powered ENEMY node) matches sv_onslaught.qc:onslaught_updatelinks. Live: the graph HAS edges because onslaught.links.resolution is wired (ResolveLinks runs post-spawn).
- `onslaught.round.handler` — Per-player ROUNDLOST/player_blocked latch + nades_RemovePlayer not driven by the port's handler (round-win/tie center+info + win jingle ARE sent in BankRoundWin). NOTE: GameWorld case Onslaught still calls a bare EnableRounds() which spins up a SECOND generic RoundHandler, but Onslaught runs its OWN Handler via ons.Tick() (where the 5→7 logic lives) — harmless/redundant, not a regression.
- `onslaught.overtime.decay` — d = max_health/window scaled by 1 + (#enemy-linked CPs) per second, routed via the self-damage (DEATH_HURTTRIGGER) generator path. Audio emitted: first overtime tick fires OVERTIME_CONTROLPOINT center + ONS_GENERATOR_DECAY (latched once via OvertimeAnnounced). NOTE: the enemy-linked-CP acceleration is meaningful (links resolve); the base self-decay still bypasses the shield (selfDamage branch).
- `onslaught.scoring.rules` — Recorded divergence — QC awards ONS_TAKES on destroying an enemy icon and ONS_CAPS on a completed build (two separate events). The port credits ONS_TAKES+SCORE+10 on icon destroy (IconDamage) and ONS_CAPS+SCORE+10 on build completion (CaptureControlPoint) — same columns, but the port deliberately does NOT add ONS_TAKES on a build-capture (documented to avoid double-credit). Net player columns match Base for each event. The scoreboard COLUMN rendering (HUD scoreboard layout for generators/caps/takes) is a presentation/HUD concern not audited here.
- `onslaught.spawn.teleport_choose` — Dedicated coverage of the teleport-antispam centerprint remains open.
- `overkill-weapons.okmachinegun.primary_auto` — FIXED (Wave-8b, this pass): W_MuzzleFlash (AttackAuto:153 EffectEmitter.Emit MACHINEGUN_MUZZLEFLASH — matches okmachinegun.qh m_muzzleeffect) and wr_impacteffect (AttackAuto:144-148 traces the surface, EFFECT_MACHINEGUN_IMPACT puff + throttled SND_RIC_RANDOM via BulletImpactFx) are now BOTH wired LIVE — closing the two STILL-OPEN items the baseline recorded.
- `overkill-weapons.okhmg.primary_auto` — FIXED (Wave-8b, this pass — the baseline's central regression is now closed): OkHmg.AttackAuto now does recoil PRNG (:154), EFFECT_RIFLE tracer (:162-163), W_MuzzleFlash MACHINEGUN_MUZZLEFLASH (:175), casing type3 g_casings>=2 (:177 EjectCasing(Bullet)), and wr_impacteffect MACHINEGUN_IMPACT + SND_RIC_RANDOM (:166-170). OkHmg.cs is NO LONGER byte-identical to main — migrated in commit 31aa799.
- `overkill-weapons.oknex.primary_rail` — FIXED (Wave-16, commit b3a9c06): CSQC rail beam now wired — OkNex.Attack:214-221 emits the actual muzzle->impact beam (EFFECT_VORTEX_BEAM, or VORTEX_BEAM_OLD under cl_particles_oldvortexbeam) as a count-0 trail, the SAME path Vortex.EmitBeam uses; the beam networks by registered id through the live effect sink (ServerNet EffectNetSink + ClientWorld RenderSink). Closes the baseline 'no SendCSQCVortexBeamParticle' gap. FIXED (Wave-16, commit b3a9c06): Yoda + Impressive announcements now fire — OkNex.Attack:199 -> Announce() derives the impressive/yoda condition from the rail's first pierced victim (cross-team, damageable, the every-other-hit toggle via a per-actor weak side-table) and the shooter's pre-trace airborne status, calling NotificationSystem.Announce(ACHIEVEMENT_IMPRESSIVE/_YODA). Mirrors vortex.qc:141-162. Closes the baseline 'yoda/impressive never fire' gap. FIXED earlier (Wave-8b): wr_impacteffect (Attack:204-212 EFFECT_VORTEX_IMPACT puff + SND_OK_NEX_IMPACT neximpact, silent on sky/miss) + VORTEX_MUZZLEFLASH. wr_glow charge glow not present (covered by oknex.charge, default-off) — inert at stock balance.
- `overkill-weapons.oknex.charge` — Recorded divergence — g_balance_oknex_charge defaults 0, so the shipped weapon is Vortex-without-charge and the entire charge subsystem is inert. The velocity-charge GetPressedKeys hook, chargepool, wr_glow charge glow, overcharge sound, and the frametime/W_TICSPERFRAME regen cadence are deliberately deferred until the cvar is set (documented in OkNex.cs).
 charge_velocity_rate (move-to-charge) GetPressedKeys hook not ported. chargepool (secondary) regen/depletion not ported. wr_glow charge glow and overcharge SND_OK_NEX_CHARGE sound not ported. Charge regen uses frametime, not QC frametime/W_TICSPERFRAME (only matters if charge enabled). Constant charge_velocity_rate (0 / none) Constant chargepool_regen (0.15 / none)
- `overkill-weapons.okshotgun.primary_pellets` — spread_pattern>0 deterministic pattern path not handled (default 0 = random, matches QC). FIXED (Wave-8b): muzzle flash (Attack:126 EFFECT_SHOTGUN_MUZZLEFLASH), shotgun-shell casing (Attack:128 EjectCasing(Shell), g_casings>=1), and wr_impacteffect (per-pellet EFFECT_SHOTGUN_IMPACT puff + the 5%/0.25s-throttled SND_RIC_RANDOM, the seam owns the throttle so a 10-pellet blast yields at most one ric) are now all wired LIVE. Closes the baseline 'no muzzle/casing/impact/ric' gap.
- `overkill-weapons.shared.secondary_blaster_jump` — FIXED (this wave, OkWeapons.cs:89-93): the animdecide_setaction(actor, ANIMACTION_SHOOT, true) on the refire_type==1 jump path is now driven — FireSecondaryBlasterJump latches AnimDecide.AnimUpperAction.Shoot (restart:true == QC restartanim) on actor.AnimUpperAction/AnimActionStart, the SAME overlay+call PrepareAttack uses for primary fire (WeaponFireGate.cs:166). Networked via ServerNet.cs:2052/2121 and rendered by PlayerModel.cs:486 (Shoot->shoot/attack/fire frame group), so third-person observers now see a fire pose on the blaster-jump. Closes the baseline 'no third-person fire-2 pose' gap. refire_type==0 path latches the same overlay via PrepareAttack. refire_type==0 secondary path: only the non-default secondary-button GATE nuance differs (QC oknex also requires WEP_CVAR secondary==2 on that branch). refire_type default is 1 for all five OK weapons, so no observable gap at stock balance.
- `overkill-weapons.shared.checkammo` — OK weapons grant IT_UNLIMITED_AMMO via the mutator, so the ammo gate short-circuits true in normal play; the per-weapon checks matter only for clip/reload accounting.
- `overkill-weapons.identity.attributes` — Deprecated netnames 'hmg'/'rpc' (kill-message routing) noted but registry uses canonical 'okhmg'/'okrpc'.
- `overkill-weapons.presentation.fx` — FIXED (Wave-8b, commit 31aa799): ALL FIVE OK weapons now emit W_MuzzleFlash (registered EFFECT names), wr_impacteffect impact puff + impact/ric sound, and the three bullet weapons (okmg/okhmg/okshotgun) eject casings (per-type g_casings gate) + recoil PRNG + EFFECT_RIFLE/RIFLE_WEAK tracer. This CLOSES the baseline's central regression (it recorded okhmg/okshotgun/oknex/okrpc + okmachinegun's muzzle/impact as all missing). Residual presentation gaps live in their per-weapon rows, NOT here. UPDATED (Wave-16, b3a9c06): the oknex networked CSQC rail beam (SendCSQCVortexBeamParticle) is now WIRED (oknex.primary_rail closed it) — the only per-weapon networked-visual gap left is okrpc's missing CSQC flying-chainsaw projectile body. Both bullet weapons + FIRE2 still lack the third-person fire-2 weapon-frame anim. The muzzle/casing/impact/ric/tracer/beam seam itself is fully wired.
- `overkill-weapons.shared.bot_ai` — okrpc bot lead: QC bot_aim also passes the projectile LIFETIME (30) as the lead-prediction-window arg; the port override only sets the lead SPEED (2500). The brain caps the lead by speed (the dominant term), so the practical aim matches, but the lifetime widening of the prediction window is not modeled. Minor. oknex has no bot override (relies on the generic always-primary fallback). This MATCHES QC at stock balance: oknex.qc:113-122 always presses ATCK1 and only presses ATCK2 when charge is enabled (default off). Faithful, not a gap, at stock.
- `physics-movetypes.flymove.slide` — FL_WATERJUMP easierwaterjump primal-velocity restore (movetypes.qc:316) and the WALLCLIP pm_time primal-velocity restore (movetypes.qc:319) at the tail of _Movetype_FlyMove are absent (port has no pm_time field). Player-centric (water-exit / wallclip) so low impact for the non-client entities this loop drives. Port does not model sv_gameplayfix_nogravityonground as a cvar — it hardcodes the nogravityonground=1 behavior (gravity only when !OnGround) in BOTH half-steps. Matches Xonotic's default 1, so faithful in practice; would diverge only if the cvar were 0.
- `physics-movetypes.walk.stepmove` — WALLCLIP pm_time primal-velocity restore at walk.qc:20 (and the FlyMove tail) is absent — the port has no pm_time field. Player-centric, low impact for the bodies this loop drives.
- `physics-movetypes.push.pushmove` — Minor broadphase-style difference (not behavioral): Base gathers riders via findradius over the whole live entity list (FOREACH_ENTITY_RADIUS, push.qc:75); the port iterates Entities() with a swept-box + final-box overlap prefilter (FlyMove.cs:350,378-382) — a flat scan rather than the area-grid broadphase, but the resulting rider set is equivalent.
- `physics-movetypes.matchticrate.interp` — Recorded divergence — Native turret gibs are server-side bounce entities rendered through snapshot interpolation. Native projectile rendering normally uses ProjectilePredictor to snap to authoritative samples and extrapolate between them. Local debris keeps dedicated committed state in the client pools. The local debris tracer covers static BSP. Model gibs use the explicit ±8 hull. Casings and Raptor fragments use point sweeps; the stock CSQC setmodel builtin supplies alias-model bounds. Moving brushes, other entity contacts and solid recovery for gibs and fragments are outside this port. Native bounce uses the shipped split-gravity, slope-stop and remaining-tic slide behavior. Alternate server gameplay-fix flags are outside the debris implementation. The projectile predictor has no cl_projectiles_sloppy control or gravity continuation. See net-entity-state.projectile.clientanimate_prediction. Base ENT_CLIENT_TRAIN registers train_draw for MatchServer. Native inline brush models are excluded from the generic entity snapshot feed. Train presentation is tracked in mapobject-func.train and was not exercised here.
- `physics-movetypes.pushentity.impact` — Recorded divergence — Port traces return null trace.Ent for world-brush hits; PushEntity substitutes a shared inert WorldSentinel entity as 'other' on a blocked sweep so a projectile's .touch fires against the world (explode-on-wall). DP's worldspawn (edict 0) serves the same role; a faithful stand-in for QC's lack of a null-world distinction, not a behavior change. Also: DP passes checkstuck=false for MOVETYPE_FLY so PushEntity skips the nudge for FLY — reproduced (FlyMove.cs:138).
- `physics-player.air.strafe_blends` — Air branch does NOT apply maxspeed_mod (physics.qc:169-174 scales com_phys_acc_rate_air/vel_max by min(mod,1) and airstop/strafe by full mod). Inert for live players: only the !IS_PLAYER spectator sets mod != 1, and a spectator ALWAYS takes the fly branch (MOVETYPE_NOCLIP/FLY), never the air branch — so the air maxspeed_mod fold is genuinely unreachable on the live path.
- `physics-player.air.warsowbunny` — FromValues decodes sv_aircontrol via the magnitude-fallback helper, so the warsow preset's genuine `aircontrol 0` (no CPM redirect) is wrongly read back as the 100 default on the per-peer wire vector — a trailing Aircontrol pass runs where Base's warsow set has none. Separate air-strafe-blends concern, noted here because it perturbs the warsow path; the warsowbunny accel leaf itself is byte-exact.
- `physics-player.jump.playerjump` — RESIDUAL (anim-action only): Base PlayerJump (SVQC, player.qc:491-495) calls animdecide_setaction(ANIMACTION_JUMP, true) alongside the grunt; the port has no anim-action seam yet (same as the walljump port), so the jump anim-action is not driven. The AUDIO portion is now FAITHFUL — the port gates on g_jump_grunt and plays the per-model jump voice (see notes).
- `physics-player.integrator.walkmove_flymove` — Recorded divergence — (1) sv_step_upspeed_scale (1) / sv_step_upspeed_max (-1) are a PORT EXTENSION that can scale/cap the upward velocity carried through a step-up; at the stock defaults they are a strict no-op (only act on positive velocity.z > the cap), so stair traversal and the stair_step_up golden trace are byte-identical to Base. (2) NudgeOutOfSolid (DP SV_NudgeOutOfSolid) is added as a desync-prevention recovery gated on sv_gameplayfix_nudgeoutofsolid (DP default ON). SV_WallFriction (the explicit up/forward step's wall-parallel velocity scale) is a deliberate no-op in BOTH Base (stock QC _Movetype_WallFriction body commented out) and the port — so it matches, but a modded server that un-comments _Movetype_WallFriction would diverge.
- `physics-player.movement_sounds` — Recorded divergence — The cosmetic random()*0.1 footstep-cadence jitter is drawn from a dedicated server-local System.Random (FootstepJitterRng), NOT the shared deterministic Prandom stream. Base's jitter is under #ifdef SVQC (server-only), so reproducing it from the shared Prandom would advance a stream the predicting client also consumes (spread/effects) and desync the seed. The value distribution (uniform 0..0.1 added to 0.3) is identical to Base; only the RNG SOURCE differs. Minor: Base's speed gate is vdist(this.velocity, <=, ...) over the FULL 3D velocity; the port uses Vec2LenSq (XY only) and a strict `<` (vs Base `<=`). On ground velocity.z is ~0 (NOGRAVITYONGROUND), so the difference is negligible, but it is not byte-identical at the threshold. No golden-trace/test coverage for footsteps (code-verified only).
- `physics-player.spectator_control` — Recorded divergence — sys_phys_fixspeed's stuffcmd of cl_forwardspeed/back/side/up is NOT ported; the net input layer instead scales the wishmove against sv_maxspeed so the wishspeed magnitude is reached the same way. The spectator maxspeed_mod factor IS applied (mp.ApplyHighSpeed + Accelerate *= mod + move *= mod) so SPECTATORSPEED still scales fly speed. sys_phys_fixspeed cl_*speed stuffcmd absent (replaced by client-side wishmove scaling — intended).
- `physics-player.specialcommand` — Minor intended divergence: Base returns true from sys_phys_override on sequence completion to SKIP that tick's physics; the port instead just fires give-all and lets physics run normally (avoids a 1-tick server/client prediction skew for a cheat-only side effect). Cosmetic, single-tick, cheat-only.
- `physics-player.pregame_hold` — Recorded divergence — Base sys_phys_pregame_hold (sv_physics.qc:48-64) freezes a player INSIDE the physics system by zeroing velocity + set_movetype(MOVETYPE_NONE) + disableclientprediction=2 while !(time >= game_starttime && !game_stopped). The port enforces the SAME freeze a layer up, in GameWorld.OnClientMove: `preMatchFreeze = Time < GameStartTime && !Warmup.WarmupStage` folds into `canMove`, and PM_Main (PlayerPhysics.Move) is simply not run when !canMove — so the held player neither accelerates nor falls. Equivalent observable behavior (no movement before the match clock starts, warmup freely playable, match-over/intermission held) via the host's per-tick movement gate rather than an in-physics MOVETYPE_NONE swap + client-prediction disable. The disableclientprediction=2 latch (sv_physics.qc:55/59) — which tells the CLIENT to stop predicting during the hold and re-enable (MOVETYPE_NONE->MOVETYPE_WALK) on release — has no direct port analogue; the port instead just doesn't apply the move server-side. Cosmetic: a predicting client could briefly predict motion during the freeze before the next authoritative reconcile pins it back, where Base suppresses prediction outright. No effect on the authoritative position.
- `physics-player.landing_event` — Recorded divergence — Base fires sys_phys_land via the phys_land event (emit(phys_land, this) at physics.qc:152, subscribed in STATIC_INIT(sys_phys)) on the ground-branch landing edge (!WAS_ONGROUND). It does three things: (1) reset jumppadcount to 0, (2) LOG_TRACEF the landing velocity when autocvar_speedmeter is set, (3) LOG_TRACEF an N-x jumppad combo when jumppadcount > 1. The port has no phys_land event bus; it resets JumpPadCount inline where Base's landing logic runs — in PlayerJump (PlayerPhysics.cs:1123, the !WasOnGround landing block) and the swim branch (PlayerPhysics.cs:385, com_phys_water sets jumppadcount=0) — so the MOVEMENT-BEARING effect (the jumppad-combo counter reset) is faithful and live. The two LOG_TRACEF calls are debug diagnostics (speedmeter / combo log), not gameplay, and are unported. The speedmeter landing-velocity LOG_TRACEF (sv_physics.qc:121-122, gated on autocvar_speedmeter) is not ported — a developer-diagnostic log only, no gameplay effect. The jumppad-combo LOG_TRACEF (sv_physics.qc:124-125, jumppadcount > 1) is not ported — diagnostic log only.
